- Loop 46 study (28 designs, running total 1,164): the tagline as a second voice. Verdict: keep the two lines, keep the break at the full stop, keep Montserrat Regular 13 pt with 150 tracking; change the alignment only — move the block from right-aligned to left-aligned, starting at the cube's left edge. Top-5: (1) one line vs two lines — one line is the norm for a tagline (SHADOW LINE, TYSHA, Nosferatu, RED TRACE), but BLACK BOX's copy is two opposed sentences, and collapsing them to one line would collapse the antithesis SYSTEMS / FAILURES into a single breath and read as a credit line; two lines are justified here — the one-line norm does not override this copy; (2) where the break falls — the break should fall where the meaning turns, which for BLACK BOX is the full stop between the sentences, so the current break is right; mid-sentence breaks in the sample are for display type, not for a 13 pt second voice (Swiss posters, KAGE, Darkness and Light); breaking inside 'THE SYSTEMS YOU DEPEND ON.' at 13 pt would force a re-parse and gain nothing; (3) how the break relates to the hero's edge — in the strong examples the second voice's flush edge IS a hero edge and the other edge ties to something too (SHADOW LINE centred on the title's width, KAGE left-aligned to a grid column with a hairline grid, Benzin microcopy in a top-right column under a hairline rule, DARK LIGHT's THIS HURTS DEEP left-aligned inside a right-hand column with a vertical spine); BLACK BOX's block currently ties only on the right, to the cube's right edge (X1020), and that tie ends on two full stops — the least informative characters; its left edge, where both sentences start, ties to nothing; the block needs its tie on the left, where the reader enters; (4) what weight and size the second voice takes — tiny, Regular/light, about a tenth of the wordmark (13 pt Regular against 128 pt ExtraBold, ~10:1) is the pattern (Benzin, KAGE, SHADOW LINE, RED TRACE); do not go to Medium/SemiBold or 16–18 pt — it would compete with the wordmark and the measurement label and widen the lines so the X568 tie is lost; emphasis in the sample comes from colour or position, never from weight (RED TRACE marks TRACE in red, KAGE marks FUNCTION in red, Swiss posters alternate a word in colour — a later loop, not this one); all-caps with 150 tracking is the credit/microcopy voice, a weakness, but changing case now would confound the alignment test — note it for Loop 47; (5) how it is aligned — left alignment dominates by a large margin for a dark poster with a right-weighted hero (KAGE, RED TRACE's banner variant, Darkness and Light, all 50 Swiss posters, Benzin, DARK LIGHT's THIS HURTS DEEP, Issue No. 1); right-aligned only for DARK LIGHT's four-line intro paragraph, a statement not a tagline above a hero; centred only for film posters with a centred title; a right-aligned block makes the entry point move from line to line (the reader must find THE each time) while a left-aligned block fixes it; BLACK BOX's hero is on the right, its spine and wedge enter from the left, and its tagline is the only right-aligned element — right alignment makes the composition right-heavy and leaves the left black above the cube empty. CHANGE TO TEST (alignment only): move the two-line block, unchanged in copy, case, size, weight, tracking and vertical position, to left alignment on the cube's left edge — line 1 'THE SYSTEMS YOU DEPEND ON.' left edge X274, top Y537; line 2 'THE FAILURES YOU NEVER SEE.' left edge X274, top Y557; glyphs still end ~Y572, still 8 px above the cube's top edge Y580. PREDICTION TO VERIFY: line 2's right edge lands within 15 px of X568 (the wedge point's X) — on the width estimate line 1 ends ~X560, line 2 ~X571. CORRECTION (main Muse, verified against the card's geometry): the X274 measurement hairline DOES run through the tagline band (Y560–990 vs band Y537–577, overlapping Y560–577), so the block must shift to X282 so no glyph touches the line — the alignment is to the hairline, not to the card edge. WHY: the left edge X274 is the cube's left edge and the left hairline (a hard grid line the card already has); the reading entry THE is now fixed, not floating; the block fills the empty black above the cube's left half and balances the right-heavy composition, giving a wordmark -> tagline -> cube -> wedge-point reading path; the cube's right edge does not need the tagline to reinforce it — it is already the strongest vertical on the card. Rejected alternatives: one line right-aligned (reads as a credit, loses the 40 px vertical mass, left edge ties to nothing); centred on cube centre X647 (no centred element to centre on; centred taglines pair with a centred title; would cross the wedge diagonal); a different mid-sentence break in three or four lines (forces a re-parse at 13 pt, fills the wordmark-to-cube gap and touches both); heavier weight or larger size (breaks the 10:1 ratio, competes with the wordmark and the measurement label, loses the X568 tie); keep right alignment but mark FAILURES in red (next-best alternative, rejected for this loop — does not fix the floating left edge or the right-heavy composition; test alignment first, red in Loop 47); move the tagline below the cube into the footer (reads as a footer credit, loses the 8 px relationship Loop 45 settled); no tagline at all (the cube is abstract; the tagline is the only place the subject is stated). Falsification: the change is wrong if line 1 or line 2's left is not X274 ±2 (or X282 if the hairline shift applies), glyphs do not end Y572 ±2, the gap to cube top Y580 is not 8 px ±2, any wedge pixel appears in the tagline band Y537–577, or any glyph touches the left hairline; if line 2's measured right edge is NOT within 15 px of X568, the wedge-point tie is not real and the change rests on alignment alone; if a fresh viewer reads the left-aligned version more slowly, or the two sentences blur into one paragraph, or the viewer reads tagline -> wedge/spine instead of tagline -> cube, revert; if at thumbnail size the left block looks marooned with a large empty black gap between its right edge (~X565) and the cube's right edge (X1020), and the right-aligned version looks more settled, revert. If the left version passes geometry, the X568 tie holds within 15 px, and the reading test is equal or better, keep left alignment and make Loop 47 the next-best question: does the second sentence need a red punch word (FAILURES in #C32D30), or does red belong only to the wedge?

Do not shift the tagline right to clear the label — that abandons the X282 alignment, which is the point of the loop. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the loop 46 study): Loop 47 — does the second sentence need a red punch word (FAILURES in #C32D30), or does red belong only to the wedge?
- Loop 47 study (24+ designs, running total ~1,188): does the second sentence need a red punch word? VERDICT: No word goes red. The tagline stays all white. Top-5: (1) a red word earns its place only when it IS the headline, at display size — never as a speck inside 13 pt prose; in every successful case the red word is a whole word, a whole line, or the whole title, set large (IDENTITY. in Effort Is an Identity, CONCLAVE the entire title in red, ATTENTION, HIRING, Precision, GOOD DESIGN's DESIGN); at 13 pt Montserrat Regular, FAILURES would be a ~13 px-high red fragment, ~95 px wide, inside a two-line block — no example colours a single word inside small running text and makes it work; (2) when a word does get the red, it is the antithesis word AND the terminal word — the last word, closing the thought (IDENTITY. closes 'EFFORT IS AN IDENTITY.'; DESIGN closes 'GOOD DESIGN'; INFORMATION. closes 'DESIGN IS INFORMATION.' with only its full stop in red; the red 'to' in face to face sits between two huge black words as the pivot); FAILURES fails both halves — it is the antithesis noun, yes, but it is word 2 of 5 in 'THE FAILURES YOU NEVER SEE.', mid-sentence, not terminal; red there would start a sentence it does not finish (the eye jumps to the middle of line 2, then must return to white YOU NEVER SEE.); line 2 has no verb at all (a noun phrase), so there is no action to colour; (3) how much red is too much: one red idea per poster, and a second red only if it is the same role, same scale, or touches the first (Effort Is an Identity's three reds are one connected system on the same left edge at headline scale; DESIGN IS INFORMATION. keeps its reds in one column system); BLACK BOX already has red in three roles (the large wedge ending in a point at (568,758); the tiny red vertical spine microcopy; the measurement system) — a red FAILURES at ~X315–410, Y555–572 would be a fourth red and a second red text, in a different scale, a different role, and a different place (~180 px above and well left of the wedge point, sharing no edge, no alignment and no touch with the wedge) — exactly the disconnected-second-red pattern that splits a poster into two competing red ideas; the wedge must remain the only large red gesture; the spine is already the small red text voice; (4) small prose stays one colour even when its punch words are darker in meaning — the decisive, repeated observation (WORDS 03: red is only the header label on a red block; the prose underneath is entirely black, including SHAME, DESTRUCTION, BLACKNESS, BURN and DEATH-CLIMAX; Effort Is an Identity's small white subline reads 'SIX SLIDES ON SHOWING UP, FAILING, RISING.' — FAILING, the direct analogue of FAILURES, stays white even though the headline word above it is red; Conclave's small line stays white; ECHOES' caption stays white while red is only the disc behind the statue); designers do not colour the emotionally loaded word inside a quiet line — they colour the container, the label, or the headline, and let the sentence do its work in one colour; (5) red at small size on black is dimmer, not louder — and it reads as an error, a link, or a label, not as emphasis (white on black ~21:1 contrast; #C32D30 on black ~3.8:1, below the 4.5:1 expected for normal-size text); at 13 pt Regular with Montserrat's open letterforms and 150 tracking, FAILURES in red would be measurably harder to read than the white words around it — the punch line would become the least legible line; a single red word in otherwise white small text is the visual language of a hyperlink, a correction, or a proofing mark; the one small-scale red-in-text form that works is punctuation at headline scale (the red full stop in DESIGN IS INFORMATION.), and at 13 pt it would be a ~5 px speck. CHANGE TO TEST: a FREEZE, not a colouring — no word goes red; FAILURES stays white, Montserrat Regular 13 pt, letter-spacing 150, line 2 unchanged at X282, Y537–577, exactly as Loop 46 left it. Record explicitly: 'Tagline: all white. Red is wedge + spine only; no red word, no red full stop, no red in line 1 or line 2.' Keeping it white preserves the two-voice structure — giant white wordmark, quiet white tagline, one red wedge that points at the cube — instead of inventing a third voice for one word. Rejected alternatives: FAILURES in red (mid-sentence, 13 pt, disconnected, duplicates the red spine, dimmer than white, reads as a link/error); NEVER in red (colours the adverb, makes the line a shouted negation, still fails scale/disconnection); SEE in red (terminal, so closest to Finding 2 — but SEE is the least informative word, a verb of perception, not the failure; red on the last word turns the tagline into a punchline/command, still fails scale/contrast/disconnection); the whole of line 2 in red (semantically neat — line 1 white systems, line 2 red failures — and the only whole-line form the survey supports in principle, but a whole 13 pt line in #C32D30 on black would be the dimmest text on the card, inverting the hierarchy, and creates a second red text block with no connection to the wedge); red full stop after SEE. (works only at headline scale; at 13 pt a ~5 px speck, reads as dust or a print error, no alignment with the wedge point); FAILURES in red ExtraBold/larger (breaks the Regular voice, adds a third weight, still fails disconnection); SYSTEMS or DEPEND in red (colours the positive, inverts the antithesis, competes with the wordmark). Falsification (A/B test, five fresh viewers, 3-second glance at 100% and 25%): the no-red decision is proved wrong only if the red version passes ALL of — viewers quote line 2 unprompted and in full without hesitation; no viewer describes the red word as a mistake, link, label, or second logo; reading order stays wordmark -> tagline -> cube with the wedge still the single red idea; FAILURES is as legible as the white words at 100% and still legible at 25%; at 200% zoom the red letterforms show no loss of edge or weight. A single 'I like the red' does not overturn it — only reading behaviour does. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 48 — if the antithesis still feels flat, does the second sentence need a weight change, not a colour change — line 2 in Montserrat Medium, still all white at 13 pt / 150 spacing, versus Regular — and does that weight shift survive at thumbnail size without breaking the quiet tagline voice?

Running total: 1,164 + 24 = ~1,188 designs studied through loop 47.
- Loop 48 study (35 designs, running total ~1,223): does line 2 need a weight change, not a colour change? VERDICT: No. Both tagline lines stay Montserrat Regular, 13 pt, 150 tracking, all white. Top-5: (1) a 100-step is below the threshold at this size — at 13 pt on a 1080 px card the cap height is ~9 px; at a 240–270 px thumbnail it is ~2 px, and the Regular-to-Medium stem difference is well under a pixel, further washed out by 150 tracking and by white-on-black bloom; 13 pt-equivalent microcopy reads as a uniform grey block at thumbnail in every grid; a Medium line 2 would not read as emphasis — at full size it would read as uneven inking, at thumbnail as nothing at all; (2) when weight does mark a sentence, the step is at least 200 — Regular to SemiBold/Bold — and usually paired with size, and then it is no longer a quiet tagline (Incite keeps both headline lines in the same Bold with the body below in Regular — the step is between headline and body, not within the headline; Dream Bigger's two-sentence microcopy stays uniformly tiny Regular while DREAM BIGGER shouts in huge Bold; Do Something First is all the same Bold, orange/white colour does the alternation, not weight); Regular to SemiBold would create a third hierarchy level (ExtraBold wordmark, SemiBold line 2, Regular line 1/footer/spine) and turn line 2 into a sub-headline; (3) the direction is wrong — where a step exists, the heavier element is normally first, or a single key word, not the whole second sentence (YOU DIDN'T COME's first two lines share one weight and the third is smaller and Regular; Lost in Thought's title is thin/light and the body stays uniformly Regular; Blues' payoff word is lighter and more open, not heavier; Future Nostalgia's title lines share one Bold and Dua Lipa below is lighter script; the one genuine heavier-second case, MY NAME IS NOBODY, works only because NOBODY is the title itself with a very large size jump as well as weight); line 2 is a full 26-character sentence, not a title or a punch word, and making it heavier makes the failure shout while the systems line whispers — the opposite of the documentary voice, and bottom-heavy in a block that sits only ~8 px above the cube; (4) antithesis is already carried by meaning, line break and position — identical dress lets the words oppose each other; adding a second cue explains the joke (LOVE/PAIN weights only the nouns, and the second sentence actually fades; LESS IS MORE weights LESS solid vs MORE outline with the supporting sentence in uniform small italic Regular; YOU DIDN'T COME / YOU CAME OUT uses identical weight, opposition in the words alone; Motivational Typography Posters weight one word per cell, never a whole sentence); a whole-line Medium is, in effect, colour by another means, and Loop 47 closed colour for the same reason — it would re-open the red question in a quieter form; (5) at thumbnail size, what survives is (a) the giant wordmark, (b) a uniform tagline block, (c) image/wedge geometry — never a one-step weight difference inside the block (DREAM BIGGER, KAMAU&CO, Blues are all recognisable from their microcopy at grid size, but in no case could Regular be told from Medium within the small text, including in enlarged crops). CHANGE TO TEST: a FREEZE — change nothing in weight; line 1 and line 2 both stay Montserrat Regular 13 pt, 150 tracking, all white, block X282, Y537–577, unchanged. Lock both lines at Regular. Rejected alternatives: line 2 Medium (invisible at thumbnail, muddy/uneven at full size, looks like a rendering or inking fault); line 2 SemiBold (visible at full size but loud, third level, competes with the wordmark and wedge, turns the failure into a slogan, crowds the ~8 px gap to the cube); line 1 Medium/SemiBold, line 2 Regular (directionally more editorial but still invents a step inside a 40 px-high block, still fails at thumbnail, makes the systems line shout while the failure whispers); both lines Medium (uniform but heavier overall, denser block, loses distance from the wordmark, fills in at thumbnail); both lines SemiBold/Bold (tagline becomes a second headline, quiet documentary register gone); line 2 red, italic, underlined, or re-tracked (red closed in Loop 47; the others are outside this loop's weight question and would change the X282 alignment, line lengths or block geometry). Falsification (A = both Regular, B = line 2 Medium, C = line 2 SemiBold as calibration; viewed at 1080 px, 270 px and 180 px, shuffled, to at least three viewers): the Medium change is WRONG and stays rejected if at thumbnail viewers cannot name line 2 as heavier at better than chance or cannot tell A from B at all; at full size viewers describe B as 'uneven', 'bolder at the bottom', 'smudged' or 'a printing error' rather than 'emphasised'; B's line 2 looks wider, extends past line 1, or makes the block feel bottom-heavy; or viewers rank B as louder/more slogan-like than A. It could only pass if a majority name line 2 as heavier at thumbnail without prompting AND still rate B as quiet as A AND nobody calls it a rendering fault. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 49 — if the antithesis still feels flat, is it the pause between the sentences, not their weight — with both lines locked at Regular 13 pt / 150, does widening the leading between line 1 and line 2 (lifting the whole block if needed to keep the ~8 px clearance above the cube) let line 2 land as a second, darker thought? The only weight variant left open after that is a single word — 'NEVER' in Medium, the rest Regular — to be tested only after the pause test.

Running total: ~1,188 + 35 = ~1,223 designs studied through loop 48.
- Loop 49 study: the pause between the sentences — leading, not weight or colour. Verdict: do NOT widen to 1.4, 1.5 or 1.6 — that range is a dead zone (too small to read as a pause, too large to read as a tight block). If anything is tested, test ONE decisive jump — leading 2.0, block lifted 10 px — and expect it to be hard to pass; the study's prior is that the current 1.2 is likely to survive, because the full stop after 'ON.' plus the line break already IS the pause, and the successful pauses studied are not made by uniform leading at all. Top-5: (1) a pause has a threshold; 1.4–1.6 does not cross it — two-sentence taglines that read as one statement sit at ~1.0–1.2; the ones that read as two thoughts have a gap of about one extra full line (baseline-to-baseline ~1.9–2.0x font size, white space ~1.5–2x cap height between the lines); at 13 pt that is +4 px (1.5, barely visible) vs +10 px (2.0, unmistakable); (2) the pause, where it works, is a single asymmetric break before the punchline — not uniform loosening (THE ONE YOU ARE LOOKING FOR IS -> YOU keeps its first four lines at ~1.3 then leaves a clearly larger break before the final 'IS -> YOU', reinforced by the arrow; Yes, of course it hurts breaks after 'course' and indents 'it / hurts' to the right); with only two lines, 'leading' and 'space before line 2' are the same number, so any widening is automatically a single break, but it must be big enough to be read as a break, not as loose setting; (3) long lines need tight leading; short lines can afford air — the successful wide examples all have very short lines (one to three words); this card's lines are long full sentences, 27–28 characters each, tracked-out (150), and depend on proximity to stay grouped — widening them separates them into two captions faster than it separates two short lines (the Swiss work stays at ~1.0–1.1 and lets the word break itself do the work); (4) widening thins the block; it does not darken line 2 — the same ink spread over a taller block lowers optical density (at 1.5 the block goes from 40 px to ~44 px tall with no extra ink; at 2.0 to ~50 px); line 2 looks lighter, more remote and more detached, not darker — 'darker' here is semantic (systems, then failures) and no amount of air makes the second sentence more ominous, only more alone (Muren's loose ~1.4 spec blocks dissolve into a field of data); (5) clearance is a zero-sum budget, and proximity flips at 2.0 — every extra pixel of inter-line gap must be paid for by eating the 8 px clearance to the cube top (Y580) or lifting the whole block toward the wordmark (ink ends Y498); at 2.0 the white gap between the lines is ~17 px while line 2 sits only ~8 px above the cube — by proximity, line 2 is then nearer the cube than it is to line 1, so it risks grouping with the hero as its caption, not with line 1 as the second half of the tagline; that flip, not the wordmark, is the hard limit on widening here. TESTABLE CHANGE: leading 2.0 ONLY — line spacing 1.2 -> 2.0 (13 pt x 2.0 = 26.0 pt baseline-to-baseline, +10.4 pt, ~+10 px on the last line); block X282 unchanged, Y537–577 -> Y527–577 (lift the whole block 10 px; height 40 px -> 50 px); line 2 glyphs end stays ~Y572, clearance to the cube top Y580 stays ~8 px; line 1 top moves to ~Y527, gap to the wordmark ink (ends Y498) falls from 39 px to 29 px; everything else locked (Regular 13 pt, tracking 150, all white, left-aligned at X282). Anchoring line 2 (rather than anchoring the top and letting line 2 drop) is essential — without the 10 px lift, line 2 would end at ~Y582, past the cube top Y580, and the 8 px clearance would be gone. Rejected: 1.4 (+2.6 px, invisible, no pause); 1.5 (dead zone — white between lines ~6–7 px -> ~10–11 px, not enough to be a break, enough to thin the block and make line 2 look lighter, not darker); 1.6–1.7 (same failure, worse — taller block, line 1 nearer the wordmark, still no clear break, reads as typesetting looseness); 2.2+ / a full blank line (inter-line gap ~20 px dwarfs the 8 px cube clearance, line 2 unambiguously groups with the cube, block splits into two labels, top crowds the wordmark); widening without lifting (at 1.5 line 2 ends ~Y576, clearance ~4 px; at 2.0 it ends ~Y582, colliding with the cube top — fails the stated clearance outright); changing size or tracking to compensate (changes two variables at once, confounds the test); a rule, dot or dash between the lines, or re-centring/right-aligning line 2 (not leading, out of scope, a new mark would compete with the red wedge and the measurement hairlines, re-alignment would break the X282 left edge). Falsification (export at 1080x1080, viewed at 100% and at thumbnail/arm's length; numbers first: line 2 glyphs must end ~Y572 and clearance to Y580 must be ~8 px, or the test was not run as specified): the 2.0 change is WRONG — revert to 1.2 and close the leading question — if a fresh reader calls it two separate bits of text, or reads line 2 as a caption to the cube rather than the second half of the tagline (cover line 1 — if line 2 still reads naturally on its own above the cube, it has detached; in the squint/thumbnail test the block shows two grey bars separated by a white band visibly wider than the 8 px band below line 2, so the block has split); line 2 is missed, read first (because it is nearest the cube), or read as an afterthought with no extra weight (the pause became a gap, not a beat); line 1 at Y527 feels attached to the wordmark (29 px gap) or the block as a whole looks like two floating lines rather than one compact unit. Pass, and only then adopt 2.0, if a fresh reader still attributes both lines to one tagline, reads line 1, pauses, and reports line 2 landing harder / as a second, darker thought — with the 8 px cube clearance intact. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study, if 1.2 survives): Loop 50 — is it the break/position, not the pause — should line 2 be set apart by where it starts, not by how far it sits below line 1? Test a small indent of line 2 only (line 1 stays at X282, leading stays 1.2), as in Yes, of course it hurts and THE ONE... IS -> YOU, where the punchline is separated by alignment/position, not by leading.
- Loop 49 build notes (v49, 2026-11-07): the pause between the sentences — leading 2.0 TESTED, FAILED, REVERTED to 1.2. The study named one decisive jump: leading 2.0 with the block lifted so line 2 stays anchored. FIRST BUILD: line spacing set to exactly 2.0, box Y537 -> Y527 (10 px lift). VERIFICATION (main Muse, from the export) showed the test was NOT run as specified — the box height grew 14.1 px (40 -> 54.1 px), not the ~10 px the brief estimated (the brief's pt math omitted the px scale factor: baseline-to-baseline went 19.8 px -> 34.7 px, +14.9 px), so line 2 ended at Y575.5–576 with only ~3–4.5 px clearance to the cube top, not the specified ~Y572 / ~8 px. CORRECTION: box lifted a further 4 px to Y523. CORRECTED BUILD VERIFIED: v49 sha256 f528a4be, 2,752 px changed vs v46, bbox x282–629, y527–552 (tagline band only — line 2 did not move relative to v46, exactly the anchor design); line 1 ink (527, 538), line 2 ink (561, 572); line 2 ends Y572 exactly, clearance 7 px (~8 px specified); inter-line white gap 22 px; wordmark-to-line-1 gap 29 px (39 -> 29 px, as predicted); label grey px 90, still clear. FALSIFICATION: the 2.0 change FAILS on the first criterion — at the 270 px thumbnail the block shows two grey bars separated by a white band (22 px) visibly wider than the 7 px band below line 2, so the block has split; by proximity (22 px above line 2 vs 7 px below it) line 2 groups with the cube as its caption, not with line 1 as the second half of the tagline; covering line 1 leaves line 2 reading naturally on its own above the cube. The study's prior was right: the full stop after 'ON.' plus the line break already IS the pause, and widening only makes line 2 more alone, not darker. VERDICT: revert to 1.2 and close the leading question. Revert sent (line spacing 2.0 -> 1.2, box Y523 -> Y537); the reverted export must verify byte-identical to v46 (sha256 7a013c3a…) before the loop is reported. LESSON: when a brief estimates a box-height change from pt math, verify the px scale factor from the export — Canva's 13 pt at 1080 px is ~1.27 px/pt, so a 1.2 -> 2.0 spacing change moves the last line ~15 px, not ~10 px; always anchor the LAST line's glyph end and compute the lift from the measured baseline-to-baseline change, not from the pt difference. v49-corrected PNG: ~/workspace/browser_downloads/sess-677548892/browser-download-20261107T003650.478734454Z-0-BLACK_BOX_test_card.png (389,184 bytes). Running total: ~1,223 + ~50 (loop 49 study) = ~1,273 designs studied through loop 49.
- Loop 50 study (24 designs, running total ~1,297): should line 2 be set apart by where it starts — a small indent of line 2 only? VERDICT: Do NOT indent line 2. Indent 0 px; line 2 stays flush at X282. Top-5: (1) deliberate versus mistake is a size threshold, and at 13 pt it is high — a step that reads as deliberate is at least a full word wide (~2–3 em, ~20–30% of the line's width) and happens at display size ('God don't / make no / junk!' — 'make no' pushed right by about a word, 'junk!' returns to the left; 'POCKET / ROCKET / FOUNDATION.' — middle line indented, third line returns left); a step of +8 px (0.6 em) or +13 px (1 em) at 13 pt is about one capital letter wide and, against hairlines measured to the pixel, reads as a typesetting slip or a ragged edge, not a decision; no grid showed a small, quiet, two-line, same-size/same-weight/same-colour tagline with only line 2 nudged right by a few pixels — that absence across 24 designs is itself evidence; (2) right is the only direction with precedent; left has none, and here it is actively harmful — every indented second line found pushes right, toward the image; a left step (outdent/hanging indent, e.g. line 2 at X258) has no precedent for a tagline and on this card would cross the X274 vertical hairline and enter the '746 × 420' label's column (label at X223, Y563; line 2 sits at Y~557–577, overlapping vertically); right, by contrast, pushes line 2 toward the cube — the very element loop 49 found line 2 grouping with; (3) the left edge is the block's only anchor, and an indent destroys it — X282 sits just 8 px right of the X274 hairline, the tagline's tie to the measurement system; indenting line 2 creates a second left edge, so the block stops being one block; that is loop 49's failure in the horizontal axis (leading 2.0 split the block vertically so line 2 grouped with the cube; an indent splits it horizontally so line 2 stops belonging to line 1 and starts belonging to the cube/wedge area on its right); in the 3-line examples a third line returns to the left edge and restores it — BLACK BOX has no third line, so there is no return; (4) the right edge cannot afford any rightward step — line 2 already ends at X618, 50 px past the wedge point X568; +26 px takes it to X644 (76 px past), the word-aligned step to ~X662 (94 px past); the indent buys punchline separation by spending the only right-edge datum the card gives, and makes line 2 — already the longer line — the widest, most protruding element in the block; (5) what actually separates a punchline in the grids is not a small indent — it is a large step, a staircase in which every line steps, a return line, a change of size/weight/colour, brackets, a dash, or a separate column; loops 47 and 48 have already closed colour (all white) and weight (both Regular), so those tools are not available here; the direct analogues for a two-line sentence whose second line is the punchline are all flush ('Insights thrive in / unlikely company' — both lines share one left edge; 'DESIGN IS NOT DECORATION / IT'S COMMUNICATION'; 'DREAM / BIGGER') — in those, the line break alone does the work. CHANGE TO TEST: indent 0 px — a FREEZE; line 1 stays at X282, line 2 stays at X282, leading stays 1.2, both lines stay white Montserrat Regular, block X282, Y537–577, glyphs ending ~Y572, ~8 px above the cube top edge Y580. Rejected: small right indent +8 px (X290, end X626 — below one capital width, reads as a mistake at thumbnail, breaks the flush edge for no perceptible step); +13 px, 1 em (X295, end X631 — still about one capital, borderline, looks like an alignment slip against a measurement system drawn to the pixel); +26 px, 2 em (X308, end X644 — the first step that would read as deliberate, but X308 aligns to nothing on the card, and its end is 76 px past the wedge point); word-aligned +44 px (X326, end ~X662 — the only indent that looks like a system, 'F' under the 'S' of 'SYSTEMS'; rejected because its end is 94 px past the wedge point and it moves line 2 44 px closer to the cube, so it groups with the cube rather than with line 1); left outdent −24 px (X258, end X594 — hanging indent, crosses the X274 hairline, enters the label column, breaks the measurement system, no grid precedent); right-aligning line 2 to end at the wedge point X568 (forces line 2 to start at X232, left of the hairline and into the label column — trades the right-edge problem for the left-edge problem); indent plus extra leading, or a staircase (loop 49 already closed leading; a staircase needs a third line or a moved line 1, which is not the question asked and would change the block's height and its gap to the cube). Falsification (A = flush X282, B = word-aligned X326, identical except line 2's X; checked at 100% and at thumbnail/poster distance with a fresh reader): the 0 px decision STANDS if in B line 2 reads as a separate line grouped with the cube/wedge rather than with line 1, B's right edge (94 px past X568) reads as overshooting or ignoring the wedge, at thumbnail B's step collapses into a ragged edge, or the fresh reader cannot say what B's step is for or calls it a mistake (negative control: a +8 px version — if the reader calls it a mistake, the threshold finding is confirmed). The 0 px decision is FALSIFIED only if ALL of these hold: B's step is instantly read as deliberate at thumbnail size; B still reads as one block with line 1; B's right-edge overshoot is not noticed and does not weaken the wedge point; and A, by comparison, reads flat — 'THE FAILURES YOU NEVER SEE.' does not land as a second thought in A. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 51 — IS IT THE BLOCK, NOT THE LINE? Loop 49's failure was line 2 grouping with the cube, a proximity problem of the whole block, and an indent only moves line 2 closer to the cube. Test moving the whole flush block as one unit: both lines stay at X282, leading stays 1.2, block moves up 12 px (Y537–577 -> Y525–565; glyphs end ~Y560, so the gap to the cube top edge Y580 grows from ~8 px to 20 px). Do not spend loop 51 on re-breaking alone: the two sentences total 53 characters, so any two-line split from X282 leaves one line at least ~330 px wide and ending past X568 — a re-break trades one overshoot for the other. LIMIT (study's own): the brief's two cited analogues, 'Yes, of course it hurts' and 'THE ONE... IS -> YOU', were not found in the grids searched and were not treated as evidence.
- Loop 50 build notes (v50, 2026-11-07): line 2 indent — FREEZE, no visual change. Verdict: do NOT indent line 2; indent 0 px, line 2 stays flush at X282. A deliberate step is at least a full word wide (~2–3 em, 20–30% of the line's width) at display size; +8 px or +13 px at 13 pt is about one capital letter wide and reads as a typesetting slip against hairlines measured to the pixel; the left edge (X282, 8 px right of the X274 hairline) is the block's only anchor and an indent destroys it — loop 49's failure in the horizontal axis, with no third line to return to the edge; the right edge cannot afford any step (line 2 already ends X618, 50 px past the wedge point X568); the direct analogues for a two-line sentence whose second line is the punchline are all flush — the line break alone does the work. No rebuild was sent; nothing was touched. v50 = v46 byte-identical (sha256 7a013c3a…, 389,313 bytes — the design is already at the decided state, verified after the loop 49 revert). No indent.' v50 PNG: ~/workspace/browser_downloads/sess-677548892/browser-download-20261107T003928.382473528Z-1-BLACK_BOX_test_card.png (same file as v46). - Loop 51 study (24 designs, running total ~1,321): should the whole flush tagline block lift 12 px as one unit? VERDICT: Do NOT lift the block. The current ~8 px clearance is already right. The slot between the wordmark and the cube is only 82 px tall (wordmark ink ends Y498; cube top Y580) and the tagline block is 40 px tall — only 42 px of slack in total. Today it is split 39 px above the block and ~3 px below it (box terms), so the block is unambiguously grouped with the cube. Lifting 12 px makes it ~27 px above and 20 px below (glyph gaps ~31 px and 20 px): roughly equidistant from both neighbours, grouped with neither. A decisive 'independent statement' position does not exist in this slot: lifting ~30 px (block Y507–547) would leave only ~9 px below the wordmark ink and still only ~33 px to the cube, reading as a crowded subtitle of BLACK BOX. The geometry, not taste, forces a choice between 'caption of the cube' and 'subtitle of the wordmark' — and this tagline is the card's thesis, not a subtitle. Top-5: (1) proximity decides the reading, and there are only two stable readings — caption or independent statement; the middle is drift (small text either sits at ~half a line-height or less from the hero and is read as its caption/label, or it sits several line-heights away AND is anchored to something else; nothing strong sits at ~one line-height from both neighbours with no anchor; the proposed 20 px gap is exactly one tagline line, the unstable middle); (2) air only reads as breathing room when it is visibly deliberate — ~2–3x the tagline's line-height, or explained by a rule, grid line or edge; a 12 px move on a 1080 px canvas (1.1% of the height) is below the threshold of intentionality and reads as a nudge or a mistake, not a decision; (3) lifting the block does not just loosen the cube — it reassigns the block to the wordmark above (a 128 pt wordmark ~10x the tagline's size wins any grouping contest once the distances are comparable; small text tucked under a giant masthead reads as subtitle or credit, never as an independent thesis); (4) left-edge alignment is doing as much grouping work as vertical distance — the tagline at X282 sits 8 px from the cube's left edge X274, so it already reads as the cube's caption by alignment as well as by proximity; keeping X282 while lifting vertically would leave a text that still points at the cube but no longer belongs to it — a floating label (detachment would require breaking the alignment too, which the loop did not propose); (5) on a black field, a small white block needs a neighbour; unanchored white text reads as an error, not minimalism (black-field designs that isolate text give it an edge or frame to belong to); the measurement system reinforces this — the '746 × 420' label at X223, Y563 currently sits level with line 2, inside the tagline's vertical band, as part of the same technical-caption system; lifting the block would put the label ~3 px below the lifted line 2's glyphs (~Y560), a new near-touch that would read as an orphaned third line. CHANGE TO TEST: KEEP — lock block X282, Y537–577; both lines flush at X282; leading 1.2; glyphs end ~Y572; glyph-to-cube gap ~8 px; glyph-to-wordmark gap ~39 px. 8 px is 0.4x the ~20 px line-height, squarely inside the tight-caption band the strongest examples use, while 20 px is 1.0x the line-height, the ambiguous band no strong example uses without a second anchor. Rejected: lift 12 px to Y525–565 (middle distance, equidistant, reassigns the thesis to the wordmark, creates the ~3 px near-touch with the label); lift ~30 px to Y507–547 (top only ~9 px below the wordmark ink, crowds the wordmark, reads as its subtitle, still only ~33 px to the cube); lift 6 px to Y531–571 (gap ~14 px still inside the caption band, changes nothing testable, invisible at poster viewing distance); move the block down to close the gap (e.g. Y541–581, gap ~4 px — risks the glyphs touching the cube top edge, turns the tagline into a literal label on the cube, loses the black channel); widen the leading instead (already tested and failed in Loop 49); add a rule or divider between tagline and cube (adds an element not in the brief, competes with the two measurement hairlines and the label). Falsification (export at 1080x1080, 100% and 50% scale, plus a squint/blur check): keeping 8 px is WRONG if line 2's glyphs visually touch or merge with the cube's top edge or its upper-left vertex area at (568,758) so the black channel disappears; a fresh reader groups line 2 with the cube (reads it as a label on the cube's top face) instead of reading both lines as one block, or misses line 2 altogether; the measured glyph-to-cube gap in the rendered export is under 4 px; or in a blind side-by-side the lifted variant Y525–565 is picked as clearer by a majority and the keep variant is described as 'cramped' rather than 'captioned'. If none of those holds — both lines read as one block, the cube reads as a separate object, and a clear if narrow black channel is visible at both scales — the 8 px clearance is confirmed and the lift is dead. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 52 — IS IT THE ANCHOR, NOT THE AIR? Test aligning the tagline's left edge exactly to the cube's left edge (X282 -> X274, an 8 px shift left), holding Y537–577 and leading 1.2, to strengthen the caption alignment that the 8 px vertical gap already creates; versus holding X282.

Lifting reassigns the thesis to the wordmark (a 128 pt wordmark ~10x the tagline's size wins any grouping contest once distances are comparable) and creates a ~3 px near-touch with the '746 × 420' label at Y563. - Loop 52 study (30+ designs, running total ~1,351): should the tagline's left edge align exactly to the cube's left edge (X282 -> X274)? VERDICT: HOLD X282. Do not shift the tagline to X274. The 8 px is not stray air — it is the clearance to the X274 measurement hairline, and it repeats the 8 px vertical clearance from the tagline glyphs (end ~Y572) to the cube's top edge (Y580). Shifting to X274 would trade a consistent 8 px module for a collision, for a gain invisible at normal viewing size. Top-5: (1) flush earns its place only when nothing intervenes and the flush is repeated — BLACK BOX fails that test, because the X274 hairline and the '746 × 420' label sit between the tagline and the cube; flush to the cube would mean flush to the hairline as well, two anchors in one place; (2) a small offset reads as a near-miss, not a decision — unless it is a repeated module (on its own, 8 px on 1080 px = 0.74% is in the worst zone, too small to read as an intentional inset, too small to read as flush; on this card it escapes only because it is not a one-off — 8 px left of the hairline = 8 px above the cube; a repeated 8 px is a system, a single 8 px is a mistake); (3) at 13 pt, an 8 px shift is visible with guides at 100% and invisible everywhere else (0.46 em, ~two-thirds of a cap height; 4 px at a 540 px-wide view, 2 px at a 270 px thumbnail) — the shift buys nothing for a normal viewer while costing something for a close reader; the tagline's job is a caption, read at close range, so its alignment should be judged at 100%, not in the feed; (4) in technical and measurement graphics, text never sits on a dimension line (labels and numbers always sit clear of rules and leader lines, with a gap of at least ~half a cap height) — that is the card's own genre ('ENGINEERING DOCUMENTARY', a measurement system of hairlines, ticks and a dimension label); putting the tagline's first 'T' (both lines begin with 'T') on the X274 hairline would break the genre's first rule, and at Y563 the hairline is the separator between the '746 × 420' label on its left and the tagline on its right — at X274 the separator has no clearance on its right side, and the label, hairline and tagline collapse into one crowded column; (5) successful captions are anchored to their own column or container, not necessarily to the hero (masthead flush, body type inset — ImagineFX, TEASER); the giant BLACK BOX wordmark is the flush element, the 13 pt tagline is the inset body element, and its anchor is already present — the X274 hairline, with an 8 px offset — not the cube's bounding-box edge (X274 is the cube image's left edge, while the cube's drawn upper-left vertex is at (568,758); there is no straight drawn vertical edge at X274 for the eye to align the tagline to, except the hairline itself). CHANGE TO TEST: HOLD — 0 px change; lock X282 as the card's left caption column, with the X274 hairline 8 px to its left and the cube top 8 px below its glyphs. Rejected: X274 flush to the cube left edge (hairline collision, label at Y563, gains ~0.74% of width, invisible at normal size, breaks the 8 px module, aligns to a bounding-box edge not the drawn vertex); X274 plus move the hairline to X266 (the hairline must stay at X274 to measure the cube's true left edge — moving it makes the measurement false and disturbs the label's relationship to the line it labels); X266, a deliberate 16 px inset (pushes the tagline further from the cube, weakens the caption grouping loop 49 showed matters, breaks the 8 px vertical module for no evidenced gain); X290 or a larger inset to the right (tagline drifts toward the centre, loses its left-column relationship with the spine and footer); centre the tagline over the cube (cube centre X647 — centred captions work only where the hero is centred and symmetrical; here the cube is lower-right, the spine and footer are left-column, and loop 50 closed the tagline as left-aligned at X282); align the tagline to the wedge point X568 or right-align it to the cube's right edge X1020 (no anchor supports either; the first puts the tagline mid-cube, the second requires re-breaking both lines and detaches the tagline from the hairline/label column entirely). Falsification (A at X282, B at X274, identical except line 2's X; judged at 100% and 200% zoom, plus 50%/phone size): the hold is WRONG if in B at 100% neither 'T' touches or crosses the X274 hairline and the label at Y563 still reads as a separate label; in a blind comparison viewers unprompted call A a misalignment while no viewer notes a collision in B; or measuring ink, the first glyphs in B sit at least 2 px clear of the hairline because of Montserrat's side bearing and a 1 px rule. The hold STANDS if in B the hairline touches, underlines or strikes through either 'T' at 100–200%, or the label and line 2 read as one line at Y563, or at 50% A and B are indistinguishable to every viewer. RESOLUTION OF THE STUDY'S KEY UNCERTAINTY (main Muse, from the card's verified history): the study did not know the hairline's vertical extent, but the card's own record settles it — the X274 vertical runs Y560→990 (established loops 27/28/40, verified in exports: the vertical at y700 = (67,65,62) = #43413E, top ticks at Y560). It therefore DOES run beside line 2 (Y561–572) through the tagline zone, so in variant B line 2's 'T' would sit on the hairline — the hold stands on the evidence, not just on the study's reasoning. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 53 — IS IT THE HAIRLINE, NOT THE TAGLINE? Should the X274 measurement hairline terminate at the cube's top edge Y580, instead of running up beside the tagline through Y537–577? If the hairline is only a cube measurement, its presence in the tagline zone is what creates the false 'near-miss' reading; shortening it would let X282 read as a clean caption inset, and would make the falsification test above decisive.

- Loop 53 study (25 designs, running total ~1,376): should the X274 measurement hairline terminate at the cube's top edge Y580, instead of running up beside the tagline? VERDICT: Yes — shorten it, but at BOTH ends, not only at the top. THE ARITHMETIC (verified by main Muse against the card's own record): the cube is 746×420 at (274,580), bottom edge Y1000; the current X274 hairline runs Y560→990 = 430 px long — 10 px longer than the 420 it is labelled with, 20 px above the cube top and 10 px above the cube bottom. It measures a 430 px interval shifted up from the cube, not the cube. It runs beside the tagline for 17 px of block height (560–577), 12 px beside actual glyphs (560–572), only 8 px to its left. (This inconsistency has been there since loop 27: the vertical was 430 px when the cube was 684×385 — it never matched the cube's height; the label claimed the cube's dimensions while the line measured a different interval.) Top-5: (1) a dimension line is object-bound — it starts and stops at what it measures (KITCHEN SHOP DRAWING's chains terminate exactly at the object's edges; Ray Gun leaders stop at the gun outline and at the label; exploded-view leaders stop exactly at each part and never continue past the topmost platter or bottommost base into the header or footer); no example runs a dimension/leader line on past its object into an unrelated text zone; (2) a tick is the terminus, not a decoration — it must sit on the end of the dimension line, at the measured edge (a tick floating above the line end claims a different interval); the top tick at Y560 does NOT need the hairline to reach it — if the hairline moves, the tick must move with it, or the tick is orphaned; the interval [Y560, Y990] is not a sacred datum (430, not 420); (3) the only legitimate overshoot is a small, unlabelled extension line — and this is not one (in CAD, extension lines project a few px/mm beyond the dimension line, with a small gap from the object, and do not carry the label; BLACK BOX's overshoot is 20 px at the top and 10 px at the bottom, is the dimension line itself, and carries a tick and a numeric label — an order of magnitude larger than a CAD overshoot, and exactly the part of the line that invades the tagline zone); (4) a rule beside unrelated text groups with that text — unless it is visibly not a dimension (Kelman's A3 Grid System: red grid lines run the full poster height and text sits beside them, but the lines have no ticks and no numeric label, text sits inside grid cells with a clear gutter, and the lines are visibly the structure; once BLACK BOX adds a 12 px tick at each end and '746 × 420' beside the top tick, X274 stops being neutral structure and becomes a claim about a specific interval — and a line making a claim that runs 12–17 px beside a two-line caption makes the caption read as part of, or a caption to, that interval; that is the false 'near-miss' reading loop 52 named); (5) paired rules do not have to share endpoints; staggered ends signal different functions (KITCHEN SHOP DRAWING has several dimension chains, each only as long as its own segment, staggered against each other; shared Y560/Y990 is precisely what makes X274 and X53.7 read as one system measuring one (wrong) interval — staggering them is how a viewer tells a spine from a dimension). TESTABLE CHANGE: X274 hairline Y560→990 becomes Y580→Y1000 (same X274, same 2 px, same #43413E; length exactly 420 px, matching the '420' in the label); top tick Y560 → Y580; bottom tick Y990 → Y1000 (same 12 px, same #989CA1, same X274); label '746 × 420' X223 unchanged, Y563 → Y583 (same text, size 7 pt, colour #989CA1, Montserrat Regular — keeps it exactly 3 px below the new top tick, as now, but beside the cube's top-left corner instead of in the tagline zone); X53.7 hairline UNCHANGED at Y560→990, ticks at Y560/Y990 (it remains the lower-zone/page spine; X274 becomes the cube dimension; their staggered ends are the point); tagline, wordmark, cube, red wedge, left spine and footer unchanged. WHY: it makes the dimension true (420, not 430), removes the 17 px parallel run beside the tagline, and leaves a clean 3 px vertical gap (577→580) between tagline bottom and hairline top, so the 8 px horizontal inset (282−274) can finally read as a caption inset, as loop 52 hypothesised; the label stays attached to its tick, and the cube's left edge gains a true datum instead of a near-miss. Rejected: keep Y560→990 (fails on arithmetic 430 ≠ 420 and on evidence — no blueprint runs a labelled dimension line through unrelated text); shorten top only, Y580→990 (length becomes 410, 10 px short; bottom tick still floats 10 px above the cube bottom — trades a +20 error for a −10 error); shorten top only and leave the top tick at Y560 (orphaned tick 20 px above the line end; the reader cannot tell whether the interval is 560–990 or 580–990); shorten to Y577 (tagline bottom) or Y572 (glyph end) (arbitrary, still 3–8 px above the cube top, length 413/418, still wrong, creates a new near-miss just above the cube); move the hairline off the cube edge (e.g. left to X266, with extension lines, proper CAD style — a bigger redesign, breaks the established 8 px X282/X274 inset relationship, adds new elements, and still has to stop at Y580/Y1000); delete the X274 hairline entirely (removes the cube's left-edge datum and the anchor for '746 × 420'; the card's whole language is measurement, and the label would have nothing to attach to); move the tagline away/right/down (loops 47–52 closed the tagline; moving it reopens closed questions and does not fix 430 ≠ 420); keep the hairline and move the label only, or keep the label at Y563 after shortening (the label would float 17 px above the new top tick, detached in the tagline zone); keep both hairlines sharing Y560/Y990 and re-label the interval as 430 (contradicts the cube's stated 746 × 420 size and the card's own label). Falsification (export the change, inspect at 100% and at thumbnail/arm's-length; numbers first: hairline length 420 ±1 px, ticks at exactly Y580 and Y1000, label at Y583 — if any is off, the test is invalid, not the idea): PASS if the tagline reads as a caption to the cube, not to the line; the top tick touches the cube image's top-left corner and the bottom tick touches its bottom-left corner with no gap or overshoot >1 px; the label sits beside the top tick, clear of the tagline; and the staggered X274 (580–1000) vs X53.7 (560–990) reads as two different functions. PROVE IT WRONG if: (a) viewers still group the tagline with the hairline, or still report a near-miss — in particular if the 3 px gap 577→580 is too tight and the line still feels continuous with the caption; (b) the staggered tops/bottoms read as a drafting error or broken pair, with viewers asking why the two verticals do not align; (c) the bottom tick at Y1000 collides with or crowds the two-item footer (footer Y1020 — 20 px clearance, same as the cube's own bottom edge; must be checked in the export, not assumed); (d) the top tick at Y580 is lost against the cube's top edge, or is confused with the red wedge's point at (568,758); (e) the label at Y583 is read as a caption to the cube image rather than as the dimension label, or is lost against the cube edge. If (b) is what fails — the pair must stay aligned — the next-best question, loop 54, is: 'Is X274 a spine, not a dimension?' Test by stripping the ticks and the '746 × 420' label from X274, keeping Y560→990, and seeing whether it then reads as a neutral grid spine beside a clean caption.

- Loop 54 study (27 designs, running total ~1,403): does the left spine earn its place? VERDICT: Keep the spine, keep its words, size, spacing, position and rotation — change ONLY its colour, from red to the measurement grey #989CA1. It earns its place as a genre label and a quiet left-edge marker; it does not earn its place in red. Top-5: (1) a spine earns its place only when it carries information found nowhere else — 'ENGINEERING DOCUMENTARY' passes (neither the tagline, wordmark, nor footer names the genre; delete it and the documentary claim vanishes); (2) vertical text works when it IS the title, large, or when it is neutral microcopy — tiny plus accent colour is the worst combination (BLACK BOX's spine is the opposite on all four counts: 12 pt, 313 px of ink, red, only 29% of the 1080 px height; at that size a spine can only be a filing label, like a book spine — legitimate, but it must then behave like a label: quiet, not loud); (3) one accent, one job — red must be reserved for the wedge (Lissitzky's 'Red Wedge' uses red for the diagonal alone; Müller-Brockmann's 'Musica Viva' uses diagonal arcs with horizontal microcopy; the Made Up Studio covers use one diagonal or illustration as the energy, with horizontal titles); BLACK BOX spends red twice, at opposite ends of the card — a small red label at top-left (Y42–355) and a large red wedge pointing to (568,758) at lower-right; the two never touch or cross, so the competition is not spatial, it is colour-hierarchical — the eye is given two red starting points, and the smaller, earlier one (top-left, where Western reading starts) pulls attention away from the wedge's point, the card's one deliberate focal gesture; (4) a spine must align, run, or frame something; a short, top-only, unaligned spine reads as an orphan, not an anchor (the spine ink Y42–355 ends 19 px above the wordmark, then a 205 px gap before the X53.7 hairline begins at Y560; it does not align with the tagline, the cube top, or the footer; its only structural rhyme is a broken left axis — red text above, grey hairline below, wordmark interrupting the middle — broken by colour as well as by the gap); (5) the diagonal, the measurement system and the giant wordmark already fill the three directional jobs; a spine can survive only as the quiet fourth voice, in the measurement colour — a spine in #989CA1 stops being a second red voice and becomes the top extension of the measurement system (grey text above, grey ticks and hairlines below, one left axis, interrupted — deliberately — by the white wordmark). The spine should say the genre and only the genre ('ENGINEERING DOCUMENTARY' is the right text; do not change it to 'BLACK BOX' or 'VOL. 01', which would duplicate the wordmark and footer); keep it rotated 90°, reading bottom-to-top as a left spine; keep Montserrat Regular 12 pt and letter-spacing 160 (wide tracking is what makes rotated Latin microcopy legible, and 12 pt is the correct register for a label one step below the 13 pt white tagline); keep it in the margin at X20.1; do not enlarge it (enlarging it to 'Jinxed'/Nike scale would turn a label into a second wordmark and would require longer text to fill the height). TESTABLE CHANGE: spine colour red → #989CA1 (the existing tick grey), nothing else — same words, size, spacing, rotation, position. WHY: it preserves the spine's two real contributions (the only statement of genre, and the only element in the top-left margin) while removing its one real fault, the second red voice; it uses a colour already on the card, so it introduces no new palette value; it creates a coherent grey scale (white ExtraBold wordmark 128 pt > white tagline 13 pt > grey spine 12 pt #989CA1 > grey hairlines #43413E 2 px — each step quieter, each voice distinct); it makes the broken left axis read as one system; and it leaves the wedge as the only red on the card, so the red now means one thing — the diagonal that points at the cube. Why not white: white is the wordmark/tagline voice; a white spine would be louder, not quieter. Why not #43413E: a 2 px hairline colour, too dark for 12 pt type on black and effectively illegible. Rejected: delete the spine outright (fails on content — the only place 'ENGINEERING DOCUMENTARY' appears; also leaves the entire left margin Y0–560 empty above the X53.7 hairline and removes the book-spine metaphor); keep it red (fails finding 3 — two red voices); recolour it white (joins the primary white voice, competes with the wordmark/tagline); recolour it #43413E (illegible); move it down to run alongside the cube, e.g. ink Y580–893 at X20.1 (sits 33.6 px from the X53.7 hairline, forming an unintended double vertical, crosses the wedge's entry from the left edge and crowds the tagline); enlarge it to a 'Jinxed'/Nike/'Call of the Night' scale (becomes a second wordmark; 'ENGINEERING DOCUMENTARY' cannot fill 1080 px without added words); turn it horizontal as a kicker above the wordmark (duplicates the tagline's horizontal left-aligned role, loses the vertical anchor and the book-spine idea, crowds the wordmark whose ink top Y374 is only 19 px below the spine's ink end Y355); change the text to 'BLACK BOX', 'VOL. 01' or a date (duplicates the wordmark or footer, wastes the one place the genre is named); move it to the right edge (no margin there — the cube's edge to X1020 and the footer's 'VOL. 01'; a right spine would fight the cube); shrink it or tighten its tracking (does not address the colour competition, makes an already small label less legible at thumbnail size). Falsification (A = red spine, B = #989CA1 spine, identical except spine colour; viewed at 100%, 50%, and as a 270 px thumbnail, at arm's length; 5-second glance test with at least three viewers, asking afterwards without prompting (a) what the card is about / what genre it is, and (b) where the red shape points): the grey change is WRONG, and red should be restored, if in B and not in A: (i) genre recall collapses (fewer than 3 of 5 viewers can name 'engineering documentary' after 5 seconds, or the spine is illegible at 100% on black); (ii) the left edge reads as empty and the wordmark looks unanchored at thumbnail size in B, where it looked held in A; (iii) the wedge looks stranded — viewers describe the red as isolated or unbalanced, with all the red in the lower half and nothing to answer it at the top; (iv) at thumbnail size the grey spine, grey ticks and grey hairlines merge into an unreadable smudge, while the red spine remained a distinct mark. The change is CONFIRMED if in B the first fixation order is wordmark → wedge point (568,758) → cube, the wedge is named as the only red element, and genre recall is the same as in A (or better, because the spine is no longer mistaken for a second headline). Also check on export, before judging: that the spine reads bottom-to-top (head tilts left); if it reads top-to-bottom, flip its rotation — that is a direction correction, not a second design change, and it should be recorded separately. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 55 — once the spine is grey, does the X53.7 hairline (Y560→990) still earn its place, or is it now a duplicate of the spine's left axis? (Plus the wedge-entry question: is the wedge's entry point on the left edge legible at thumbnail size?)

- Loop 55 study (30+ designs, running total ~1,433): once the spine is grey, does the X53.7 hairline still earn its place? VERDICT: DELETE it entirely. Top-5: (1) a dimension earns its place only by endpoints plus a value — X274 passes, X53.7 fails (in technical drawings every dimension line touches the measured edges at both ends and carries a number; a line in the same 2 px #43413E / #989CA1-tick vocabulary as X274, but with no label and endpoints that touch nothing, is read not as a second dimension but as a failed or stray one); (2) a spine earns its place by carrying text, not by having a rule beside it (in almost every poster examined the vertical text IS the spine and no rule accompanies it; a text spine needs no hairline chaperone — adding one beside it, 33.6 px away, creates a second axis for the same job); (3) two verticals on one side earn their places only when they do visibly different jobs in visibly different languages — or when they are a whole system, not a lone pair (a solid colour band containing text, a single line at the page edge, a full faint grid; when two lines share weight, colour and tick style, as X53.7 and X274 do, the eye reads them as twins and asks which one is the real measurement — here the answer is X274, so X53.7 is the duplicate); (4) a lone line in a margin is read as a page edge, margin or crop mark — so it must sit at an edge or align to a real margin, or it reads as a near-miss (X53.7 sits 53.7 px in from the left edge and aligns to nothing — not the tagline X282, not the cube X274, not the footer, not the spine X20.1; in Swiss and technical work the genre's contract is precision, so a top 20 px off, a bottom 10 px off and a footer gap of 30 px read as alignment errors, not as intention); (5) empty black field is a legitimate, trusted choice — minimal posters do not fill a quiet quadrant with a filler rule (the lower-left field X0–274, Y580–1020 is empty except for the wedge crossing it; that emptiness is not a hole to be propped up; the one honest job X53.7 could claim — extending verticality into the lower half where the spine (ending Y355) is absent, and bracketing the empty field with X274 — is not enough, because it does that job in X274's exact visual language without X274's justification, and because the wedge already crosses and activates that field). THE ARITHMETIC: spine ink ends Y355; the X53.7 line starts Y560 — a 205 px gap, so they do not form one continuous axis; spine box at X20.1, line at X53.7 — 33.6 px apart, two parallel left axes; X53.7 is 220.3 px left of X274; the X53.7 endpoints match nothing (top Y560 is 20 px above the cube top Y580; bottom Y990 is 10 px above the cube bottom Y1000 and 30 px above the footer Y1020; it is 430 px long and nothing on the card is 430 px; it has no label); by contrast X274 matches the cube exactly at both ends (Y580 and Y1000) and carries its value '746 × 420'. Loop 54 matters: the spine and the X53.7 ticks are now the same grey #989CA1, and the line shares X274's 2 px #43413E weight — the three left elements now read as one grey system that does not align, rather than as three distinct jobs. TESTABLE CHANGE: delete the X53.7 hairline (2 px #43413E, Y560→990) AND its two 12 px #989CA1 ticks (Y560, Y990); keep X274 (Y580→1000, ticks, label '746 × 420' at X223, Y583), the grey spine, tagline, cube, wedge, wordmark and footer exactly as specified; no replacement line, label, colour or shift. WHY: it measures nothing (no label; endpoints 20 px and 10 px off the cube; 430 px matches no element); it duplicates X274 in form without X274's function; it duplicates the spine in position (33.6 px away, same grey family since loop 54) without the spine's function (it carries no text); its 205 px gap from the spine means it does not even read as the spine's continuation. After deletion the left side has exactly one spine (text) and the measurement system has exactly one dimension (X274, labelled, exact) — one job, one mark, each. The wedge also gains a cleaner entry from the left edge, with no dark hairline crossing it in the margin (the wedge crosses X53.7 at y~579). Rejected: keep it as is (unlabelled, orphan endpoints, identical twin to X274, now in the spine's grey family — keeping it would mean keeping a mark whose only defence is habit); re-align it to the cube, Y580→1000 at X53.7 (becomes an exact duplicate dimension, 220.3 px from the real one, with no object at X53.7 to measure); label it, e.g. add '430' beside it (invents a false measurement — 430 px is not the height, width or gap of anything on the card, and a dimension that lies is worse than one that is silent); move it onto the spine's axis, or extend it full-height Y42→1000 at X53.7 (still 33.6 px from the spine text, not at the page edge, and a full-height line would cross the wedge and dominate the quiet lower field — trades a small duplicate for a large one); thin or recolour it to 1 px #989CA1 to match the spine (makes it even more like the spine, increasing the duplication, and fixes neither endpoint); keep only the ticks at Y560 and Y990, without the line (two stray 12 px ticks with no line and no object would read as printing artefacts); recolour it red #C32D30 (breaks loop 54's settlement that the wedge is the only red, and sets a red vertical against the red diagonal); merge it into X274 (deletion with an extra step, and X274 already exists). Falsification (A = with X53.7, B = without; identical except for the deletion): (a) difference check — only pixels in the strip X~47–60, Y560–990 (line plus ticks) may differ; spine, X274, label, tagline, cube, wedge and footer must be pixel-identical (if anything else moved, the test is invalid, not the change); (b) at 100% and at ~270 px thumbnail, view the deleted version alone — it passes if the lower-left field reads calm and empty, the cube still feels anchored on its left by X274 and below by the footer, and the wedge's entry from the left edge to (568,758) is easy to follow; it fails if the field reads as a hole, the cube looks as if it floats, or the wedge looks as if it enters from nowhere; (c) blind check — show the deleted version, without the original, to two or three people who have not seen the current card and ask 'what measures the cube?' and 'is anything missing on the left?' — falsified if two or more say the left side feels unfinished or point to where X53.7 was, OR if they cannot find the cube's height (that second outcome would mean X274 alone reads as a stray and the pair was a system after all, in which case reinstate X53.7 exactly as specified and do not substitute a redesigned line); (d) wedge check — trace the wedge from the left edge to (568,758) in both versions; if it is materially harder to follow in the deleted version because the X53.7 crossing gave it a reference point, the deletion is falsified on that ground alone. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 56 — once X53.7 is gone, does X274 alone still earn its place, or does a single, now-solitary dimension line look like a stray, so that the '746 × 420' label should sit closer to X274, its ticks should change, or X274 should go too?
