- Loop 76 study (24 designs, running total ~2,040; none from loops 61–75): VERDICT — ADD ONE THING, AND ONLY THAT THING. The single biggest remaining weakness is not separation, it is the seam's lost peak. After feathering, the seam is a soft, saturated blue colour band at the fold, not a light event at the fold. At full size it can read as a blue wash painted on the fold, while the right-face fringe — though fainter — is the only nearby signal with a sharp, light-like edge. Separation already holds, so the fringe and the gap should be left alone. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 76): add a single 2 px pale blue-white core, centred exactly on the C–B centreline, running the full length of the seam from about (697,767) to about (647,903) in card coordinates, on the same angle as the seam. Width: 2.0 px on the 1080 px poster (1 px each side of the centreline), ±0.5 px — 10% of the seam's 20 px total width, and about 0.5 px at 270 px, so it is effectively invisible at thumbnail. Colour: pale blue-white, not pure flat white — e.g. #DBEAFE to #BFDBFE, or white at about 85–90% opacity over the #1D4ED8 core, so the blue shows through and it reads as light through blue glass; it must be brighter than both side faces (L* ~76 pale face, L* ~36.5 dark face) and brighter than the #1D4ED8 core, making it the brightest point inside the cube. Edges: feather the core's own edges by about 0.5 px; do not give it a hard outline. Do not change anything else: seam stays about 20 px total (never below 15 px), fringe stays untouched at about 18 px east of the centreline, top fringe stays untouched, faces, tonal step, 10 px white silhouette, wedge tip at (568,758) and all type/footer stay untouched. COLLISION CHECK: no collision. The core is 1 px each side of the centreline; the fringe's cyan edge starts at about 16 px east, leaving about 15 px clear between core edge and fringe. The top fringe (655,754)–(688,758) is on the top face, north of the seam's top end (697,767). The wedge tip (568,758) is far west of the seam. The core is inside the cube, well clear of the 10 px white silhouette. No red is introduced inside the cube. The tonal step must remain ΔL* ≥35 (target ~40). Top-5: (1) a bright core on a fold outranks a hard chromatic fringe (Mahmud's 'ARIF — 3D Glass Typography', Dribbble — glass letters have a bright white highlight on each fold and a thin red/cyan/green chromatic fringe on the side edge; at full size the white fold highlight dominates per letter and the fringe is secondary, and at thumbnail the fringe almost disappears while the glint remains; Cockx / Microsoft Design's 'MICROSOFT • Teams Backgrounds', Behance — soft blue glass ribbons each have a thin bright pale core along the fold, with a faint pink/cyan fringe hugging the edge; the fold, not the fringe, is read first); (2) a clean gap of about 1.5× the fringe width is enough to separate two signals, provided the gap is a clean face colour (Anonymous Designer's 'Floral Distorted Glass', Dribbble — vertical glass strips stay separate because the gap between them is clean white; where strips crowd and the gap narrows, they merge into a dark smear; in BLACK BOX the 6 px gap is the flat dark right face (46,90,120) — a clean, uncoloured valley between the seam's feather ending at ~10 px east and the fringe's cyan edge starting at ~16 px east; that is 30% of the seam width and 1.5× the fringe width, so the two do not blur into one smear); (3) a soft band with no core reads as atmosphere, not as refraction at a fold (Washington's 'Till the light separates us', Behance — a broad soft teal beam across black dominates by width and brightness, but reads as light in air, not as a fold; Reina's 'Prism Danger', Behance — a broad soft pink/teal chromatic smear behind huge type reads as background wash; Villegas's 'Chromatic Tempest Series', Behance — wide soft chromatic bands marble and merge where they have no pale core; where a pale core is present, a band holds as a single band); (4) a thin bright line reads as light, not as a white stripe, when it is narrow, brightest, and anchored to a fold (Cherkaoui's 'Liquid Glass Orb', Dribbble — thin white/pale refraction streaks sit exactly on the orb's internal curves, brighter than the surrounding dark blue, and read as light, while wider, dimmer soft blue bands in the background do not compete; Riley for Unseen Studio's 'Expand & Contract', Dribbble — a black glass sphere's thin white rim glint reads as light on a curved surface, not a stripe, because it follows the form and is brighter than its ground; ImageSpell Studio's 'HAUTE OPTICS // Centered Spherical Loupe', Dribbble — 'Zero-distortion center with steep perimeter chromatic aberration' — the clean centre wins over the peripheral chromatic fringe because it is central and undistorted); (5) hard chromatic edges only pop when they have high contrast against their ground (Cordata's 'Chromatic Halloween', Behance — small cyan/magenta offsets on black-and-white stickers pop because the ground is black/white; in BLACK BOX the fringe is pale blue-white on dark blue-grey (46,90,120), low in luminance contrast, and its cyan/magenta edges are about 1 px each inside a 4 px fringe, next to a saturated #1D4ED8 seam — so its sharpness advantage is cancelled by its low contrast; it stays a faint echo, as intended). Rejected: leave the card alone (close second — separation holds, and at 270 px the hierarchy is already proven; rejected because dominance at full size is now only by width and saturation, not by light; the 2 px core is thumbnail-invisible, reversible, and is exactly the device the research shows converts a soft band into a light event); move the fringe further east (e.g. to 24 px) to widen the gap (6 px of clean dark face already separates the signals; moving it would push it toward the cube's right edge and weaken its parallel-to-the-fold reading, for no gain); narrow the seam to about 15 px or harden its edges back (would undo loop 75's feathering, risk a return to a hard stripe, and would not add a luminance peak); widen the core to 3–4 px, or use pure white at 100% (at 4 px it is 20% of the seam and, in the Floral and De La Rocha's 'SHAPES | PRISMS | FORMS & FOMO' examples, a wide white band reads as a stripe or a shape, not as a glint; at 270 px it would also become a visible ~1 px white line competing with the seam's blue mark); strengthen or weaken the right-face fringe, or add a core to the fringe (weakening would lose the second signal; strengthening or adding a second bright line would create two competing lights and destroy the hierarchy); add a second core, or a core on the top fringe (the top fringe must stay a 30%-opacity whisper; a second bright line would make three competing lights). FALSIFICATION: at 270 px / 3 m — the seam still reads as a single about 5 px blue mark on the fold; no white line is visible at thumbnail; the cube still has two distinct side faces and an unmistakable fold; both fringes are invisible; wordmark, tagline and wedge tip are unchanged; fail if a white mark appears at thumbnail. At full size — a viewer asked 'which is the light?' points to the fold, not to the fringe; the three signals are read in order — seam (now with a thin glint at its centre), then right-face fringe, then top fringe; seam and fringe still read as two separate lines with a visible dark gap between them; fail if the core is described as a separate white stripe, or if seam and fringe merge into one wide blue smear. Measurable check in the file: core width 2.0 px ±0.5 px, centred on the C–B line within ±0.5 px, same length as the seam; seam total width still about 20 px (≥15 px); cross-section perpendicular to the fold shows a pale core peak, a fall to #1D4ED8, a fall to the dark face, then a small pale bump for the fringe, with a trough at about 13 px east within about 5 L* of the dark face; ΔL* between the two side faces still ≥35; fringe centre still about 18 px east, width about 4 px, cyan west / magenta east; top fringe untouched; no red inside the cube; 10 px white silhouette unchanged; if any of these fail, revert the core. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 77 — after the core is added, does the brighter seam make the top-face fringe (about 2 px, 30% opacity, (655,754)–(688,758)) unreadable or redundant — should loop 77 test removing the top fringe entirely against keeping it at 30%, or does the core's brightness instead make the top fringe read more clearly as the third, faintest signal?

- Loop 76 build notes (v76, 2026-11-07): add a pale core on the seam's centreline. CHANGE: the cube image only, replaced in place via the Replace flow (image count stayed five, layer below wedge, above halos; the file was granted in the task's `files` field). New file `glass-cube-black-v76.png` (built by main Muse from v75 by adding the core on top of the seam's centreline). VERIFIED BY MAIN MUSE: v76 sha256 57b74c8f (377,075 bytes). The core is present: cross-sections at Y=820/850/900 all show the structure — #1D4ED8 (29,78,216) on both sides, then the pale core #DBEAFE (219,234,254) at the centreline, then #1D4ED8 again; at Y=850 the core reads (221,235,252), L*=92.5, and at Y=900 it reads (219,234,252), L*=92.1 — the brightest point inside the cube. - Loop 77 study (36 designs, running total ~2,076; none from loops 61–76): VERDICT — REMOVE THE TOP-FACE FRINGE ENTIRELY. The single biggest remaining weakness is that fringe: a 33 px long, ~2 px wide, ~30% opacity chromatic mark at (655,754)–(688,758), inside the solid #F5F5F5 top face. It is now both redundant and unreadable, and the brighter seam core has removed the reason it was added. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 77): delete the top fringe only — revert that strip to solid #F5F5F5, exactly as in the v72 cube top. No other change: leave the seam (saturated blue #1D4ED8, ~20 px total, with its 2 px #DBEAFE core, L* ~92, (697,767)–(647,903)), the right-face fringe (~4 px, ~18 px east of the fold), the white 10 px silhouette, the wedge and its tip at (568,758), the wordmark, tagline, microcopy and footer exactly as they are. Do not darken the top face, do not touch the seam or the right-face fringe, and do not add any red inside the cube. WHY: (1) The top face is already the brightest plane (mean L* ~96.5, against ~76 front-left and ~36.5 right, ΔL* ~40 across the fold). That tonal step alone explains 'lit glass' — the fringe adds no new information about plane orientation or light direction, which the seam already supplies. (2) The fringe is structurally orphaned: short, eastern-portion only, parallel to the lower edge of the top face but not touching it. In the work studied, refraction that reads is edge-anchored/contiguous with a fold; an isolated interior line reads as a smudge or compression artefact. (3) It fails at both viewing distances for different reasons: at 270 px it is ~0.5 px and invisible, so it contributes nothing to the thumbnail; at full size it is visible only on close inspection, where its shortness, faintness and isolation make it look accidental rather than deliberate — loop 74 had already judged it the least coherent of the three signals, and the new L* ~92 seam core has since made it the faintest signal on the brightest plane, which inverts expectation (faint signals belong in shadow, where the right-face fringe works). The premise for adding it in loop 73 — that the top would otherwise read as a 'lid' — no longer holds: the seam core now provides the light event that explains the top's brightness. COLLISION CHECK: removal creates no collision. Top stays brightest (L* ~96.5 > front-left ~76), the ΔL* ~40 step is unchanged, seam and right-face fringe are untouched, the wedge tip at (568,758) is untouched, the silhouette is untouched, and no red element is introduced inside the cube. The only risk, a 'lid' reading, is contradicted by the evidence below. Top-5: (1) a flat, bright top needs no fringe (Novelo's 'Refraction studies', Dribbble — a pale faceted cube/sphere whose top face is flat and pale, with refraction only on the side faces; it reads unmistakably as glass, the top explained by being the brightest plane bounded by clear edges); (2) solid flat tops are the norm in glass cubes (Xu's '3d Glass Cube with Spline', Dribbble — four small cubes whose top faces are solid flat colour (white, yellow, pink, blue-grey) with no refraction marks at all; refraction lives in the side faces and gradients; the tops read as glass tops by tonal step and boundary alone); (3) one strong signal explains the light; an empty interior does not hurt (Wise's 'Light Prism', Dribbble — black ground, white triangle outline, a single rainbow spectrum exiting to the right; the triangle interior is empty black, with no faint mark inside, yet it reads unmistakably as a prism; the strong external/edge signal does all the explanatory work); (4) refraction that reads is edge-anchored; isolated faint marks drown (Coyle's 'Prism', Dribbble, and Pixelbuddha's 'Abstract Prism Textures' / 'Radiance Prism Photo Effect', Dribbble — in Coyle the strong blue/purple refraction sits at the fold/edge and the rest of the glass is flat; in Pixelbuddha the beams are large, saturated and contiguous; a faint, short, isolated chromatic trace next to such a signal does not register as a second cue — it collapses into texture/noise); (5) minimal black work is one dominant signal plus silence; faintness only reads when it is long, repeated or the main signal on its own object (Weber's 'Refractions.', Dribbble — one strong chromatic-aberration wordmark on black, no faint second mark; Ayman's 'Portfolio 2026', Behance — huge black 'Portfolio', one white mark, silence elsewhere; Naumann's 'WWP°264 "traces"' and 'HOLO 4/5', Dribbble — faint chromatic traces read only because they are long, repeated and structured across the whole field, a single 33 px trace does not meet that threshold; Up-Brands' 'LUMI & VOID', Dribbble — faint dichroic signals earn their place when each is the main signal on a separate object/plane, not a third, weaker echo on the same object beside a dominant one). Rejected: keep at 30% (no build) (the 'completes the light system' hypothesis, but the system is now complete without it — seam (dominant) + right-face fringe (second) already give a strict two-step hierarchy, and the top's brightness is already explained by the seam core; keeping it preserves a mark that is invisible at 270 px and looks accidental at full size); strengthen it (e.g. to 50–60%, wider or longer) (would destroy the hierarchy by making the third signal compete with the seam, add visible noise on the brightest plane at 270 px, and still leave it an interior line unanchored to an edge; strengthening to match the seam was expressly ruled out, and any lesser strengthening does not fix the structural problem of isolation/shortness); move it to the edge / lengthen it along the whole lower edge of the top face (would put a chromatic band directly on the fold the seam already occupies in visual terms, risk blurring the top/front-left boundary, and turn a whisper into a second seam competing with the C–B fold); replace it with a subtler tonal gradient on the top face (would darken part of the brightest plane and risk dropping the top below the front-left face or weakening the tonal step; the evidence (Novelo, Xu) shows a flat top works, so no replacement is needed). FALSIFICATION: at 270 px / 3 m — no viewer should be able to tell which version is which; in both, the cube must still show two distinct side faces, an unmistakable fold, the top as the brightest plane, and the wedge tip as the first landing after the wordmark; if removal changes the thumbnail reading at all, the verdict is wrong — but the fringe is ~0.5 px there, so a thumbnail difference would itself falsify the claim that it was invisible. At full size — viewers asked 'what is the top face made of — glass or a lid?' must score v77 equal to or better than v76, must name the seam as the light event and the right-face fringe as the second signal, and must not report a smudge/artefact on the top face in v77; if v77 is reported as a 'lid'/opaque/flat more often than v76, or viewers report something missing from the top, the removal has failed and the fringe should be reconsidered (not strengthened to match the seam). Measurable check in the file: in v77 the strip (655,754)–(688,758) must sample as solid #F5F5F5 (mean L* ~96.5); the top face must remain the brightest plane (L* ~96.5 > front-left ~76); ΔL* front-left to right must remain ≥35 (target ~40); seam core #DBEAFE L* ~92, right-face fringe, 10 px white silhouette and wedge tip (568,758) must be pixel-identical to v76; the file diff must show change only in that 33 × ~2 px strip. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 78 — now that the hierarchy is seam (dominant) + right-face fringe only, does the right-face fringe (~4 px, ~18 px east of the fold) still earn its place, or does the seam core alone carry the cube? Test removing the right-face fringe against keeping it: does it still read as a refraction line in the dark face, or does it now read as a second seam competing with the fold?

- Loop 78 study (40 NEW designs, running total ~2,116; none from loops 61–77 except the recounts noted below): VERDICT — REMOVE THE RIGHT-FACE FRINGE ENTIRELY. The single biggest remaining weakness is that the fringe is a second, parallel chromatic line ~18 px east of the one fold that matters: at full size it competes with the seam and reads as a second fold / false bevel, and at 270 px / 3 m it is ~1 px and effectively invisible, so it earns its place at neither distance. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 78): delete the whole right-face fringe — the faint ~4 px-wide chromatic line, cyan west / pale core / magenta east, running approximately (707,792)–(671,890), ~18 px east of the C–B fold centreline — and leave the underlying right face as flat (46,90,120). No replacement, no widening of the seam, no lightening of the right face, no repositioning, no other change. The seam, its 2 px #DBEAFE core, all three faces, the silhouette, wedge, wordmark, tagline, microcopy and footer are untouched. WHY: in every geometric glass cube studied, dispersion sits ON a fold or edge, never as a second parallel line inside a face. Faces are carried by a flat tonal step, not by an interior line. A parallel line offset from a fold is therefore read as structure — another fold, bevel or face — not as texture. The one exception proves the rule: in abstract, geometry-free refraction work (e.g. DISPERSION by Alexey Grigoriev; Light Prism Series by Danny Ivan) many parallel streaks work because there is no fold for them to compete with; in a cube, there is. And the dark face does not need the fringe: dark, flat faces read as glass in This is Cube, Luminal Orbit and GEOMETRY OF ICE through tonal step + edge colour + silhouette alone. At 270 px the fringe contributes nothing, so removing it cannot weaken the 3 m read; at full size it removes the only competing signal. COLLISION CHECK: seam untouched (blue #1D4ED8, ~20 px total, plus 2 px pale core #DBEAFE L* ~92 on the centreline, (697,767)–(647,903), remains the sole chromatic line in the cube; the fringe lay east of it and its deletion cannot touch it); core untouched, still on the fold centreline, with no second pale line to confuse it with after removal; right face reveals the existing flat dark face (46,90,120), L* ~36.5, with no hole, gap, inpainting or re-render needed, boundaries unchanged, cube still 746×420 at (274,580); front-left face untouched, pale glass ~(199,201,189), L* ~76–80, tonal step ΔL* ~40–44 across the fold, now cleaner — one fold, one step, one line; top face untouched, plain solid #F5F5F5 (loop 77's removal stands); silhouette untouched, 10 px white stroke; red wedge untouched, centreline (0,430)→(568,758) at 30.0°, #C32D30, tip touching (568,758), nearest fringe point ~(707,792) ~140 px away; wordmark, tagline, microcopy and footer all far from the fringe (Y 792–890, X ~665–715) and unchanged; canvas geometry unchanged, only pixels inside the fringe's bounding box (approx. X 665–715, Y 785–895) change. Top-5: (1) dispersion sits ON a fold, never beside it (This is Cube — Vitalii Novoseltsev, Behance — on black, a tilted glass cube carries all its colour exactly on its fold edges, a thin rainbow line on each edge centreline, while its large dark faces are flat tonal planes with no parallel interior line; a dark face reads as glass by tonal step + edge colour + silhouette, and the fold line alone is the refraction signal); (2) the discipline of keeping colour on the fold (Glass Dispersion Logo Design & 3D Animation — Paul Rover, Behance — a small glass cube on black above the wordmark CATEGORY: dispersion is confined to the cube's edges/folds, faces are flat dark / pale blue, and the separate rainbow strip sits at the bottom of the poster, outside the cube, never inside a face parallel to a fold); (3) a dark face without an interior line is not dead, it is quiet (Luminal Orbit — Glass Cube Motion Study — negi design, Dribbble — small blue glass cubes in a ring on near-black, no interior fringe at all, cubes read through silhouette, highlight and tonal faces; directly answers the 'flat dead space' worry); (4) the physical logic — refraction colour belongs on the fold, not beside it (GEOMETRY OF ICE (Part 3) — Jan Erik Waider, Behance — real ice facets: colour and brightness sit on the fold/edge itself, each facet a flat tonal plane, no chromatic line offset from a fold); (5) if extra refraction is wanted, it should be a wash or an edge, not a hard offset line (Prism Crystal Suncatcher - 2D Animation — Yeonwoo Lee, Behance — refraction is a soft wash across the whole crystal and glow at its edges, never a hard second line parallel to an edge inside a face). Rejected: keep the fringe as-is (adds nothing at 270 px; at full size a second signal in the same colours (cyan/magenta) as the seam, parallel to it and 18 px away — the geometry of a second fold); move the fringe closer to the fold / merge it into the seam (thickens and blurs the dominant signal, risks swallowing the 2 px #DBEAFE core, changes the seam, which is not the weak element); move it further east, or make it thinner/fainter (distance does not change its reading — a fainter parallel line is still a parallel line, and a fainter one is even less visible at 270 px); replace it with a soft tonal gradient or glow on the right face (the flat dark face already works per Top-5 nos. 1, 3, 4; a gradient would soften the ΔL* ~40–44 step that defines the fold and would be a second, different change); keep the fringe and remove the seam core instead (the core is the dominant, on-fold signal; the fringe is the subordinate, off-fold one — removing the strong signal to save the weak one inverts the hierarchy); add a matching fringe to the top face for symmetry (loop 77 removed the top-face fringe for the same competition reason; symmetry of a fault is not a fix). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — show v77 and v78 side by side at 270 px, viewed from 3 m, 2 seconds each, to 10 viewers who have not seen the card; ask 'How many faces does the cube have, and how many coloured lines are in it?'; PASS for v78 if it scores at least as well as v77 on '3 faces, 1 line' and no viewer fails to see the cube in v78; FAIL if v78 loses cube/face recognition to v77, or viewers report the cube as flatter/deader in v78. Full-size blind-read test — show v78 at full size, 5 seconds, to 10 fresh viewers; ask them to trace/describe every line inside the cube; PASS if at least 8/10 name exactly one refraction line, on the fold, and none reports a second line, bevel or extra face in the right face; FAIL if 3 or more still report a second line/bevel/extra face, or fewer than 8 name the seam as the refraction line. Measurable file checks (on the v78 file, vs v77): (a) along the former fringe centreline (707,792)–(671,890), sampled pixels equal the right-face colour (46,90,120) within ±3 per RGB channel, with no cyan or magenta chroma; (b) fringe width = 0 px — no chromatic pixels 10–30 px east of the fold centreline, outside the seam's own feather; (c) seam unchanged: centreline still #1D4ED8, core still #DBEAFE, L* ~92, at the fold midpoint ~(672,835); (d) right-face mean L* remains ~36.5 ±2 and ΔL* front-left vs right remains 40–44; (e) pixel diff v77→v78 is confined to approx. X 665–715, Y 785–895 — if any pixel outside that box changed, the build did not follow this instruction. COUNT CORRECTION BY MAIN MUSE: the worker claimed 44 new designs, but 4 of them were studied in loop 77 and are recounts, not new — Yeonwoo Lee 'Prism Crystal Suncatcher', Kasumi Design '3D Holographic Glass prism Assets', Amine Kaddari 'Glass Cubes 3D Material Render', vadimotion 'Liquid Glass Cube'. True new count is 40, running total ~2,116 (not ~2,120). One further possible recount is flagged, not counted as certain: Paul Rover 'Glass Dispersion Logo Design & 3D Animation' — the loop 77 worker treated 'Glass Dispersion' as excluded, but it does not appear in the written record (SKILL.md), so it is counted as new here with the uncertainty noted. PROCESS FIX: loop 77's full 36-title source list was never written into the banned-designs file (only its top-5 titles were in SKILL.md), which is how the 4 recounts slipped through — the banned file now holds the full loop 77 list plus loop 78's 40 new titles (~/workspace/canva-assets/banned-designs-loops61-77.txt, now covering loops 61–78). NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 79 — now that the seam core alone carries the cube, does the 2 px #DBEAFE core survive at 270 px / 3 m, or does the fold collapse to a dark tonal step with the blue line lost — i.e. test core width 2 px vs 3 px (and seam feather strength) at both distances, without touching the faces, wedge or type?

Falsification: the decisive fresh-viewer tests (270 px / 3 m blind read and the full-size 'one line or two?' test) were NOT RUN (no fresh viewers available, reported honestly to Kind), but the measurable checks pass (former fringe centreline reads (46,90,120); no chromatic pixels 10–30 px east of the fold centreline outside the seam's feather; seam core #DBEAFE L* ~92; right-face mean L* 36.5; ΔL* 44.1; file diff confined to the fringe band). v78 PNG: ~/workspace/browser_downloads/sess-1522548488/browser-download-20261107T074958.116925625Z-6-BLACK_BOX_test_card.png (373,544 bytes). NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the loop 78 study): Loop 79 — now that the seam core alone carries the cube, does the 2 px #DBEAFE core survive at 270 px / 3 m, or does the fold collapse to a dark tonal step with the blue line lost — i.e. test core width 2 px vs 3 px (and seam feather strength) at both distances, without touching the faces, wedge or type?

- Loop 79 study (35 NEW designs, running total ~2,151; none from loops 61–78 except the recount noted below): VERDICT — WIDEN THE BLUE SEAM FEATHER ONLY; do NOT widen the core. The single biggest remaining weakness: at 270 px / 3 m the fold does not fail because the core is 2 px — it fails, if it fails at all, because its only surviving carrier, the saturated blue seam, is only ~20 px wide in full size, i.e. a ~5 px blue mark at 270 px. The 2 px #DBEAFE core is already 0.5 px at 270 px and is therefore effectively invisible at thumbnail, whatever its full-size width. Widening it cannot rescue the thumbnail, and at full size it risks becoming a white stripe. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 79): widen the blue seam feather only; do NOT widen the core. Core UNCHANGED — 2 px, #DBEAFE, L* ~92, centred exactly on the existing centreline (697,767)–(647,903), length ~145 px; no change to colour, length, endpoints or position. Seam total width 20 px → 26 px, i.e. ±13 px either side of that centreline instead of ±10 px. Colour at centre unchanged: saturated #1D4ED8 at full strength adjacent to the core, falling softly to the face colour at ±13 px, with no hard edge. Use a soft linear feather so that at the old ±10 px boundary the blue is still ~30–40% opacity, not 0%. The extra 3 px each side is feather only. At 270 px this makes the blue mark 26 × 0.25 = 6.5 px instead of 5.0 px (+30%), while the core remains 0.5 px. At full size the seam is 26 px, 3.5% of the 746 px cube width — still a refraction glow, not a band. Nothing else changes: no face, wedge, type, silhouette, colour or position change. WHY: in every relevant design, what survives at small size is a broad saturated colour area or a large tonal step, never a 1–3 px pale line. Thin pale lines vanish in thumbnails and at distance, while the object still reads through its face planes. The BLACK BOX fold already has the strongest possible tonal carrier — ΔL* ~44 across the C–B fold (pale front-left ~(199,201,189), L* ~76–80, against dark right (46,90,120), L* ~36.5) — so the cube will still read as a two-face cube at 270 px even with the core lost. What is at risk at distance is not the cube shape but its glass-ness: the single blue refraction mark. The studies show that mark must be broad enough to survive as colour, not as a hairline: Prism Forge and NEON Portraits survive as broad saturated streaks/bands tens of pixels wide; the Suncatcher survives as broad pastel washes plus point sparkles; Sol Gomez Villasuso's cube survives with no highlight line at all, through large red/teal blocks. Conversely, Zosia Biela's thin pale glass edges disappear entirely at thumbnail size, and the cube still reads — proving a 2→3 px change (0.5→0.75 px at 270 px, still sub-pixel, still blended into the blue by any downsampler) cannot be the thumbnail fix. At full size, a 3 px pale line 145 px long, L* ~92 against a dark face L* ~36.5 (ΔL* ~55), would read as a deliberate white stripe, not a glint — the point-sparkle and broad-glow examples, not long thin white lines, are what read as glints. Widening the soft blue feather by 30% therefore buys the only real thumbnail gain available, at the lowest full-size risk: a soft edge cannot become a stripe. COLLISION CHECK: centreline (697,767)–(647,903) unchanged; endpoints unchanged; length ~145 px unchanged; core stays 2 px #DBEAFE on the centreline, so the seam + core remains the ONLY chromatic line in the cube, no new line, no second colour; faces unchanged in colour and L* (the extra feather is semi-transparent blue at low opacity at ±10–13 px; the hard tonal step ΔL* ~44 at the fold is preserved — the blue sits on the fold, it does not recolour either face as a whole); top face #F5F5F5 unchanged (the seam's top endpoint (697,767) is well below the top face, and widening is perpendicular to the fold, not along it, so it cannot reach the top face); silhouette untouched (the fold is interior; at its midpoint (672,835) the widened seam spans roughly X 660–684, Y 830–840, hundreds of pixels from the outer silhouette); wedge tip untouched (seam top endpoint (697,767) is ~129 px to the right of the tip (568,758); a 3 px widening each side cannot reach it; blue #1D4ED8 and red remain separated by >100 px of pale face; no merging at thumbnail, the gap is still ~32 px at 270 px); wordmark (Y 118–242), tagline (Y 262–364), microcopy (Y 40) and footer (Y 1020) all far from the seam (Y 767–903); canvas 1080×1080, cube (274,580) 746×420, the widened seam stays entirely inside the cube; one-red rule preserved. BUILD NOTE BY MAIN MUSE: the study's "Gaussian (sigma ~6.5 px)" and its "26 px ±1 px at 5% of peak" do not agree with each other — a Gaussian with sigma 6.5 px falls to 5% at ±15.9 px, i.e. ~32 px total, not 26. A LINEAR feather from the flat region to the face colour at ±13 px satisfies both of the study's measurable checks (30–40% blue at ±10 px, 5% at ~±12.6 px → ~25 px total, inside 26 ±1), so the build uses a linear feather, as in loop 75. The build repaints the whole feather zone (not a patch on top of the old feather), because the old feather already reaches the face colour at ±10 px — patching would put a hard step there. Top-5: (1) a thin pale core is not load-bearing for the fold ('Glass cube and spherical light' — Sol Gomez Villasuso, Behance — a glass cube on black/grey, its vertical fold a hard edge between a red left face and a teal sphere/right face, with no highlight line at all; the cube reads at full size and in the search thumbnail through large colour/tonal blocks alone); (2) 2→3 px cannot rescue thumbnail legibility ('Glass Cube 3d Animation' — Zosia Biela, Behance — an open glass cube on blue, its edges thin pale lines ~1–2 px; in the full project the edges are barely visible, in thumbnails they disappear entirely, yet the cube still reads as an open box through its face planes, blue ground and spheres); (3) the surviving refraction signal should be broad and saturated, with white as a point, not a stripe ('PRISM FORGE: Dynamic & Geometric Logo Design' — Moo AI, Behance — a faceted grey 'P' on black; facets read through large tonal planes, the refraction that survives is broad saturated streaks (cyan, magenta, yellow, tens of pixels wide) and a white-hot point/glow at the centre, not a long thin white line); (4) broad colour, not a hairline, carries refraction at distance ('NEON PORTRAITS with PRISMATIC GLASS' — NKXD°, Behance — prismatic light on faces survives the thumbnail as broad colour bands/washes across the face, tens of pixels wide; the thin glass rims and hairline reflections vanish at small size); (5) a glint should be a point/soft glow inside colour ('Prism Crystal Suncatcher - 2D Animation' — Yeonwoo Lee, Behance — a crystal/moon suncatcher on orange: the crystal reads through broad pastel washes and facet planes, and its glints are four-point point sparkles, not long pale lines; widening a long pale line is the wrong way to make a glint). Rejected: widen core 2 px → 3 px (at 270 px 0.75 px, still sub-pixel and blended into the blue by downsampling — no measurable thumbnail gain; at full size 50% wider, 145 px long, L* ~92 against L* ~36.5, would read as a white stripe / chalk line, breaking the 'only chromatic line' rule in spirit and competing with the 10 px silhouette); widen core to 4 px or more (same reasons, more strongly — unambiguously a stripe at full size); remove the core / reduce to 1 px (the core is the full-size glint; removing it would leave only a soft blue band with no hot centre, and Prism Forge / Suncatcher show a hot centre is what makes refraction read as light, not paint); widen the seam to 32 px or more, or make the blue hard-edged / 100% opaque to ±13 px (at full size a blue band, not a refraction glow; at 270 px, 8 px, it would start to compete with the red wedge as a second coloured bar; 26 px is the smallest widening that gives a +30% thumbnail gain); increase blue saturation/opacity without widening, or darken the right face further to raise ΔL* (saturation is already #1D4ED8 at full strength on the centreline, ΔL* ~44 is already very large; neither addresses the width threshold at 270 px, and darkening the face would change the verified face tones); move the seam, add a second seam/line, or lengthen the seam (would move the fold, create a second chromatic line, or touch the wedge tip / top face, and none is needed — the fold position is verified); change nothing (the 5 px thumbnail blue mark is at the threshold where compression and distance can wash a soft feather out, leaving only the tonal step; the studied broad-colour examples show a modest widening is the conservative insurance for that mark). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — build the 26 px version and a 20 px control, both at 270 px, shuffled, no labels; PASS if, in 5 viewers who have not seen the full-size card, at least 4/5 in each version name the object as a cube / two-faced glass cube without prompting, AND at least 4/5 in the 26 px version (vs at most 2/5 in the control) report a blue line/mark on the fold without being told it is there; FAIL if the 26 px version does not beat the control on the blue-mark question, or if any viewer calls the mark a second wedge / blue stripe; also at 270 px, measure the seam in the exported file — the blue mark must be 6–7 px wide (26 × 0.25, ±0.5 px tolerance) and the core must not be separately visible as a white pixel row. Full-size blind-read test (1080 px, normal viewing distance, then 3 m): PASS if at least 4/5 viewers call the centre 'a glint / refraction / light on the fold' and none calls it 'a white stripe / white line / chalk line'; and at least 4/5 still call the cube glass (not a flat two-tone shape); FAIL if 2 or more call it a white stripe, or if the seam is called a blue band rather than a refraction line; at 3 m, PASS if at least 4/5 still locate the fold (point to the C–B fold) within 5 seconds; FAIL if the fold is located only by the tonal step with no blue reported. Measurable file checks (on the exported 1080×1080 file): across the fold at its midpoint (672,835), perpendicular to the centreline: core exactly 2 px wide, centre pixel(s) #DBEAFE (±3 per channel), L* 90–94; blue peak #1D4ED8 within ±3 px of the centreline; total seam width, defined as where blue saturation falls below 5% of peak, 26 px ±1 px (was 20 px ±1 px); no hard step at ±10 px (opacity there 30–40%, not 0%); faces sampled 20 px either side of the fold at mid-height: left ~(199,201,189), L* 76–80; right (46,90,120), L* 35–38; ΔL* ≥42; no other pixel in the cube may be #DBEAFE except the 2 px core, and no red except #C32D30; endpoints, wordmark (Y 118–242), tagline (Y 262–364), wedge tip (568,758) and footer (Y 1020) pixel positions unchanged from v78 within ±1 px. COUNT CORRECTION BY MAIN MUSE: the worker claimed 36 new designs, but 1 is a recount — Yeonwoo Lee 'Prism Crystal Suncatcher - 2D Animation', studied in loop 77 and again in loop 78, now a third time (the banned entry 'Prism Crystal Suncatcher' did not match the suffixed title). True new count is 35, running total ~2,151 (not ~2,152). TITLE-VARIANT LESSON: the same work under a suffixed/expanded title ('- 2D Animation', 'Logo Design & 3D Animation') counts as banned too — future briefs must say so explicitly, and the banned file should carry the base title as the match key. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 80 — now that the blue seam is 26 px wide, does it at 270 px still read as one glass refraction line, or does it start to read as a blue band / second coloured bar that competes with the red wedge (tip at (568,758), ~129 px away) — and is the top face #F5F5F5 still unambiguously the brightest, flattest plane in the cube, or does the wider blue now draw the eye first at thumbnail?

- Loop 79 build notes (v79, 2026-11-07): widen the blue seam feather only, 20 → 26 px poster. CHANGE: the cube image only, replaced in place via the Replace flow (image count stayed five, layer below wedge, above halos; the file was granted in the task's `files` field). New file `glass-cube-black-v79.png` (built by main Muse from v78 with a numpy repaint of the feather zone only). The core is UNCHANGED — 2 px poster #DBEAFE on the centreline (697,767)–(647,903). The seam profile (file px, C=(1700,750), C–B unit (−0.3417,0.9398), perp (0.9398,0.3417)): flat #1D4ED8 for |d| ≤ 18 file, LINEAR feather 18 → 52.3 file to the face colour (52.3 file = 13 px poster). At |d| = 40 file (the old ±10 px boundary) the blue is ~36% (study wanted 30–40%); the 5% point is at |d| ≈ 51–52 file = 12.7–12.9 px poster each side, total seam width 25.4–25.6 px poster (study wanted 26 ± 1). The whole feather zone was repainted, not patched on top of the old feather, because the old feather already reached the face colour at ±10 px — patching would have put a hard step there. The west face colour is sampled per-s at d = −46 (just outside the old band) with guards against the white silhouette stroke (min > 245), near-black outside the cube (max < 100) and seam blue (B−R > 60); the east face is flat (46,90,120). The band is clipped to the hexagon (loop 70 lesson); s range unchanged 30…585.2. VERIFIED BY MAIN MUSE: v79 sha256 487ca475 (561,353 bytes). Cross-sections at s=150/300/450: core (219,234,254) at offset 0, flat #1D4ED8 (29,78,216) to |d|=18, then a smooth linear falloff — at |d|=40 file the west reads (151,170,220) against a true face (220,222,221) and the east reads (40,86,154) against (46,90,120), both matching the predicted 36% blend within a few points; at |d|=52 file both sides are within a few points of their face colours. The pale core still spans exactly 8 px file (2 px poster) across the centreline at s=300, with (135,165,238) anti-aliased edge blends at ±4–5. Changed 36,975 px vs v78, bbox x1452–1738, y761–1293 file — all in the feather annulus. Faces unchanged: front-left (199,201,189) central, right face (46,90,120) at all samples, top face (245,245,245); ΔL* 44.1 as before. Strong-red px inside the cube region: 155 in both v78 and v79 (unchanged). No red added. BUILD LESSON (loop 79, caught before the build shipped): the glass render's bright front-left face reads ~(220,222,221) — that is REAL face colour, not the white silhouette stroke. My first build used a guard of min > 210 to reject "near-white" samples and baked a darker (199,201,189) fallback halo into the feather's west edge (a ~24-point step against the true face). Fixed by raising the white threshold to min > 245 (the stroke is 255; the render's brightest is ~220–235) and rebuilding from v78. MEASUREMENT LESSON: a 5%-of-peak width must be measured as a blend-fraction projection onto (blue − face), NOT as a B-channel excess — on the west side the face B (~221) is already brighter than the blue B (216), so B-excess is meaningless there; and the face reference must be sampled OUTSIDE the new feather (v78 at d=−46, or v79 at d=−60) — sampling it at d=−46 in v79 reads the feather itself and under-reports the width (10.9 px instead of the true 12.7 px). GEOMETRY LESSON (study's own numbers): a Gaussian feather with sigma 6.5 px falls to 5% at ±15.9 px, i.e. ~32 px total, not 26 — the study's Gaussian and its 26 ± 1 px check disagreed, and a linear feather satisfies both of its measurable checks, so the build used linear, as in loop 75. Falsification: the decisive fresh-viewer tests (270 px / 3 m blind read, 26 px vs 20 px control, and the full-size 'glint or white stripe?' test) were NOT RUN (no fresh viewers available, reported honestly to Kind), but the measurable checks pass (seam width 25.4–25.6 px poster; ~36% blue at ±10 px; core exactly 2 px; faces unchanged; file diff confined to the feather annulus). EXPORT VERIFIED BY MAIN MUSE: v79 export sha256 0058f584 (373,787 bytes, up from v78's 373,544). CLEAN DIFF: only 2,835 px changed vs v78, bbox x633–706, y766–903 — all inside the seam band (the source-file bbox x1452–1738, y761–1293 maps to card x635–706, y769–901). No re-render noise this time. The seam core is identical to v78: Y=820 brightest X=677 (222,236,252); Y=850 brightest X=666 (217,232,252). (The worker's (196,215,248) core sample was again an off-centreline editor-zoom reading; the export file reads (217,232,252), as in v77–v78.) The right face still reads exactly (46,90,120) east of the seam at Y=800/820/850 (max dev 0); top strip still solid (max dev 1); top-face interior (245,245,245). The wedge tip still touches the vertex: vertex (568,758) (248,237,238), identical to v78; shaft red (195,45,48) byte-identical. Wordmark, tagline, top microcopy, footer and background-top bands all sum-abs diff 0 (byte-identical). At 270 px, with the correct threshold, the saturated-blue mark (B−R > 100, which isolates the seam from the dark face's B−R = 74) went from 4 px to 5 px at thumb Y=205 and 4 px to 6 px at thumb Y=212 — the study's predicted 5.0 → 6.5 px for the full 5%-width, confirmed in direction and magnitude (the soft feather falls below the saturated threshold); the core is not separately visible as a white pixel row at the seam rows (0 white px at Y=212 in both versions). THUMBNAIL-THRESHOLD LESSON: B−R > 30 catches the dark face itself (46,90,120 has B−R = 74), so it cannot measure the seam at 270 px — use B−R > 100 for saturated seam blue. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the loop 79 study): Loop 80 — now that the blue seam is 26 px wide, does it at 270 px still read as one glass refraction line, or does it start to read as a blue band / second coloured bar that competes with the red wedge (tip at (568,758), ~129 px away) — and is the top face #F5F5F5 still unambiguously the brightest, flattest plane in the cube, or does the wider blue now draw the eye first at thumbnail?

- Loop 80 study (34 NEW designs, running total ~2,185; none from loops 61–79): VERDICT — 26 px is already too wide. At both distances it reads as a blue band / second coloured bar, not as one glass refraction line, and it competes with the red wedge and with the top face. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 80): narrow the seam, and remove its pale core as part of that same seam change; change nothing else. Replace the present 26 px seam + 2 px pale core with ONE single seam, 12 px wide in total on the 1080 px poster, centred on the unchanged centreline (697,767)–(647,903), colour #1D4ED8 throughout. Construction: 8 px solid #1D4ED8 at the centre, feathering linearly to transparent over 2 px on each side (8 + 2 + 2 = 12 px total). No #DBEAFE core anywhere; no other colour, position, length or angle change. Length stays ~145 px (centreline length 144.9 px); width/length falls from 17.9% (26/145) to 8.3% (12/145). At 270 px the seam becomes 3.0 px wide × 36 px long (solid ~2 px), instead of 6.5 px wide. Chromatic seam area falls from ~3,770 px² (26 × 145) to ~1,740 px² (12 × 145), a 54% reduction. WHY: in every successful glass/prism work studied, saturated colour is either (a) a whole face/volume, with thin pale edges, or (b) a thin fringe/line at an edge — never a mid-width saturated strip stranded down the middle of a fold. The 26 px seam is neither a face nor a line: 26 px is 3.5% of the cube's 746 px width and 17.9% of its own 145 px length, so at 270 px it is 6.5 px wide against a 36 px length — the proportions of a bar, not a line. A bar of saturated #1D4ED8 is the only other saturated shape besides the red wedge, so the eye reads two coloured bars: red (wedge, arrowhead 48 px) and blue (seam), 129 px apart, instead of one red accent + one glass line. The top face #F5F5F5 is still the brightest plane by luminance (L* ~96, against blue L* ~35 and red #C32D30 L* ~35–40), but luminance does not win at thumbnail: chroma area does. Halving the blue area restores the intended hierarchy — white wordmark/top face first by luminance and area, red wedge first by chroma, blue seam subordinate. Evidence: 'Refracted' (Hup Studio) keeps the glass itself almost colourless — colour lives in the flower behind it, refraction is distortion, not a coloured stripe; 'Micro refractions' (Farid Ghanbari) uses broad, feathered, multi-hue caustic washes, never a single hard saturated bar; 'Prism light motion' (Gleb Kuznetsov) puts colour as a broad pale wash across the whole top face, with only thin pale edge lines; 'MICROSOFT • Fluid Framework' (Wes Cockx / Microsoft Design) makes blue whole faces/volumes with thin pale edges; 'Cube refraction c4d' (Artur Mineev) and '717C Glass 3D' (andbro.co) use colour only as thin rainbow fringes at glass edges, distorting what is behind the glass; 'REFRACTION CUBE 3D' (Denis Kuzminyh) is the closest black-ground analogue: its top face stays flat black, and saturated magenta/blue appear only as a thin rim along the top edge; 'Neon Circle wave line Animation on Black Background' (Easin arafat) shows saturated blue on black reading as a line only because its rings are thin strokes; on black posters, 'Do Something First' (Syed designs) and 'Fight Brutalism' (Riley @ Studio.909) likewise keep to one dominant accent/type and otherwise black/white — no second mid-width coloured bar. COLLISION CHECK: centreline (697,767)–(647,903) unchanged to ±1 px; length, angle and endpoints unchanged; only half-width changes from 13 px to 6 px each side of the centreline; pale core #DBEAFE deleted — it was invisible at 270 px and, at full size, split the seam into two parallel blue strips with a pale gap, increasing the perceived width; deleting it is part of this one seam change, not a second change; front-left face ~(199,201,189), L* ~76–80: seam now extends 6 px onto it instead of 13 px; blue-on-pale contrast remains high, so the line is still legible along its full length from that side; right face (46,90,120), L* ~36.5: seam extends 6 px onto it instead of 13 px; #1D4ED8 (29,78,216) against (46,90,120) is lower-contrast on this side (mainly the B-channel difference, 216 vs 120), so the right half is fainter — accepted: the left half carries the line at thumbnail, and the full 12 px is still measurable at full size; the tonal step ΔL* ~44 across the C–B fold is unchanged; the seam no longer masks it with a 26 px band; top face #F5F5F5 untouched, still the brightest, flattest plane; seam top end (697,767) is far below the top face (cube top ~Y 580) and does not touch it or its 10 px white silhouette stroke; no fringe is reintroduced on either face; red wedge #C32D30 unchanged — centreline (0,430)→(568,758) at 30.0°, tip still touching the cube's upper-left vertex (568,758), arrowhead still 48 × 36 px; gap from wedge tip to seam top end stays ~129 px (dx 129, dy 9), but that gap is now 10.8× the seam width (129/12) instead of 5.0× (129/26), so the two no longer read as a matched pair of bars; at 270 px the gap is ~32 px, the wedge arrowhead ~12 px wide, the seam ~3 px — wedge dominant, seam subordinate; wordmark (ink X 16–1077, Y 118–242), tagline (box X 17, Y 262, W 972.6, H 102; ink Y 272–352), microcopy 'N° 01' / 'EST. 2026' (Y 40), footer '@BLACKBOXSYSTEMSLAB' / 'VOL. 01' (Y 1020) all untouched and clear of the seam (seam Y 767–903; footer is 117 px below the seam's lower end, Y 903→1020); canvas geometry: the whole 12 px seam lies inside the cube image (X 274–1020, Y 580–1000) and inside the 1080 × 1080 canvas; the fold is interior, so a 6 px half-width cannot cross any cube edge or the canvas edge; no element moves, so no new overlap is created. BUILD NOTE BY MAIN MUSE: the build must RESTORE the face in the annulus the old feather occupied (24.1 < |d| ≤ 52.3 file) — simply drawing the narrower seam on top would leave the old feather's outer part still blue and the seam would stay 26 px wide. East side restores to flat (46,90,120); west side restores to the face colour sampled per-s at d=−56 (pure face in v79, since both feathers ended at ±52.3) — the render is smooth across d there, so the sampled colour is a good approximation of the hidden face, verified after building by checking for a step at |d|=40 and |d|=52.3. Top-5: (1) even saturated colour on a black cube must be a rim/line, not a bar down a fold, and the flat top plane stays uncontested ('REFRACTION CUBE 3D Concept of a Multimedia Art Object' — Denis Kuzminyh, Behance — on a black ground its cube's top face stays flat black while saturated magenta/blue appear only as a thin rim along the top edge); (2) refraction reads as a face wash plus a thin edge — a hard saturated stripe down a fold is not how this language works ('Prism light motion' — Gleb Kuznetsov, Dribbble — the cube's top face is a broad pale iridescent wash (palette includes #F5F5F3, close to the card's #F5F5F5); its edges are thin pale lines); (3) a blue of this hue works as a fringe of a few pixels; made solid and 26 px wide it becomes a separate object ('Cube refraction c4d' — Artur Mineev, Dribbble — black ground, white type; the cube's colour is thin rainbow fringes along its edges, including a blue fringe close to #084DB2); (4) the binary: colour is either a face (large, graded) or a line (thin); a 26 px strip in the middle of a fold is neither, so it reads as a foreign bar ('MICROSOFT • Fluid Framework' — Wes Cockx / Microsoft Design, Behance — blue is whole faces/volumes, graded across large planes, with thin pale edges); (5) the brightest, flattest plane should carry the read; a wide saturated line inside the glass steals that read ('717C Glass 3D' — andbro.co, Dribbble — the glass is near-white/clear, the white '717C' lettering is distorted by refraction, and colour appears only as thin fringes at the glass edges). Rejected: keep 26 px / widen further (width/length 17.9% is a bar at both distances; widening only increases chromatic area and the wedge competition); return to 20 px (loop 78 width) (20/145 = 13.8%, 5 px at 270 px — still bar-like, and it is a revert, not a tested fix); narrow to 6–8 px total (at 270 px that is 1.5–2 px, at risk of aliasing out against the dark right face (46,90,120) and of disappearing at 3 m; 12 px (3 px at thumbnail, 2 px solid) is the smallest width that stays reliably visible); desaturate/lighten the blue (e.g. to a pale #93C5FD) (it would vanish against the pale front-left face (199,201,189), lose the refraction identity, and introduce a third blue tint; the problem is width, not hue); shorten the seam to the upper half of the fold only (a shorter 26 px bar has a worse width/length ratio, reads even more as a bar, and looks unfinished/broken at full size); move the seam off the C–B fold (the fold is where refraction physically occurs; moving it breaks the glass logic); brighten the top face to pure #FFFFFF or enlarge it (#F5F5F5 is already L* ~96; the gain is imperceptible and does not fix the band ratio — the seam would still compete); thin the wedge or move its tip away from (568,758) (the wedge is the single intended chromatic accent; the competition is caused by the seam's width, and moving the tip would break the verified tip-to-vertex touch). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — export the card at 270 × 270 px, show it for 3 seconds at 3 m to 5 viewers who have not seen v79/v80, ask 'What do you see first? What is the blue mark? What is the brightest/flattest part?'; PASS if ≥4/5 say 'one glass cube' and describe the blue as 'a (thin) line / edge / seam / refraction' (not 'a bar / stripe / band / second bar'), name the white top face (or the white wordmark) as the brightest/flattest thing before the blue, and do not name the blue before the red wedge; FAIL if ≥2/5 say 'blue bar / stripe / band' or name the blue first. Full-size blind-read test: same 5 viewers, full 1080 px at normal viewing distance, no time limit, ask the same questions plus 'Where is the blue, and what is it doing?'; PASS if ≥4/5 call it 'a line / seam on the fold' and locate it on the C–B fold (697,767)–(647,903), and say the top face is the brightest, flattest plane; FAIL if ≥2/5 call it 'a band / stripe / bar' or say it competes with / is a second wedge. Measurable file checks (on the built v80 file, before any blind test): (a) on a cross-section perpendicular to the centreline at its midpoint (672,835), saturated #1D4ED8 pixels span 12 px total ±2 px (10–14 px including feather); (b) zero pixels of #DBEAFE in the seam; (c) centreline endpoints (697,767) and (647,903) within ±1 px of v79; (d) top-face centre sample still #F5F5F5; (e) wedge tip still (568,758) ±1 px and wedge colour still only #C32D30; (f) at a 270 px export, the seam measures 3 px ±1 px wide. Any of (a)–(f) failing means the change was not built as specified. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 81 — with the seam narrowed to 12 px, does the red wedge tip still need to touch the cube's upper-left vertex (568,758), or does the tip now read as pointing at / colliding with the cube at 270 px — i.e. should the tip be pulled back a few pixels along its 30.0° centreline, or is the touch now clean because the seam no longer competes with it?

- Loop 81 study (35 NEW designs, running total ~2,220; none from loops 61–80): VERDICT — pull the wedge tip back 10.0 px along its 30.0° centreline. Weakness: the red wedge tip kisses the cube's upper-left vertex (568,758) exactly. At that point three things meet — white top face #F5F5F5, pale front-left face ~(199,201,189), and the black field — and the 48×36 px red arrowhead collapses onto them. At 270 px / 3 m that is a 3–4 px red/pale/white muddle, not a readable strike; at full size it reads as a collision / rendering smudge on the cube's cleanest corner, not as pointing. Narrowing the seam to 12 px in loop 80 did not fix this: it removed a competitor inside the cube, but the tip–vertex kiss is a separate junction fault, on the outside of the cube. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 81): pull the wedge tip back 10.0 px along its 30.0° centreline, shortening the wedge from the tip end only. Old tip (568,758); new tip (559.3,753.0) — shift vector (−8.7,−5.0) px, i.e. 10.0 × (cos 30°, sin 30°) = 10.0 × (0.866, 0.500). Gap created: exactly 10.0 px of pure black #000000, measured along the centreline, between the new red tip and the vertex (568,758). Everything else frozen: centreline origin (0,430) and angle 30.0° unchanged; new centreline length (0,430)→(559.3,753.0) = 645.9 px (was 655.9 px); arrowhead unchanged at 48 px wide × 36 px long, now with its base centre at (528.1,735.0); wedge colour still the sole red #C32D30; seam, faces, silhouette, wordmark, tagline, microcopy and footer unchanged. Do NOT translate the whole wedge PNG: that would move the left-edge entry from (0,430) to (0,425). Shorten/trim the tip end only, keeping the entry, angle and arrowhead geometry. Why 10 px, not 6 or 12: at 270 px (×0.25) 10 px = 2.5 px of black — the smallest gap that survives downscaling as a clean sliver; 6 px = 1.5 px at thumbnail, below the ~2 px survival threshold, and would re-collapse into a kiss. At full size 10 px = 0.93% of the 1080 width, 1.34% of the 746 px cube width, 27.8% of the 36 px arrowhead length — the same order as the existing 10 px white silhouette stroke, so it reads as a deliberate system measure, not an error. 12 px (= the seam width) would echo the seam and is halfway back to the 24 px gap rejected in loops 61–66. WHY: in every relevant full project opened, an arrow/line either stops clearly short in empty field, or crosses decisively through an object — none parks its tip exactly on a vertex or edge. Arrow Posters and Electric Boogie show red arrows carrying their meaning in clear space, with no contact at all. Stray Arrow and PAARD PUSH FORWARD show tips kept in white/black void beyond the object they point at. RED GIANT shows thin lines that either run clean through circles or stop in open cream — never a tangent kiss. MIRROR HOUSE shows a glass cuboid whose vertices read precisely because nothing touches them. Mirror | Eyes shows prismatic bands legible because they cross open skin and never coincide with a feature corner. The seam change supports the pull-back, not keep-as-is: with the seam now a single clean 12 px blue line (3 px saturated blue at 270 px), the cube interior is quiet, so the only remaining noise at the junction is the red tip itself — removing it from the vertex lets both the seam and the vertex be read cleanly. The centreline still passes exactly through (568,758), so the eye completes the last 10 px; pointing is preserved, impact is replaced by aim. COLLISION CHECK: wedge tip new (559.3,753.0), red #C32D30, 10.0 px from vertex along 30.0°; no red pixel touches the cube; vertex (568,758) clean and untouched; top face, front-left face and silhouette meet there with no red overpaint; arrowhead (48×36) wholly in black void; nearest point is the tip, 10 px from the vertex; its side corners are ~52 px from the vertex; its base centre (528.1,735.0) is ~46 px from the vertex along the centreline; no part overlaps the cube silhouette; seam unchanged, 12 px, centreline (697,767)–(647,903); nearest seam point to the new tip is (697,767), 138.4 px away — no interaction, no second red/blue junction; faces untouched (front-left pale ~(199,201,189), L* ~76–80, right face (46,90,120), L* ~36.5; tonal step ΔL* ~44 across the C–B fold preserved); silhouette 10 px white stroke on the top face untouched; no red crosses it; wordmark (Y 118–242), tagline (Y 262–352, box X 17), microcopy (Y 40), footer (Y 1020) all ≥200 px from the tip area; unaffected; canvas geometry: tip (559.3,753.0) is inside the 1080×1080 canvas, 520.7 px from the right edge, 327.0 px from the bottom; gap 10 px = 2.5 px at 270 px; arrowhead at thumbnail = 12.0×9.0 px, still a readable arrowhead; the wedge still enters at (0,430) on the left edge; colour discipline: still one red only, #C32D30 (wedge, glows, halos); no new colour, line, star, spine or hairline introduced. BUILD NOTE BY MAIN MUSE: the wedge is a separate full-bleed PNG (`slash-wedge-point-746-gap0-head48-steep30.png`), not the cube — the build edits that file surgically (erase the old arrowhead + tip end, redraw the arrowhead translated by (−8.66,−5.0) and a short shaft bridge at the measured local width), never a whole-PNG translate and never a from-scratch rebuild, so the shaft outside the tip region stays byte-identical. Top-5: (1) a red arrow reads as pointing — more cleanly — when it stops short in empty space ('Electric boogie | Identity' — Anna Cheboksarova, Behance — on the black posters the red diagonal loop's arrowhead is a small open V floating in black void, and the small red arrows sit in the gaps between letters, never touching a letterform); (2) direction is carried by shaft, head and chevrons, not by contact with a target ('Arrow Posters' — Roshan Sam, Behance — the thin red arrow under "ARROW" sits in a clear beige field, its tip ending well short of any letter or edge, with three red chevrons at the tail); (3) even a "hit" arrow keeps its tip legible by ending in empty field, not on an edge ('Stray Arrow' — Yu Osawa, Behance — the horizontal arrow pierces the samurai head, but its tip is kept clear in white space beyond the red disc behind it); (4) arrows used as direction, not as spears striking a target ('PAARD PUSH FORWARD' — Studio Lennarts & De Bruijn, Behance — large yellow arrowheads stop inside their own colour blocks and never pierce the next block, circle or type); (5) the ambiguous middle — a 0 px kiss on an edge — is the one relationship competent minimal posters avoid; clear separation or decisive overlap are the only clean options, and separation is the one that preserves BLACK BOX's clean vertex ('RED GIANT - Minimalist poster design' — Vlad Grama, Dribbble — thin vertical lines either run cleanly through the red/orange circles or stop in open cream; none parks tangentially on a circle's edge). Rejected: keep as-is (tip touching 568,758) (the seam narrowing removed an internal competitor, not the external kiss; at 270 px the tip + vertex + arrowhead still collapse into a red/pale/white blob, and at full size red over a three-way corner reads as collision, not strike); pull back 6 px, new tip (562.8,755.0) (1.5 px at 270 px, below the ~2 px survival threshold — anti-aliasing would re-merge tip and vertex; also smaller than the 10 px silhouette stroke, so it would look accidental); pull back 12 px, new tip (557.6,752.0) (works at thumbnail (3 px) but at full size equals the seam width, creating a second 12 px measure, and is halfway back to the rejected 24 px gap — it starts to read as a miss); pull back 24 px (loops 61–66 gap), new tip (547.2,746.0) (already tried and rejected; at 6 px at thumbnail the arrow visibly misses the cube and the strike/aim is lost); push the tip INTO the cube (overlap by 10–20 px) (red would overpaint the pale face and white silhouette, break the clean vertex, and at thumbnail merge with the blue seam area; it turns pointing into impaling); remove or shrink the arrowhead (a second change, and the arrowhead is the direction cue that survives at 270 px (12×9 px); removing it would weaken the read the gap is meant to protect); rotate the wedge to aim at the cube centre or another vertex (changes the 30.0° centreline and the (0,430) entry — a different design, not a junction fix); widen the seam again to compete with / distract from the tip (reverses loop 80 and reintroduces the problem the seam narrowing solved). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — show the v81 export at 270 px wide (or printed at 270 px, viewed from 3 m) to ≥5 viewers who have not seen the brief, with no prompt beyond "what do you see?"; PASS if ≥4/5 say the red arrow points at the cube's corner and there is a small black gap, and ≤1 says "touching / colliding / muddle"; FAIL if ≥2 say touching, colliding, or cannot separate the tip from the cube, or if ≥2 say the arrow missed the cube. Full-size blind-read test: same viewers at full size (1080 px); PASS if ≥4/5 say the arrow "points at, but does not touch, the corner" and the gap "looks intentional"; FAIL if any say "it missed / fell short" or "it is touching / crashing into the cube". Measurable file checks (v81 export, before any other change): (a) tip pixel at (559,753) is red #C32D30; (b) 3×3 sample centred at the gap midpoint (563.6,755.5) is pure black #000000 in all 9 pixels; (c) pixel at the vertex (568,758) is NOT #C32D30; (d) tip-to-vertex distance = 10.0 ± 0.5 px, centreline angle = 30.0° ± 0.5°, entry still (0,430); (e) arrowhead still 48×36 px, seam still 12 px total with 3 px saturated blue at 270 px, wedge colour still #C32D30 only; (f) at 270 px the black gap between red tip and pale face measures 2.5 ± 0.5 px; (g) no pixel outside the wedge tip region differs from v80. Any (a)–(f) failure falsifies the build; (g) failure means a second change crept in. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 82 — now that the tip stands 10 px short of the vertex, does the 48×36 px arrowhead still read as an arrowhead at 270 px (12×9 px), or does it collapse into an undifferentiated red blob — i.e. should the arrowhead be enlarged, sharpened, or left exactly as it is?

Seam identical (saturated-blue run 11 px at Y=820 and Y=850, centre (29,78,216)); right face dev 0; top strip dev 1; wordmark/tagline/microcopy/footer/background zones all sum-abs diff 0. At 270 px the centreline reads: v80 red → pink (229,150,151) → white (no gap); v81 red → dark red (65,27,28) → near-black (11,13,43) → pale (206,216,233) → white (253,254,254) — a ~2 px dark separation between solid red and the white vertex, close to the study's 2.5 ± 0.5 px given thumbnail resampling. Arrowhead: the worker measured its profile tracking the ideal 48×36 triangle within 1–2 px (strict-red widths 6, 15, 23, 31, 38 px at 6, 12, 18, 24, 30 px behind the tip vs ideal 8, 16, 24, 32, 40 px; base 41 px loose-red) — the full 48 px was not measured as flat solid red because the base edges are anti-aliased blends, the same as the old wedge (which measured 46.6 by the same method). Thumbnail: ~/workspace/canva-assets/v81-thumb-270.png. v81 PNG: ~/workspace/browser_downloads/sess-3516657959/browser-download-20261107T083633.502053862Z-0-BLACK_BOX_test_card.png (378,145 bytes). NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the loop 81 study): Loop 82 — now that the tip stands 10 px short of the vertex, does the 48×36 px arrowhead still read as an arrowhead at 270 px (12×9 px), or does it collapse into an undifferentiated red blob — i.e. should the arrowhead be enlarged, sharpened, or left exactly as it is?

- Loop 82 study (29 NEW designs, running total ~2,249; none from loops 61–81): VERDICT — enlarge and slightly sharpen the arrowhead, nothing else. Weakness: at 270 px the arrowhead collapses. It is 48 × 36 px full size, i.e. 12 × 9 px at thumbnail, with an area of only 54 px². The shaft beside it is ~24 px wide near the head, i.e. 6 px at thumbnail, so the head flares only (12 − 6) / 2 = 3 px per side beyond the shaft. Three pixels per side, on a 30° diagonal, is not enough for two sloping edges and a point to survive anti-aliasing: it reads as a slight thickening / red blob at the end of the wedge, not as a triangle. At full size the same head is also timid: 48 px is only 6.4% of the 746 px cube, and only 2.0× the shaft width at its base, with a blunt apex of 67.4°. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 82): replace the 48 × 36 px triangle with a 72 × 60 px triangle, in the same single red #C32D30, with the tip, centreline, entry and angle frozen. Tip (unchanged): (559.3,753.0). Centreline (unchanged): (0,430) → (559.3,753.0), 30.0°; unit vector (0.8660, 0.5000). New size: 72 px wide (across the centreline) × 60 px long (along the centreline). New base centre: (507.3,723.0) — tip minus 60 × (0.8660, 0.5000). New base corners: (489.3,754.2) and (525.3,691.8) — base centre ± 36 × (−0.5000, 0.8660). New apex angle: 61.9° (2 × arctan(36/60)), sharpened from 67.4°. New area: 2,160 px² (0.5 × 72 × 60), 2.5× the old 864 px²; hypotenuse 69.9 px (was 43.3 px). At 270 px: 18.0 × 15.0 px, area 135 px², flare (18 − 6) / 2 = 6.0 px per side, hypotenuse 17.5 px. Head / shaft ratio at the base: 72 / 24 = 3.0× (was 2.0×). Build: redraw only the triangular head polygon with the three vertices above; extend the existing tapered shaft along the centreline to meet the new base at (507.3,723.0) at its existing ~24 px width. Do not move the tip, do not change the shaft taper, colour, glow, or any other element. WHY: the successful arrowheads in the study all earn their reading by one of two means — a wide flare, or a long, sharp point — and the current head has neither enough of either. Neil Hubert's "Red Arrow" (Dribbble) is a long, sharp triangle whose length makes it unmistakable even in the small search thumbnail; its sloping edges are long enough to survive downscaling. Shahnur Islam Rimon's "Letter Y arrow logo design" (Behance) uses diagonal blue bars that flare to a head about 3× the shaft, readable even in the tiny YORA mark and across the banner at distance. Graphéine's "Be API" pointer survives at small size because its tip is acute and its taper is long, not because it is wide. By contrast, Stephen Pearson's "Red Arrows Formations" (Dribbble) shows nine red jets each about 15 px in the thumbnail: as a diamond group they read, but each jet alone would be a red blob — exactly the fate of a lone 12 px head. Kibong Yi's "Arrow" shows the same failure in miniature: a small gold head on a thin shaft is almost lost in the search thumbnail. The 72 × 60 px proposal takes both successful routes at once, in moderation: width 1.5× (flare 3 px → 6 px per side at thumbnail) and length 1.67× (apex 67.4° → 61.9°, hypotenuse 10.8 px → 17.5 px at thumbnail), while staying subordinate to the cube at full size (9.7% of cube width, 9.3% of wedge length). COLLISION CHECK: arrowhead itself — only the head polygon changes; vertices as above, all inside the 1080 × 1080 canvas (X 489.3–559.3, Y 691.8–754.2); wedge tip unchanged at (559.3,753.0); vertex (568,758) and 10 px gap unchanged — tip-to-vertex distance remains 10.0 px along the 30° centreline; the black gap between tip and vertex is untouched; both new base corners are 78.8 px from the vertex, and the new base centre is 70.1 px from it, so no red reaches the vertex and its clean white (254,254,254) is preserved; seam — blue seam centreline (697,767)–(647,903), 12 px wide, is south-east of the head (head X ≤ 559.3, Y ≤ 754.2); no overlap, no colour mixing, and the seam remains the only chromatic line in the cube; cube faces and silhouette — the head sits south-west of the cube's upper-left vertex, in black void; its corners are ~79 px from the nearest cube ink (the vertex); it does not touch the pale front-left face, the dark right face, the #F5F5F5 top face, or the 10 px white silhouette stroke; bounding-box overlap with the cube image (274,580)–(1020,1000) is not shape overlap — the cube shape itself is north-east of the vertex; wordmark (Y 118–242), tagline (Y 262–352), microcopy (Y 40), footer (Y 1020) — head Y 691.8–754.2 is far from all of them; no text is covered or crowded; shaft — the shaft is extended 24 px further along the centreline (old base 528.1,735.0 → new base 507.3,723.0) at its existing width; the taper 16 px → ~24 px is unchanged, and the head now overhangs it by 24 px per side at the base, which is the intended flare; canvas geometry — no edge clipping; leftmost point 489.3 px, topmost 691.8 px; colour discipline preserved: one red only, #C32D30; no outline, halo, notch or second colour is added. BUILD NOTE BY MAIN MUSE: same surgical-edit method as loop 81, on the v81 wedge file — erase the old head + the shaft segment the new head covers (s ∈ [583,650], |perp| ≤ 40), redraw the shaft bridge (s 580 → 585.9) at the measured local shaft half-width and the new 72×60 head triangle at 4× supersampling; the shaft outside the head region stays byte-identical. Top-5: (1) lengthening and sharpening the head, not just widening it, is what survives downscaling ("Red Arrow" — Neil Hubert, Dribbble — a long, sharp red triangle: head length is large relative to shaft thickness, apex about 45°, sloping edges long and straight; it reads as an arrow even in the small Dribbble search thumbnail); (2) a ~3.0× head-to-shaft flare is the threshold for a diagonal arrow to read at distance / small size — the exact ratio adopted (72 / 24) ("Letter Y arrow logo design" — Shahnur Islam Rimon, Behance — thick blue diagonal bars flare to a wide pointed head, about 3× the shaft, and the same mark reads in the tiny YORA logo and across a large banner); (3) a lone ~12 px pointed shape collapses, and grouping cannot rescue the BLACK BOX head because it is a single head ("Red Arrows Formations" — Stephen Pearson, Dribbble — nine red jets in a diamond, each ~15 px in the thumbnail, read as a pattern, not as individual arrows; alone, each would be a blob); (4) narrowing the apex (67.4° → 61.9°) and lengthening the triangle helps the point survive anti-aliasing, independently of width ("Be API - Brand Design" — Graphéine, Behance — a white pointer on orange whose tip is acute with a long taper; the point survives at small size because it is long and isolated); (5) an oversized head is not inherently wrong on a poster — but it also sets the limit: the BLACK BOX head must stay subordinate to the 746 px cube, which is why 72 px (9.7% of cube width) was chosen over 96 px ("Red Arrow - Graphic Design" — Maria Lintsbakh, Behance — a huge red arrow/star head dominates the poster behind the portrait). Rejected: keep exactly as-is (48 × 36 px) (flare only 3 px per side at 270 px, area 54 px², apex 67.4° — the thumbnail evidence (Pearson; Kibong Yi) shows this collapses into a blob, and at full size it is timid against the cube); sharpen only (48 × 60 px, apex ~43.6°) (thumbnail is 12 × 15 px, flare still only 3 px per side, so it would read as a needle/spike, not a triangle; at full size it would look thin and spiky against the 746 px cube); pure 1.5× scale (72 × 54 px, apex 67.4° unchanged) (it passes the flare test (18 × 13.5 px at thumbnail), but the apex stays blunt and the sloping edges stay shorter (13.5 px at thumbnail) than in the chosen 72 × 60 px version; the extra 6 px of length costs nothing in collisions); double to 96 × 72 px (2×) (thumbnail 24 × 18 px and flare 9 px per side would read, but full size it is 12.9% of cube width and 3,456 px² (4× the old head) — a spearhead that competes with the cube and crowds the 10 px gap visually); move the tip closer to / onto the vertex (gap 0–5 px) (it would help proximity reading, but it destroys the loop-81 achievement — the clean white vertex and the 10 px black gap — and risks red-on-white contamination; tip, centreline and angle are frozen); add a white outline, halo, or a notch separating head from shaft (adds a second graphic device / colour discipline break to a poster whose rule is one red only and no extra lines; the problem is size and angle, not separation); widen the shaft instead (e.g. to ~32 px near the head) (it reduces the flare ratio, makes the whole wedge heavy, and does not make the head itself triangular at thumbnail). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — export the revised card at 270 px, display it at 3 m (or at equivalent angular size), show it for 3 seconds to 5 people who have not seen the brief; Pass if at least 4 of 5 say, unprompted, "arrow" or "triangle pointing to the cube / to the top-right", and can indicate the direction (north-east, 30°); Fail if 2 or more say "blob", "dot", "red smudge", or cannot tell the direction. Full-size blind-read test: show the 1080 px card at full size to the same 5 people; Pass if at least 4 of 5 say the head is a clear arrowhead and neither "too small / timid" nor "too big / dominating the cube", and at least 4 of 5 say it is pointing at the cube without touching it; Fail if 2 or more call it timid or oversized, or think it touches the cube. Measurable file checks (all must pass): head polygon vertices exactly (559.3,753.0), (489.3,754.2), (525.3,691.8); base centre (507.3,723.0); width 72.0 ± 0.5 px, length 60.0 ± 0.5 px, apex 61.9° ± 1°; tip-to-vertex distance 10.0 ± 0.5 px along 30.0°; fill #C32D30 only, with no new red pixels outside the new polygon; shaft width at the new base ≤ 24.5 px; the pixels between tip and vertex are pure black (0,0,0); the vertex pixel remains (254,254,254); at 270 px the head bounding box (18 × 15 px) contains at least 110 red-dominant pixels and its tip pixel is red. If the blind-read tests fail, the next step is to revisit the head size (see loop 83), not to move the tip or close the gap. (CHECK NOTE BY MAIN MUSE: "the pixels between tip and vertex are pure black" holds in the wedge PNG (transparent there) but NOT in the export — loop 81 established that the export's gap pixels read dark blue-grey blends because the gap midpoint sits on the white silhouette stroke's anti-aliased corner; judge the export by the dark red → dark → white centreline read, not by a literal (0,0,0).) NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 83 — now that the head is 72 px wide (3.0× the ~24 px shaft at its base), does the shaft taper (16 px at the left edge → ~24 px near the head) look too thin and weak behind the wider head — should the shaft near the head be widened to ~28–30 px to support the head, or does the sharp 3:1 stepped flare at the base junction need to stay exactly as it is to keep the head distinct?
