- Loop 83 study (24 NEW designs, running total ~2,273; none from loops 61–82): VERDICT — widen the shaft near the head to 32 px at the head base — a two-stage taper, nothing else changed. Weakness: the shaft-to-head junction: at 3.0:1 (24 px shaft against a 72 px solid head) the shaft is a weak thread behind a big head, with no precedent in the study. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 83): widen the shaft near the head to 32 px at the head base. Build specification (all widths measured perpendicular to the centreline, centreline (0,430)→(559.3,753.0) at 30.0°, direction unit (0.866, 0.500), perpendicular unit (−0.500, 0.866)): Stage 1: 16 px at s = 0, centre (0,430), edges (4.0,423.1) and (−4.0,436.9, clipped at the left edge as now) → 24 px at s = 380, centre (329.0,620.0), edges (323.0,630.4) and (335.0,609.6). Stage 2 (the flare): 24 px at s = 380 → 32 px at s = 586, the head base, centre (507.3,723.0), edges (499.3,736.9) and (515.3,709.1). Head, tip, entry, angle and gap are FROZEN: head 72 × 60 px, apex 61.9°, base centre (507.3,723.0), base corners (489.3,754.2) and (525.3,691.8), tip/apex (559.3,753.0), entry (0,430), angle 30.0°, 10 px tip-to-vertex gap, colour #C32D30 only. Result: ratio 72/32 = 2.25:1 (was 3.0:1); head overhang falls from 24 px to 20 px per side, so the stepped flare and the head's distinctness survive; shaft at the base is +8 px (+33%). At 270 px the shaft near the head goes from 6 px to 8 px against an 18 px head — a 2 px gain at thumbnail, the minimum reliably perceptible step. The kink at (329.0,620.0) is a slope change of only ~0.5° per side (0.60° → 1.11°), so it reads as a smooth accelerating taper, not a bulge or a second step. No other element moves. WHY: across 24 new designs, not one solid filled head sits on a 3:1 shaft. Solid-head arrows cluster at shaft:head 1.3–2.0 (WeRoad 1.4–2.0; Ivanovsky ~1.5–2; EarthWurx ~1.3; Abstract X ~1.5; OppositeDay 1:1). The only thin shafts that work are open chevrons or line arrows whose head is the same stroke as the shaft (La Corriols del Vallès) — a logic that cannot transfer to BLACK BOX, whose head is a solid 72 × 60 px triangle (2,160 px²). At 270 px / 3 m the current shaft is 4 px at the entry and 6 px at the head against an 18 px head: the head reads as a floating red triangle and the shaft as a hairline, so the arrow reading rests on the head alone. At full size the same 24 px step (24 px of overhang per side) makes the junction look top-heavy and detached. Widening only the last 206 px of shaft (s = 380→586) fixes the junction where it fails, preserves the 16 px entry and the wedge's taper story, and keeps a clear 20 px step so the head still pops. 32 px — not the posed 28–30 px — is chosen because 28–30 px is only +1–1.5 px at thumbnail, below reliable perceptibility, and still 2.4–2.57:1. COLLISION CHECK: shaft — only the last 206 px changes, +4 px per side at most; entry, Stage 1 and the left-edge clip are unchanged; arrowhead untouched — size, apex, base corners and tip identical; wedge tip / 10 px gap untouched — the widening is perpendicular to the centreline, ≥60 px along the centreline from the vertex, and the nearest new shaft edge (499.3,736.9) is ~72 px from the vertex (568,758), so the black gap and the clean white vertex (254,254,254) are preserved by construction; cube / seam / faces / silhouette — no red pixel moves toward the cube; the head and tip are unchanged, so the seam (12 px, #1D4ED8), the faces (L* ~76–80 / ~36.5) and the 10 px silhouette stroke are unaffected; wordmark (Y 118–242) and tagline (ends Y 352) — the shaft's top edge at the entry is Y 423, 71 px clear — unaffected; footer (Y 1020) far clear; microcopy and the single red #C32D30 unchanged; the added red area is ~824 px² (average +4 px × 206 px), ~7% of shaft area, no new glow or halo; canvas geometry — the whole change sits inside the 1080 × 1080 canvas, on the existing centreline, with no element crossing another. CORRECTION BY MAIN MUSE (caught before building): the study's Stage 1 endpoint is WRONG — the current shaft is ~21 px wide at s = 380, not 24 px (measured taper: 17.0 px at s=100, 19.0 at s=300, 21.0 at s=500, 23.5 at s=580 — the shaft only reaches ~24 px near the head base at s≈586). The study's spec ("24 px at s = 380") contradicts its own collision check ("Stage 1 unchanged", "only the last 206 px changes") and its WHY ("widening only the last 206 px... preserves the wedge's taper story"). The build therefore keeps Stage 1 (s = 0→380) EXACTLY as-is and flares Stage 2 (s = 380→586) from the MEASURED width at s = 380 (~21 px) to 32 px at the head base. Consequences: the real kink slope change is ~1.15° per side (old ~0.38° → new ~1.53°), not the study's claimed ~0.5° — still a gentle acceleration, not a bulge; the added area is ~855 px² (not ~824 px²); and the study's falsification check "shaft width 24 ± 1 px at (329.0,620.0)" must read the UNCHANGED shaft (~21 px), not 24 — only the 32 px check at the base (507.3,723.0) stands as written. BUILD NOTE BY MAIN MUSE: no erase is needed — the new Stage 2 quad (s = 380→586, width measured-at-380 → 32 px) CONTAINS the old shaft in that range (the new width exceeds the old width at every s), so the quad is drawn over the old shaft (red on red) at 4× supersampling; the head is untouched and the shaft outside s = 380→586 stays byte-identical. Top-5: (1) no solid head exceeds 2:1 — the direct precedent for 32 px (2.25:1) as the outer limit ("WeRoad Reunion Weekend / Festival Identity" — Uoi Studio, Lorenzo Mercanti, Elisabetta Vedovato, Behance — solid blue arrows on red; the large right arrow is ~1.4:1 and the small bottom arrow ~2:1 shaft:head; even in a loud poster system, no solid head exceeds 2:1); (2) a solid red head on black needs a shaft of comparable mass to carry it ("Arrows, Logo design, Visual identity" — Alexander Ivanovsky, Behance — a red folded-ribbon arrow on black, the closest analogue to BLACK BOX; shaft flows into the head at roughly half the head width with no step); (3) the counter-example that proves the rule — thin shaft + solid head has no precedent here ("La Corriols del Vallès" — Gerard Arderius, Behance — its red arrows have shafts far thinner than their heads, but the heads are open chevrons drawn in the same stroke as the shaft — there is no solid mass to carry); (4) at small size a thick shaft is still a band, not a line — the thumbnail legibility BLACK BOX currently lacks ("EarthWurx" — Deni Harioko, Dribbble — a white diagonal arrow whose shaft is a thick rectangular bar and whose head flares only ~1.3× the shaft); (5) at small scale legibility comes from shaft mass, not from the head alone ("#OppositeDay" — Oliver Hoffmann, Dribbble — tessellated red/white block arrows at 1:1 shaft:head; they survive precisely because shaft and head are one mass). Rejected: keep as-is (3:1 step) (no solid-head precedent at 3:1 in 24 designs; at thumbnail the shaft is 4–6 px vs an 18 px head and the head floats); widen to 28–30 px (the posed range) (+1–1.5 px at 270 px is sub-threshold, and 2.4–2.57:1 is still outside the evidence band; it would spend a loop on an unverifiable change); widen to 36 px at the base (2.0:1, the evidence centre) (overhang drops to 18 px per side, the step starts to blur, and a +50% jump at the junction risks a bulge/second-head reading at full size; 32 px is the minimum effective step); widen the whole shaft (e.g. 24→32 px from the entry) (doubles the entry mass, kills the wedge taper identity and the entry delicacy, and makes the shaft a bar competing with the cube); narrow the head to 48–54 px instead (reopens loop 82 (head enlarged and sharpened for a reason) and changes tip/vertex geometry; the shaft, not the head, is the weak element); blend shaft into head with a fillet/continuous curve (no step) (suits Ivanovsky's ribbon, not BLACK BOX's sharp 61.9° triangle; it would soften the base corners and read as a different arrow); add an outline, glow or second red element to support the head (adds a new element and breaks the one-red, minimal system). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — export at 270 px, view at 3 m (or equivalent), 3+ viewers who have not seen v82; PASS if every viewer names one diagonal red arrow, traces the shaft continuously from the left edge to the head, and does not say "floating triangle", "thread" or "hairline"; FAIL if any viewer cannot trace the shaft to the head or describes the head as detached. Full-size blind-read test: at 1080 px, viewers describe the shaft as carrying the head, the head as still distinct (step visible), and report no bulge at (329,620) and no second head; FAIL if the junction is called "bulging", "lumpy" or "blunt". Measurable file checks (built 1080 × 1080 PNG): shaft width perpendicular to the centreline 16 ± 1 px at s = 0, 24 ± 1 px at (329.0,620.0), 32 ± 1 px at (507.3,723.0); head 72 ± 1 px wide × 60 ± 1 px long, base corners (489.3,754.2)/(525.3,691.8) ± 1 px, tip (559.3,753.0) ± 1 px; tip-to-vertex gap 10 ± 1 px along the centreline, gap pixels black (0,0,0), vertex (568,758) white (254,254,254) ± 2; overhang 20 ± 1 px per side; shaft/head colour #C32D30 only, with no red pixels outside the shaft-plus-head polygon. At 270 px the shaft near the head must measure 8 ± 1 px and the head 18 ± 1 px. (See the correction above: the "24 ± 1 px at (329.0,620.0)" check reads the unchanged shaft, ~21 px, not 24.) - Loop 84 study (36 NEW designs, running total ~2,309; none from loops 61–83): VERDICT — redraw the arrowhead only: lengthen the head 72 × 60 px → 72 × 84 px (apex 61.9° → 46.4°) AND close the gap 10 px → 6 px. Weakness: the arrowhead is wider than it is long (72 × 60 px, ratio 0.83, apex 61.9°), and the 10 px gap is 16.7% of the head's length. At 270 px / 3 m that is an 18 × 15 px blunt triangle hovering 2.5 px from the vertex — it reads as a flat triangle that has stopped short, not as a needle pointing at the corner. At full size the 61.9° apex is blunt against the clean white cube vertex. The gap alone is not the fault, and the shaft is not the fault; the head's proportions are. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 84): redraw the arrowhead only (one head geometry, in #C32D30, on the unchanged centreline): Head: 72 px wide × 84 px long (was 72 × 60), length/width 1.17 (was 0.83). Apex angle: 46.4° (was 61.9°). Calculation: 2 × arctan(36/84) = 2 × 23.2° = 46.4°. Gap: 6 px (was 10 px), still a deliberate gap of pure black. New tip (apex): (562.8,755.0) — vertex (568,758) minus 6 px along the 30.0° centreline: (568 − 5.2, 758 − 3.0). New base centre: (490.1,713.0) — tip minus 84 px along the centreline: (562.8 − 72.7, 755.0 − 42.0). Base station moves from s = 586 to s = 566. New base corners: (472.1,744.2) and (508.1,681.8) — base centre ± 36 px perpendicular to the centreline. Width unchanged at 72 px, so the shoulder step over the 32 px shaft (20 px each side) is unchanged. Entry (0,430), angle 30.0°, shaft taper anchors (16 px at the left edge, 20 px at s = 380, centre (329,620)) and the blue seam are frozen. The only shaft consequence, stated explicitly: the shaft is extended 20 px along the same centreline, at the same taper, to meet the new base at s = 566, where its width is still 32 px; its taper rate 20 → 32 px is now over 186 px instead of 206 px. Nothing else moves. At 270 px the new head is 18 × 21 px (longer than wide) and the gap is 1.5 px — 7.1% of head length, instead of 16.7%. WHY: no studied arrowhead was wider than long, and none had an apex as wide as 61.9°. The systematic benchmark, HTML Arrows (Dixon & Moe, Awwwards), shows every Unicode arrow (U+2190–U+219F), including the north-east arrow U+2197 in exactly the BLACK BOX direction, with a small narrow head, head length at least equal to its width and an apex of roughly 30–45°. Eight Arrows (vishal-hotwani, Awwwards) has a head about 1.37 times as long as wide with an apex of about 40°, on a thin shaft — and it still reads as sharp at small size. La Filmoteca d'estiu (DEMOCRÀCIA®, Behance) makes its point as one long tapered white wedge, apex about 20–25°, length far greater than width, whose tip touches/overlaps the figures below it — no floating gap, no short triangle. Catch — Wayfinding System Concept (Aleksei Slepov, Behance) does not use a wide filled triangle at all: its point is a sharp shaft corner/minimal chevron, proving direction is carried by the shaft and the head must only terminate it sharply, not become a separate blunt block. Ligne 18 — Find your line (L4nc, Dribbble) treats the line/trajectory as the point, at the official 18 m indoor archery distance, and deliberately moves away from red-and-black target language — at distance a short head plus a visible gap reads as a miss, not as intention. Fieldhouse (Muhammad Ibrahim, Dribbble) likewise carries direction with a long red diagonal bar and no triangle head, its end flush with its target. Together these say: lengthen the point, do not widen it; a 6 px gap then reads as a hairline separation, while a 10 px gap behind a 60 px head reads as a miss. Top-5: (1) HTML Arrows — Dixon & Moe (Awwwards): a complete chart of Unicode arrows U+2190–U+219F; every head is narrow, length ≥ width, apex roughly 30–45°; the north-east arrow U+2197 is the BLACK BOX direction. Proves 61.9° / width > length is outside the normal arrowhead range. (2) Eight Arrows — vishal-hotwani (Awwwards): blue arrow measured at about 1.37 length/width and about 40° apex on a thin shaft, still sharp at small size. Proves a longer-than-wide head is the working proportion, and gives the 40–46° target band for the new 46.4° apex. (3) La Filmoteca d'estiu — DEMOCRÀCIA® design (Behance): a long, acute white tapered wedge (apex about 20–25°) whose tip touches/overlaps its target. Proves points read by length and taper, not by width, and that a floating gap is not how a pointed poster element meets its target. (4) Catch — Wayfinding System Concept — Aleksei Slepov (Behance): the arrow's point is a sharp shaft corner/minimal chevron, with no wide filled triangle. Proves a wide 72 × 60 px triangle is not needed to point, and that a blunt filled head becomes a separate block rather than a termination. (5) Ligne 18 — Find your line — L4nc (Dribbble): archery identity built on the line/trajectory at 18 m, deliberately avoiding red-and-black target language. Proves that at distance the trajectory and the gap are judged together — a short head plus a relatively large gap reads as a miss, which is why the head must be lengthened as the gap is closed to 6 px. Rejected: close the gap to 6–8 px only, keeping 72 × 60 px / 61.9° (the head would still be wider than long and blunt at full size, and at 270 px still 18 × 15 px; the gap would be smaller but the point would still not look sharp. The evidence condemns the proportion, not only the gap); keep exactly as-is (61.9° is outside the 30–50° band in every studied arrow, the 0.83 length/width ratio is the reverse of the studied ratio, and 10 px is 16.7% of head length, which is why the thumbnail reads as a miss); narrow the head to 56 px wide × 60 px long (apex 50.0°) (it would shrink the head's presence (14 px wide at 270 px) and weaken the loop-82 enlargement, whereas lengthening to 84 px preserves the 72 px width and makes the head 21 px long at thumbnail); lengthen further to 96 px (apex 41.1°) (the head would become spear-like (length/shaft 3:1), the base would retreat to s = 554, and the gain over 84 px / 46.4° is not supported by the evidence; 46.4° already sits in the studied 40–50° band); close the gap to 0 px / touch the vertex (it would break the loop-81 intent (a gap of pure black, vertex clean white), risk red anti-aliasing onto the white vertex at full size, and read as touching/merging at 270 px); replace the filled head with an open chevron (as in Catch) (it would be a second, larger change to the wedge's identity and would lose the solid red mass that loop 82 built; lengthening the existing filled head is the smaller, buildable fix). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — show the 270 px poster at 3 m to at least 5 viewers who have not seen the brief, with no prompt about the arrow. Pass if at least 4 of 5 say, unprompted, that the red arrow points at / to the cube's corner, and none says it "missed", "stopped short" or "points at empty black". Fail if 2 or more say it missed, or if any reader cannot tell which corner it points to. Full-size blind-read test: show the 1080 px poster at normal reading distance to at least 5 fresh viewers. Pass if at least 4 of 5 describe the head as "sharp" / "pointed" (not "blunt", "flat", "a triangle" or "a play button") and say the tip is clearly aimed at the white vertex. Fail if 2 or more call it blunt, or if the gap is described as a mistake. Measurable file checks (1080 px PNG): tip pixel at (563,755) is #C32D30; the 6 px on the centreline between tip and vertex are pure black (0,0,0); vertex pixel (568,758) is white (254,254,254) with no red within 6 px of it; head length tip-to-base-centre is 84 ± 1 px, head width at the base is 72 ± 1 px, apex angle computed from the base corners is 46.4° ± 1°, length/width is 1.17; shaft width at the new base (490.1,713.0) is 32 ± 1 px; at 270 px the head measures 18 × 21 px and the gap 1.5 px. Any check outside those tolerances fails the build. (See the build notes: the "tip pixel is #C32D30" check and the "6 px pure black / no red within 6 px" checks carry the loop 81 nuances — a sharp anti-aliased tip's geometric tip pixel is a faint blend, never solid red, and in the export the gap midpoint sits on the silhouette stroke's anti-aliased corner and reads dark blue-grey, not (0,0,0); the checks hold in the wedge PNG, and in the export they are judged by the red → dark → white centreline read.) THE ONE CHANGE (loop 85): delete the intermediate taper station at s = 380 and re-grade the shaft as ONE single straight taper, 16 px at the left-edge entry to 32 px at the head base, with no intermediate station at all. Head (72×84 px, apex 46.4°), tip (562.8,755.0), 6 px gap, entry (0,430), angle 30.0° and seam are all frozen and unchanged. Build specification — centreline unchanged: entry (0,430), direction 30.0°, base centre s = 566 (490.1,713.0), tip s = 650 (562.8,755.0). New stations only: s = 0, width 16 px; s = 566, width 32 px. Each shaft edge is one straight line: upper edge (4.0,423.1) → (498.1,699.1); lower edge (−4.0,436.9) → (482.1,726.9), visible at the left edge as (0,420.9) → (0,439.3). Check widths (not stations): s = 190, 21.4 px; s = 283, 24.0 px; s = 380, 26.7 px at centre (329,620) — upper edge (335.7,608.4), lower edge (322.3,631.6); s = 475, 29.4 px; s = 566, 32.0 px. Colour #C32D30 unchanged; no new shape, collar, fillet or station. Why the current taper fails, recomputed from the stated stations: segment A 16→20 px over 380 px is 0.30° per side; segment B 20→32 px over 186 px is 1.85° per side — a 6.1× slope jump, 1.55° per side (larger than the ~1.15° estimated in loop 83's build notes, because loop 84 steepened Stage 2 to 186 px). At 270 px (×0.25) segment A widens by only 1 px over 95 px (invisible) while segment B widens by 3 px over 46.5 px, so the thumbnail reads as a parallel 4–5 px line plus a sudden flare to 8 px — two shafts, not one accelerating taper. At full size the same 6× jump is a visible vertex at (329,620). The longer head worsens this: head length 84 px is 2.6× the 32 px shaft base, so the thin, almost-parallel first two-thirds of the shaft look underweight beneath it. WHY, grounded in the designs studied: no successful tapered arrow in the 19 new designs uses a two-stage taper with a slope jump. The taper language that reads is either (a) one straight taper per edge, or (b) no taper at all — constant width with a clean shoulder into the head. The current design is the only one with two different slopes joined at a mid-shaft vertex, and that vertex is exactly where it breaks at both distances. Top-5: (i) La Corriols del Vallès, Gerard Arderius (Behance) — RECOUNT (studied in loop 83; the worker cited it again, but it adds no new evidence here): a poster of red arrows on yellow, every shaft constant width, heads ~2× shaft — proves a diagonal arrow needs no taper at all and a clean shoulder reads at poster distance. (ii) Unused Logo Concept — TriVanta Flight, Abida Sultana Nisa for LOGOHAUS (Dribbble): three converging slivers, each a single straight taper to a point with no intermediate station — proves the taper that reads is one straight edge. (iii) Unused Logo Concept — Aurexion Rise, Abida Sultana Nisa for LOGOHAUS (Dribbble): same single-straight-taper construction in three separated forms, no kink anywhere. (iv) Arrows, Sava Stoic (Dribbble): two curved arrows on black, constant-width shafts, triangular heads ~2× shaft — proves the shaft/head transition needs only a clean shoulder, not a flare. (v) Diagonal Arrow Logo, Khaled Ghanem (Behance): DA monogram with a constant-width diagonal stroke and small triangular head — proves a diagonal shaft reads without any widening toward the head. Corroborated by Hunting arrow labels, Forrest Marlowe (Dribbble) — a real arrow shaft is perfectly constant width — and Arrow project, Ashwinraj K (Behance) — where the head dominates, the shaft disappears, the failure mode the longer head risks here. Rejected: keep as-is (the 6.1× slope jump is measurable, not a matter of taste, and at 270 px segment A is sub-visible while segment B is a flare); smooth the kink with a Bézier/fillet keeping 16→20→32 px (rounding a vertex by a few px is invisible at 270 px and leaves two slopes; it adds curve parameters without removing the cause); constant-width shaft (24, 28 or 32 px) (32 px doubles the entry and crowds the left edge; 24–28 px leaves a 22–24 px shoulder per side (head ratio 2.6–3.0×), making the head look detached, and discards the taper that gives the wedge its acceleration); new gentler two-stage stations (e.g. 16→22→32 px) (any station off the single straight line reintroduces a kink; a station on that line is redundant); widen the base to 36–40 px to shrink the shoulder (it steepens the second-stage flare, worsens the kink, and changes the frozen head/shaft ratio); shorten the head back to 60 px (it reverses loop 84 and does not touch the shaft geometry); add a collar/ring at (329,620) (a second shape on a poster whose rule is one red wedge and one blue seam). - Loop 86 study (50 NEW designs — the worker claimed 53, corrected by main Muse — running total ~2,378): VERDICT — widen the shaft base from 32 px to 36 px, nothing else changes. Weakness: the head-to-shaft shoulder at thumbnail. At full size the 20 px-per-side step reads as a deliberate shoulder, but at 270 px / 3 m the head (18 px) sits on a shaft of only 8 px, with a 5 px step per side — a step equal to 62.5% of the shaft width, and a shaft anchoring only 44.4% of the 72 px head base. The head therefore risks reading as a separate, floating triangle on a thin stick, rather than as one arrow. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 86): widen the shaft base from 32 px to 36 px. Head UNCHANGED: 72 px wide × 84 px long, apex 46.4°, apex/tip (562.8,755.0), base centre (490.1,713.0), base corners (472.1,744.2) and (508.1,681.8), length/width 1.17. Shaft: ONE single straight taper, 16 px at the left edge (entry (0,430), angle 30.0°) → 36 px at the head base (s = 566). New taper 1.01° per side (was 0.81°), 2.02° total; no kink, no second stage. New shaft-base corners, at the head base: (481.1,728.6) and (499.1,697.4) — base centre (490.1,713.0) ± 18 px perpendicular to the 30.0° centreline. Half-width at base 18 px (was 16 px). New widths: at s = 283, 26.0 px (was 24.0 px); at s = 380, 29.4 px (was 26.7 px); at s = 566, 36.0 px. New shoulder: (72 − 36) / 2 = 18.0 px per side (was 20.0 px). Head/shaft ratio 72/36 = 2.00:1 (was 2.25:1). Shaft now anchors exactly 50.0% of the head base (was 44.4%). Step/shaft falls from 0.625 to 0.50. At 270 px (×0.25): head 18.0 px, shaft 9.0 px, step 4.5 px per side (was 18 / 8 / 5.0 px). At s = 380 at thumbnail, shaft 7.4 px (was 6.7 px). FROZEN, no move required: tip (562.8,755.0), 6 px gap to the cube vertex (568,758), entry (0,430), angle 30.0°, seam, colour #C32D30, all other elements. WHY, grounded in the designs studied: the arrows that read as one mass at small size do it by making the shaft carry the head — thick shaft, step small relative to the shaft — not by shrinking the head. BLACK BOX's head is long (84 px, length/width 1.17) and its back is a straight base with no notch, so the shaft alone must anchor it; at 44.4% anchorage it is below the ~50–60% anchorage seen in the successful examples. Widening the shaft to 36 px crosses the 50% / 2.00:1 threshold with the smallest possible intervention, preserves the head's 18 px thumbnail presence and its 46.4° apex, and leaves an 18 px step at full size — still unmistakably a deliberate shoulder, not a merge. Top-5: (i) UpShift — Gradient Symbol Representing Growth, Md Nur Islam (Behance): a diagonal arrow on black whose head is only ~1.6× its thick, continuous shaft; the step per side is under a third of the shaft width; reads as one mass at both the large black tile and the small white tile — proves thicken the carrier, do not shrink the head. (ii) Triangle up, Rido Saputra (Behance): the white arrow inside the dark triangle has a head far wider than any single line, yet it reads as one arrow because its shaft is three thin lines that together span ~60% of the head base and touch it across its width — proves anchorage across the base is what prevents a floating head; a single central stick anchoring 44.4% is the weakness. (iii) Arrow Head Logo Mark, Kevin Craft (Dribbble): the '004 Directional' marks are heads with no shaft at all — wide, short chevrons with a deep V-notch at the back — proves a head without a shaft needs a notch or a short/wide proportion to read as direction; BLACK BOX's head has a straight back and a long 1.17 proportion, so it cannot be left to float — its shaft must do the anchoring. (iv) >>-- Arrowhead -->, Van Berkemeyer (Dribbble): two small diagonal arrows whose heads are ~2× their shafts, but the heads are short, barbed/flint-like, not long triangles — proves a ~2.25:1 ratio can work only when the head is short; BLACK BOX's long 84 px head at 2.25:1 is the wrong combination, and moving the ratio to 2.00:1 by thickening the shaft is the proportionate correction. (v) Rank, Modern Growth Arrow Logo Design, Brand Identity, Nasim Ahmed (Behance, Jan 2026): the arrow is integrated into the letter R — the diagonal head flows into the vertical stem with no step at all, and the stem is ~half the head width — proves a shaft at ~50% of head width, with the junction treated as a continuation, reads cleanly at logo size and at full size. Rejected: narrow the head to 64 px (new base corners (474.1,740.7) and (506.1,685.3), apex 41.7°, length/width 1.31, step 16 px, thumbnail head 16 px vs shaft 8 px) — it reaches the same 2.00:1 ratio by shrinking the signal instead of strengthening the carrier (11% less head area at thumbnail, a sharper, more needle-like head that looks more detached, not less, and the shaft would still be only 8 px at 270 px; the successful studies shrink nothing, they thicken the shaft); keep the shoulder exactly as it is (at full size the 20 px step is indeed a clean deliberate shoulder — that is not in dispute — but at 270 px the step (5 px) is 62.5% of the shaft (8 px) and the overhang (40 px total) exceeds the shaft width (32 px); after loops 77–85 removed the fringes, narrowed the seam and re-graded the shaft, this is the next bottleneck; leaving it leaves the thumbnail risk unaddressed); widen the shaft base to 40 or 44–48 px (ratio 1.8–1.5:1) — a +25–50% widening gives a 1.21–1.69°/side taper and 32–35 px at s = 380; the shaft would compete with the cube, and the step would fall to 16–12 px, losing the deliberate shoulder at full size; 36 px is the minimal change that crosses 50% anchorage; add a fillet, notch or chevron cut at the head base — it would add a second shape/new edge, change the head geometry and break the single-mass silhouette; it is not one of the framed changes and is not needed once the shaft is 36 px; lengthen/shorten the head or move the tip — tip and 6 px gap are frozen; lengthening increases detachment, shortening blunts the 46.4° apex; thicken the shaft uniformly (16 px at the left edge as well) — it would destroy the taper that gives the wedge its speed and thicken the elegant thin entry. FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — render the built poster at 270 px, view from 3 m (or equivalent angular size), 5+ viewers who have not seen v85; ask unprompted 'one arrow, or a triangle on a stick?' and 'which way does it point?'; pass if ≥4/5 say one arrow, ≥4/5 point to the cube vertex, and nobody says 'floating / detached triangle'; fail if ≥2 say detached/floating, or ≥1 cannot find the shaft. Full-size blind-read test: at 1080 px from ~1 m, same viewers; ask 'clean deliberate shoulder, or a break between two shapes?'; pass if ≥4/5 say clean/deliberate shoulder and the step is still visible (not merged); fail if ≥2 say break, or ≥2 say the head and shaft have merged into one bar. - Loop 87 study (34 NEW designs — the worker claimed 33 but listed 34 (7 opened + 27 inspected, not 26) — running total ~2,412): VERDICT — widen the entry only: re-grade the shaft to ONE single straight taper from 24 px at the left edge to 36 px at the head base, keeping the 36 px base frozen. Weakness: the wedge entry. At 16 px it does not read as entering from off-canvas: at 270 px it is a 4 px red mark against a 9 px shaft at the head (ratio 0.44), and at full size it reads as a cropped-off stub / a taper vanishing at the edge, because the shaft narrows toward the left, so the eye completes the taper to a point just off-canvas. THE ONE CHANGE (loop 87): widen the entry only — re-grade the shaft to ONE single straight taper from 24 px at the left edge to 36 px at the head base, keeping the 36 px base frozen. Centreline unchanged: (0,430) → (562.8,755.0) at 30.0°. Entry width 24 px measured perpendicular to the centreline at s = 0; vertical intercept at x = 0 is 27.7 px, top edge (0,416.1), bottom edge (0,443.9). New taper: width(s) = 24 + 0.0212·s, i.e. 0.61° per side (was 1.01°); width at s = 380 is 32.1 px (was 29.4 px), at s = 200 is 28.2 px, at s = 100 is 26.1 px. Head base 36 px at s = 566, head-base centre (490.1,713.0), unchanged. No other element moves. WHY, grounded in the designs studied: in every studied bleed, the element meets the frame at full, or near-full, width. A taper that is thinnest at the frame reads as a tip or a vanishing point, not a slice through a continuing object; a crop reads as deliberate only when the cut is substantial. At 24 px the entry is two-thirds of the base (0.67), 6 px at 270 px against 9 px at the head — a legible diagonal bar, not a hairline — while a visible taper (0.61°/side) and the 2:1 head/shaft step are preserved. 24 px is the smallest widening that changes the read: +50% at both distances (4 → 6 px at thumbnail; 18.5 → 27.7 px vertical cut at full size). It is also a clean build number: one-third of the 72 px head width, twice the 12 px seam. Top-5: (i) ART WEEK 21 | Dynamic Minimalist Poster Design, Nazli Aydin (Dribbble): magenta arrows with constant-width shafts bleed off the right edge at full width while their 3D cube heads sit inside the frame; the bleed reads as continuation precisely because width at the edge equals width everywhere (ratio 1.0) — BLACK BOX at 0.44 is the inverse of this. (ii) Art exhibition in a museum, Aurélie Gasser (Behance): the CALATRAVA poster crops letters hundreds of pixels tall, cutting off about a third of the letterforms — a crop reads as deliberate only when the cut element is large and the cut is substantial; a 16 px / 4 px sliver is the opposite case. (iii) FRAME — Poster Series & Exhibition Identity, Marta Wasilewska (Behance): a thin blue arrow loops fully inside the frame and never touches an edge, reading as a drawn mark, not an entry — touching the edge at thin width is the worst of both readings, it claims entry while drawing like an interior mark. (iv) KATACHI — The Structure Beneath, Révolté (Dribbble): "the one rule I held without exception across every single execution: #C8391A warning red appears once" — a single-red system like BLACK BOX — and its diagonal hazard is a constant-width stripe, never tapered to a point at a frame. (v) Solune — Brand Identity Design, Typefool / Jon Nuredini (Behance): black field, single white mark, small type, nothing touches any edge — in BLACK BOX the wedge is the only edge-touching element, so its entry carries the whole off-canvas claim alone and must be unambiguous (Null Project Vol.1, Mercedes Bazan, shows the same contained black-field discipline). Rejected: keep the entry as-is (4 px vs 9 px at thumbnail and a taper narrowing to the edge both read as starting/thinning out, not entering); widen to 20 px (5 px at thumbnail, ratio 0.56, still below the two-thirds threshold and too small a change to alter the read); widen to 28 or 32 px (taper flattens to 0.41°/0.20° per side, mid-shaft thickens to 33.4–34.7 px at s = 380, the shaft becomes a near-parallel stripe and begins to compete with the cube; no studied bleed required more than two-thirds); start the wedge inside the canvas, e.g. at (120,499.3) with a flat cap (it creates a floating stub end in the black void, reads as a dart starting mid-air, contradicts the off-canvas idea, and adds a second terminal to resolve); bleed it differently by extending the drawing beyond the left edge (from x = −200) (with the same taper the width at x = 0 would fall below 16 px — the opposite of what is needed); round or chamfer the entry cap, or add a glow/halo at the entry (cosmetic, changes no width ratio, and a rounded cap reads as a terminal, not a cut, while a halo would muddy the black void); move the entry to another edge or change the 30.0° angle (it would move the centreline, tip, 6 px gap and head, violating the frozen elements with no supporting evidence); make the shaft parallel at 36 px throughout (it kills the wedge taper and the loop-85 single-taper grading). FALSIFICATION: 270 px / 3 m blind-read test — show v87 at 270 px from 3 m for 5 seconds to 5+ people who have not seen v86 and ask "where does the red shape come from?"; pass if at least 4 of 5 say "from off the left edge / outside the frame" and none say "it starts at the left edge", "it thins out" or "it's a line starting here"; fail if any reader calls it a starting line. Full-size blind-read test: at 1080 px from 1 m, ask the same question plus "is the left end a clean cut or a stub?"; pass if at least 4 of 5 say clean cut / entering and none say stub, cropped-off or pointed end. Measurable file checks: entry width 24.0 ± 0.5 px perpendicular at x = 0; vertical intercept 27.7 ± 0.5 px, top (0,416.1), bottom (0,443.9), ±1 px; width at s = 380, centreline point (329.1,620.0), 32.1 ± 0.5 px; width at s = 566, 36.0 ± 0.5 px; taper 0.61° ± 0.05° per side; centreline angle 30.0° ± 0.2°; tip (562.8,755.0) ± 1 px; gap 6 px with vertex pixel (254,254,254); head 72 × 84, apex 46.4°; colour #C32D30 only. If the blind read still fails at 24 px, the next step is 28 px — not a crop change. - Loop 88 study (28 NEW designs — the worker claimed 29, corrected by main Muse — running total ~2,440): VERDICT — the head no longer dominates. At v87 the head-to-shaft ratio is exactly 2.00:1 at the base (72 px head against a 36 px shaft), and only 2.24:1 at s = 380 (72 / 32.1). At 270 px that is an 18 px head against an 8 px shaft at s = 380 and a 9 px shaft at the base, with a shoulder step of only 4.5 px per side. The 0.61°-per-side taper is too shallow to carry the acceleration, so the wedge reads at both distances as a fat red bar with a small cap, not as a shaft accelerating into a climax. THE ONE CHANGE: grow the head, by uniform scale, anchored at the tip — do not re-grade the shaft, do not move the tip, do not change the angle, colour or seam. New head: 96 px wide × 112 px long (was 72 × 84), i.e. a ×4/3 uniform scale of the existing head. Length/width stays 1.17:1; apex angle stays 46.4° (half-angle 23.2°, tan = 48/112 = 36/84). Tip (apex) UNCHANGED: (562.8,755.0). Gap to the cube vertex (568,758) UNCHANGED: 6.0 px (distance √(5.2² + 3.0²)). New base centre, moved 28 px back along the 30.0° centreline from (490.1,713.0): (465.8,699.0). New s at the base: 538 px (was 566). New base corners, ±48 px perpendicular to the centreline from the new base centre: (441.8,740.6) and (489.8,657.4) (were (472.1,744.2) and (508.1,681.8)). Shaft at the new base: 35.4 px wide (24 + 12 × 538/566). New ratios: head/shaft 96/35.4 = 2.71:1 at the base; 96/32.1 = 2.99:1 at s = 380. Shoulder step per side: (96 − 35.4)/2 = 30.3 px (was 18.0 px). Head area: ½ × 96 × 112 = 5,376 px² (was 3,024 px², +78%). Head as a share of total wedge length: 112/650 = 17.2% (was 12.9%). At 270 px (×0.25): head 24.0 px wide × 28.0 px long, shaft 8.0 px at s = 380 and 8.9 px at the base, shoulder step 7.6 px per side. Everything else frozen: entry 24 px at (0,430), top edge (0,416.1), bottom edge (0,443.9); taper 0.61° per side, 24→36 px; angle 30.0°; colour #C32D30 only; seam unchanged. Top-5: UP Logo exploration, Arrow logo — Zikospace (Dribbble): a white negative-space arrow whose head is about 2.8–3.4× its shaft, on a short shaft — proves a head at 2.0× is below the range in which a head dominates a thick shaft; ~2.7–3.0× is the working minimum. Sun, arrow - Logo Explorations — Victor Murea (Dribbble): a broad, shallow chevron head about 4× its shaft is the whole width of the mark — proves that with a thick shaft, dominance comes from head width, not head length alone. Arrow Logo Mark — Ahmed (Dribbble): a large diagonal triangular head about 3.7× its bar, occupying about half the mark — proves a long, thick shaft still reads as accelerating when the head is several times the shaft. Growth | Typographical Poster — Karl Bembridge (Behance): the 't' is a thin teal growth-chart arrow; its modest head works only because the shaft is thin — proves the ratio, not the absolute head size, is what matters, and that the BLACK BOX's thick shaft therefore needs a larger head. P Logo Design | Direction & Business Growth logo — Anik Khandaker / Riya Moni (Behance): a black P with a wide white chevron head against a narrow white shaft — proves, in negative space, that the step from shaft to head must be large and legible at small size, as the new 30.3 px step (7.6 px at 270 px) is. Rejected: keep the head exactly as-is (72 × 84) — ratio 2.00:1 at the base, 18 px vs 9 px at 270 px, step 4.5 px per side at thumbnail — the evidence band starts at ~2.7:1, and the head reads as a cap on a fat bar at both distances. Re-grade the shaft thinner, e.g. 24→28 px (taper ~0.40°/side, 26.8 px at s = 380, ratio 72/28 = 2.57) — it restores the ratio arithmetically but undoes loops 86–87, thins the shaft to 6.7 px at 270 px, makes the taper almost invisible, and leaves the head still only 18 px at thumbnail — the climax stays small in absolute terms; the studied marks keep thick shafts and enlarge the head. Widen the head only, e.g. 96 × 84 (apex ~59.7°, length/width 0.88) — it blunts the apex and undoes the loop-84 sharpening; the head becomes a squat flat triangle, not a piercing wedge. Lengthen the head only, e.g. 72 × 112 (apex ~35.6°, length/width 1.56) — width and ratio stay 2.00:1, so the thumbnail problem is unsolved; it makes a needle, not a dominant head. Grow the head more, e.g. 108 × 126 (×1.5, ratio ~3.05 at the base, area +125%) — it overshoots the evidenced band, risks the head reading as a separate red triangle detached from the shaft, and gives the red mass more weight than the cube's left edge needs. Add a second chevron, a double head, or a stronger glow/halo — it adds a new element and breaks the single-red / only-chromatic-line discipline without fixing the geometric ratio. COUNT CORRECTION BY MAIN MUSE: the worker claimed 29 new designs, but ONE is a recount — 'Letter G Growth Arrow Logo' (Tornike Uchava, inspected in search results) is an exact title match in the banned file (line 302, from the loop 84 region) — a distinctive multi-word title, so the exact match is a recount. True new count is 28, running total ~2,440. The verdict does not depend on it (an inspected item, not Top-5, not cited in the WHY). FALSE-POSITIVE NOTE (for the next count check): five of the worker's titles ('ARROW Logo Design + Visual Assets', 'Arrow Logo Mark', 'Arrow Automation Logo Concept', 'Arrow Capital', 'Arrow Automation Logo Concept for Industrial Production Machines') merely START with the banned single-word base title 'Arrow' (Kibong Yi's work) — per the loop 85 ruling (same title, different author is new, for the generic word 'Arrow') and the loop 82 'Red Arrow' ruling, these are distinct works by distinct authors and count as NEW. A `startswith` check is only valid for distinctive multi-word base titles, never for a single generic word. GEOMETRY CHECK BY MAIN MUSE (verified before building): the ×4/3 scale preserves the apex angle and the tip, so the new head's edges are the SAME LINES as the old head's edges — the new head = the old head + a trapezoid from s = 538 to s = 566 (width 96→72 px). The shaft from s = 538 to s = 566 (width 35.4→36 px) lies inside that trapezoid. So NO erase is needed: draw the trapezoid (s = 538→566, half-width 48→36) over the existing wedge at 4× supersampling. The only new red is the trapezoid minus the shaft's area in that range: ½×(96+72)×28 − ½×(35.4+36)×28 = 2,352 − 999.6 = 1,352.4 px² geometric. TWO WORDING SLIPS IN THE STUDY (not build errors, noted for the verification): (1) the collision check's 'head y-range 657.4–740.6' omits the tip at y = 755 — the head's true y-range is 657.4–755.0; the conclusion (no overlap with wordmark/tagline/microcopy/footer) still holds since y755 is also clear of them. (2) The falsification check 'no red pixel east of x = 563 or north of y = 657 except the tip' is misplaced — the tip is at (562.8,755.0), which is neither east of x = 563 nor north of y = 657; the substance (the head stays west of x = 563 and south of y = 657) is right. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the loop 88 study): Loop 89 — with the head grown to 96 × 112 px (area 5,376 px², +78%, shoulder step 30.3 px per side), does the larger red mass now outweigh the cube and pull the eye to the head itself instead of through it to the cube vertex and the blue seam — and does the 30.3 px shoulder step read as a clean acceleration break or as a hard notch at full size? In other words: is the red now too dominant, and should the next change be a reduction in the red glow/halo, a softening of the shoulder, or nothing at all?

- Loop 89 study (24 NEW designs — the worker claimed 24, verified by main Muse, no recounts — running total ~2,464): VERDICT — the single biggest remaining weakness is not the solid head itself, but the red glow/halo around it. The solid head is only 5,376 px² — 0.46% of the 1,080×1,080 canvas and about 1.7% of the cube's 746×420 px bounding box; at 270 px it is a 24×28 px triangle, about 336 px² against the cube's 187×105 px. By area it cannot outweigh the cube. Its perceived weight is chroma plus halo: a halo spreads tinted black well beyond the solid edge, more than doubling the effective red footprint, and at thumbnail it merges head and shaft into a red smear that stops the eye. The 30.3 px shoulder step, by contrast, is a clean 2.71:1 head/shaft ratio with straight head edges and a 46.4° apex — a normal arrowhead break, not a notch. THE ONE CHANGE: reduce the red glow/halo only; freeze all geometry. Delete the wide faint red ambient glow over the black field entirely — set its opacity to 0%. Set the wedge/head outer glow to one tight layer only: #C32D30, Gaussian blur 16 px, opacity 20%, spread/choke 0. Cap its reach at 16 px perpendicular from any solid #C32D30 edge, and clip/mask it so NO glow pixel enters the cube rectangle X 274–1020, Y 580–1000, and NO glow pixel lies at or beyond the tip along the centreline past (562.8,755.0). In particular, no glow in the 6 px gap and none in a 12 px-radius disc centred on the cube vertex (568,758). Everything else frozen exactly as v88: tip (562.8,755.0), 6 px gap, entry 24 px at (0,430), centreline angle 30.0°, head 96×112 px, apex 46.4°, base centre (465.8,699.0), base corners (441.8,740.6) and (489.8,657.4), shaft taper 24 px at the left edge → 36 px at the head base (s=566), 35.4 px at s=538, seam 12 px solid #1D4ED8 centred on (697,767)–(647,903). Top-5: 'A high-contrast red and black brutalist poster of Alan Walker' — Tamanna Akter (Dribbble): huge dark-red WALKER type dominates a black field and the portrait; the red block is the destination, not a pointer — proves saturated red mass stops the eye when it becomes a block. 'Where Minimalism Comes From: Russian Constructivism' — Grigorii Lapin for The Designest (Dribbble): the huge red 'M' stops the eye; the black CONSTRUCTIVISM wedge, a sharp taper with no shoulder step, points straight through — proves taper without a step reads as through-line, block does not. 'LIVE SESSION — Constructivist Poster Design' — Gamid Rustamov (Dribbble): 45° diagonal, red kept thin, flat and unglowed (thin lines, small triangles, one flat red triangle); the black diagonal carries the reading — proves a diagonal vector works when red has no halo. 'Vitrium Opus - 3D Glass Typography Posters' — Ion Lucin (Behance): glass typography on black, colour inside the glass, no glow halo; the pale glass stays the hero — proves the cube can dominate without a competing red glow. 'RED BOMB™ — Structural Packaging & Preserves Identity' — Harrynson Ramirez / Trento Studio (Behance): a red label is deliberately the hero on heavy black stock — proves that red-as-hero is a real, intentional effect, and the one to avoid when red is meant only to point to the cube. Rejected: shrink the head back (e.g. to 84×98 px, or to the loop-87 72×84 px) — it undoes loop 88, and at 270 px the head would fall to about 21×24.5 px, below a legible pointer; the solid head is already only 1.7% of the cube's bounding box, so area is not the problem. Soften the shoulder with a fillet/chamfer (e.g. 12–18 px radius at the base corners) — at full size it would blur the acceleration break and make the head read as a separate rounded triangle bolted onto the shaft; at 270 px a 12–18 px fillet is only 3–4.5 px and invisible, so it buys nothing at thumbnail. Keep everything exactly as-is — the glow is the unmeasured multiplier, it more than doubles the effective red area and, at thumbnail, merges head and shaft into a smear; keeping it leaves the loop-89 question unanswered. Darken/desaturate the red (e.g. away from #C32D30) — it changes the single brand red, weakens thumbnail contrast, and is unnecessary; the glow, not the hue, is the lever. Move the tip, widen the 6 px gap, or widen the blue seam — all are frozen unless the change requires it; this change does not, and moving the tip would break the pointing geometry verified in loops 77–88. TRANSLATION BY MAIN MUSE (the study's change is specified as Canva effect settings, but the card's glows are NOT Canva effects — they are three separate elements, and there is no blur/opacity setting on the wedge to halve): the buildable change is (1) DELETE the ambient 'Blur Gradient' graphic (native, 979×1100 at X −40, Y 0, 40% transparency, both stops #C32D30, behind everything just above Background) — the wide faint red tint; (2) DELETE the outer cube halo PNG (862×485 at (216,547), core alpha 75%) — the wider halo layer; (3) set the tight inner halo PNG's Canva Transparency to 20 (876×451 at (209,567), core alpha 95%, L-shaped left+top edges only) — one tight layer at 20% opacity (baked core 95% renders at ~19% effective). The study's 'Gaussian blur 16 px' is a property of the baked PNG and is not adjustable in the editor; the tight halo is already the tight layer. The study's 'cap reach at 16 px' and 'no glow in the cube rectangle / gap / vertex disc' are already satisfied by the layer order: both halo PNGs sit BEHIND the opaque cube image (frame X 274–1020, Y 580–1000), so no halo pixel can show inside the cube rectangle, the gap (562–568, 755–758), or the vertex disc. MEASURED IN THE v88 EXPORT BY MAIN MUSE (the study's premise, confirmed): the ambient glow tints the black field around the shaft with R 20–27 at 16–40 px perpendicular from the shaft at s = 200, and R 36–45 at s = 300 — a visible red haze strongest near the head, and at thumbnail it spreads the red ~10 px around the shaft, merging head and shaft into a smear. At s = 100 the tint is only R 6–11. Around the head north/west of the cube frame the field reads R = 0 (hidden by the cube image). FALSIFICATION-CHECK CORRECTION BY MAIN MUSE: the study's check 'at 20 px perpendicular from the shaft at s = 380, R ≤ 12' passes trivially as written — s = 380 is inside the cube's frame, hidden by the opaque cube image, so R is already 0 there. The REAL test is at s = 200–300 (outside the cube frame), where v88 reads R 22–45; after the rebuild those must drop to near 0. - Loop 90 study (20 NEW designs — the worker claimed 20, verified by main Muse, no recounts — running total ~2,484): VERDICT — the single biggest remaining weakness is the seam itself. At 270 px it is only a 3 px saturated-blue mark, a lone, unrepeated line on a now 80% pure-black field, with nothing else to help the eye find it. It is the pointer's destination and the cube's only chromatic line, yet at thumbnail / 3 m it is at the edge of visibility, and at full size 12 px is underweight for a 145 px fold. THE ONE CHANGE: widen the blue seam, and nothing else — from 12 px total to 20 px total, centred on the existing centreline (697,767)–(647,903), endpoints unchanged. New construction, perpendicular to the centreline: 16 px solid #1D4ED8 in the centre + 2 px linear feather on each side (16 + 2 + 2 = 20 px). Colour, centreline, endpoints, length (~145 px) and flat ends unchanged. No pale core, no new glow, no length change. At 270 px (×0.25) this is 5 px wide in total, 4 px solid saturated blue, against 3 px / 2 px solid now — a 67% increase in seam area, from ~1,740 px² to ~2,900 px² (145 × 20). At full size 20 px is 13.8% of the seam's length, still a seam, not a band, and still narrower than the shaft entry (24 px) and far narrower than the head (96 px). Half-width is 10 px perpendicular to the centreline: offset ±9.4 px in x and ±3.5 px in y from the centreline (perpendicular unit 0.939, 0.345). Top-5: 'Do you like Blue?' — GHOM JEA (Behance): blue glass folds read because the blue at each fold is a broad saturated band with a soft pale edge, repeated fold after fold — fold colour needs area, not just an edge. 'Cut Light' — Yutaka Kadota (Behance, enlarged crop inspected): a white cube on pure black; its fold is a hairline, yet the cube is unmistakable because the whole face is luminous and the bottom edge is a broad lit band — a hairline seam alone cannot carry a black poster, and BLACK BOX has no luminous face to compensate. 'HIGHLIGHT THE DARKNESS' — Emmanuel Chege (Behance): a glass on black is legible because its rim highlight is a thick, bright, multi-stroke white band — a highlight on a dark ground must be broad and bright to read; a single thin line is not a highlight. 'Glass Edge | Brand Identity' — Nouran Ashraf (Behance): the glass cube mark is defined by thick white edge strokes on deep blue — cube edges are drawn bold, even in a minimal identity, when they must be found at small size. 'Plakat festiwalu Jazz nad Odrą' — Monika Waryszak (Behance): thin gold lines on a near-black poster survive only because ~100 wavy lines repeat across the whole field — the seam, with no repetition, cannot borrow that survival mechanism; width is its only available mechanism. COUNT: 20 new, 0 recounts (banned file now 512 titles covering loops 61–90). The clip tapers the new seam's B end to the B vertex, exactly as the v80 seam already does; the C end (s = 30) stays a flat cap. (The study's 'the widened strip stays inside the cube (X 274–1020, Y 580–1000)' checks the cube's FRAME, not the hexagon — the hexagon clip is the real constraint, per the loop 70 lesson.) - Loop 91 study (28 NEW designs — the worker claimed 28, verified by main Muse, no recounts — running total ~2,512): VERDICT — the single biggest remaining weakness is the tip-to-vertex gap, not the seam. THE ONE CHANGE: keep the seam exactly as it is at 20 px, and widen the red tip-to-vertex gap from 6 px to 14 px, by moving the whole red wedge back 8 px along its own 30.0° centreline. New tip (555.9, 751.0); vertex stays (568, 758); gap 14.0 px full-size, 3.5 px at 270 px. The wedge is translated, not reshaped: angle 30.0°, entry centre (0, 430), entry width 24 px, shaft taper, head 96 × 112 px, apex 46.4° and head area 5,376 px² are all unchanged. Consequential positions only: head base centre (465.8, 699.0) → (458.9, 695.0); base corners (441.8, 740.6) → (434.9, 736.6) and (489.8, 657.4) → (482.9, 653.4); centreline distance from entry to tip 650 → 642 px, to head base 538 → 530 px; shaft width at the head base 35.4 → ~35.2 px (head/shaft ratio 2.71 → ~2.73). The seam is frozen: centreline (697, 767)–(647, 903), 20 px total (16 px solid #1D4ED8 + 2 px linear feather each side), no pale core, C-end flat cap, B-end tapered by the hexagon clip. Top-5: 'A letter + arrow in negative space + pointer logo design symbol' — Alex Tass (Dribbble): the negative-space arrow inside the blue A is legible at thumbnail because its white gap is roughly stroke-width, ~20% of the mark — a deliberate break must be a shape, not a sliver; 6 px / 6.25% of the arrowhead is below that working proportion, 14 px / 14.6% is in it. 'Negative Space Marks 15 Years' — Daniel Bodea / Kreatank (Behance): 15 years of negative-space marks read because their cut-outs are broad, chunky shapes — a 1.5 thumb-px black sliver is not a cut-out in that sense; a 3.5 thumb-px gap is. 'The Thin Blue Line' — SANUK KIM (Behance): wide blue diagonals laid over a halftone face read as separate strips/bands that divide the face — that is what a band does, and it is 3–5× wider, proportionally, than the 20 px seam (2.7% of the cube), so the seam is below band scale and can stay. 'Abstract Glass Ribbon' — Danny Ivan (Behance): a glass ribbon stays one continuous object with only a thin coloured edge on its fold; a coloured fold accent does not split a form whose silhouette is continuous — the cube's continuous hexagon does the same work. 'Kyivstar Business Cube' — Multiple Owners (Behance): each cube reads as one object from tonal faces meeting at thin edges; separate objects are separated by wide dark gaps — in BLACK BOX the cube faces have no gap at all (they touch the blue), so the cube is one object, and the arrow-to-cube gap is the poster's only true separation, so it must be wide enough to read as one. COUNT: 28 new, 0 recounts (banned file now 520 titles covering loops 61–91). BUILD CORRECTION BY MAIN MUSE: the shoulder step is 30.30 → 30.38 px per side (it INCREASES ~0.1 px, because the shaft is narrower at the new head base: 35.41 → 35.24 px), NOT '~30.3 → ~30.1' as the study says. Head/shaft ratio 2.711 → 2.724 (the study's '~2.73' is a rounding of 2.724). FALSIFICATION-CHECK CORRECTIONS BY MAIN MUSE: (1) 'every pixel on the tip→vertex centreline segment is pure black' is unmeetable as written — the pixels adjacent to the tip (the tip's anti-aliased edge) and adjacent to the vertex (the silhouette stroke's anti-aliased corner) are blends, not pure black (the loop 81 lesson). Judge by the gap's CENTRAL pixels being dark and the red → dark → white centreline read. (2) 'no white pixel within 14 px of the tip' is wrong as written — the vertex (at exactly 14 px from the tip) IS white. The intent is: no white pixel in the gap EXCEPT the vertex itself. NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 92 — with the gap now 14 px, does the arrow still read as confidently aimed at the vertex at full size, or does the larger break read as a miss — and is 14 px the right size, or would 12 px have been enough?

- Loop 92 study (21 NEW designs — the worker claimed 21, verified by main Muse, no recounts — running total ~2,533): VERDICT — the single biggest remaining weakness is the gap itself at full size: at 14 px it is 14.6% of the 96 px arrowhead width and 39.8% of the 35.2 px shaft width at the head base, large enough, on a 1080 px poster, for the eye to read the wedge as stopping short of — hesitating before — the cube's upper-left vertex, rather than as confidently aimed at it. At 270 px the cost of closing it is negligible (3.5 px → 3.0 px of dark, still a clear deliberate break), while at full size the gain is real. The aim is carried by the 556 px shaft and by exact alignment, not by the size of the break, so the break should be as small as thumbnail legibility allows. THE ONE CHANGE: narrow the gap from 14 px to 12 px, by moving the whole wedge forward 2.0 px along its own centreline, at 30.0°, with nothing else changed. New tip (557.6, 752.0); new head base centre (460.6, 696.0); new head base corners (436.6, 737.6) and (484.6, 654.4); head base station s = 532 (was s = 530). Gap ratios after the change: 12 px = 12.5% of head width 96 px (was 14.6%); 10.7% of head length 112 px; 1.6% of cube width 746 px (was 1.9%); 34.1% of shaft width at the head base, 35.2 px (was 39.8%). At 270 px: gap 3.0 px of dark (was 3.5 px); head width 24 px, so the gap is still 12.5% of head width at thumbnail. Unchanged: angle 30.0°, entry centre (0, 430), entry width 24.0 px, top edge (0, 416.1), bottom edge (0, 443.9); head 96 × 112 px, apex 46.4°, head/shaft ratio 2.72:1, shaft taper 0.61° per side, shaft 32.1 px at s = 380 and 35.2 px at the head base, vertex (568, 758), cube, seam, wordmark, tagline and footer all unchanged. Top-5: 'Target' — Mujahid Robiul (a short, off-axis projectile reads as a miss, while contact reads as a hit — the difference is axis plus proximity, not gap alone; the strongest warning against a larger gap). 'arrow+target' — Rob Eagle (aim at a point is conventionally shown by contact; any gap is a deliberate statement, so it should be minimal). 'Free Minimalist Arrow Logo' — logodrop.co (a white chevron head detached from its shaft by a wide negative-space gap still reads unambiguously as one arrow because the axis is exact — a 12 px gap cannot break the arrow if the 30.0° alignment is exact). 'Pointer' — Alex Lookdesign (stopped-short can read as pointing, but only for a containing target, so a point vertex needs a tighter gap than a circle does). 'The Space Between Us' — Thomas de Franzoni ('what connects us is not what we share, but the space we leave for one another' — the 12 px break is that space, not a failure to reach). COUNT: 21 new, 0 recounts (banned file now 541 titles covering loops 61–92). BUILD CORRECTIONS BY MAIN MUSE: (1) the '556 px shaft' is WRONG — the shaft's length (entry to tip, along the centreline) is 641.9 px (v91) / 643.9 px (v92); the study's '556' is the tip's x-coordinate (555.9), not the shaft's length. The trajectory-to-gap ratios are ~46:1 (14 px) and ~54:1 (12 px), not 40:1/46:1. The conclusion still holds — in fact it is stronger. (2) The shaft width at the NEW head base (s = 532) is 35.28 px (≈35.3), not 35.2 — the study's '35.2' is the value at s = 530 (the old head base). A 0.08 px rounding slip. The shoulder step at s = 532 is 30.36 px; the head/shaft ratio is 2.721. (3) '16.7% shorter' — the gap shrinks by 2 px, which is 14.3% of the 14 px gap (the study's 16.7% is (14−12)/12, the change relative to the new value). FALSIFICATION-CHECK CORRECTION: 'at least 3.0 px of black at 270 px (12.0 px at full size) with no red or white pixel' — as established in loop 91, the gap's pixels in the export read as dark blends (the silhouette stroke's anti-aliased corner), not pure black. The correct check is: no red-dominant pixel and no white pixel in the gap, with the gap reading dark, and the red → dark → white centreline read holding. BUILD (script `build-v92.py`, surgical edit of the v91 wedge PNG → `slash-wedge-point-746-gap12-head96x112-taper1-base36-entry24-steep30.png`, sha256 `c7aadd46`, 28,723 bytes): the new head is WIDER than the old head at a fixed s (by 1.58 px), so the new head CONTAINS the old head in the overlap region (s = 532…642) — no erase is needed for the head's sides. The erase is only the old head's base trapezoid sides (s = 530…532, outside the shaft) — erase (old head MINUS new head MINUS shaft), dilated ~2 px with a soft edge — then draw the new head triangle at 4× supersampling. THE SHAFT IS EXCLUDED FROM THE ERASE: the shaft at s = 530…532 lies inside the old head but outside the new head, so a naive (old MINUS new) erase would delete the shaft there and leave a 2 px transparent gap in it — the shaft polygon (s = 0…566, half-width 12…18 px) is subtracted from the erase mask. FIRST BUILD ATTEMPT (sha256 `7f4b6c6e`) WAS DISCARDED: it erased (old MINUS new) WITHOUT excluding the shaft, so the shaft at s = 530 read NO red pixels at all and the red-px delta was −104 instead of the expected +71. The fix: exclude the shaft from the erase AND redraw the shaft over s = 522…534 at 4× supersampling (the erase's ~2 px dilation + blur eats ~2.5 px per side into the shaft's edge at s = 530…532) — composited BEFORE the new head. Shaft widths identical at s = 100/200/283/380/475, and s = 530 now reads the shaft's 35.0–35.5 px (expect 35.2) — the shaft is restored. Entry intercept identical (417, 443, 27). Tip: last solid red at s = 642 (207, 47, 51, alpha 240), ~2 px short of the geometric tip at s = 644 (558, 752) (alpha 2, a faint blend — the loop 84 note); gap pixels s = 645…656 alpha 0 (transparent). Diff: 1,717 px in the head zone; 130 px OUTSIDE the head zone (s 518.9–525.0, |d| 0–20.1) — the shaft-redraw's LANCZOS footprint: 83 px at abs-diff 1–5, 25 px at 6–15, 17 px at abs-diff 51–255 (transparent pixels whose RGB changed while alpha went 0→1 — invisible); the shaft's widths at s = 519/522/525/528/530 are all correct, so the 130 px are resampling noise, not a geometry defect (the same class as the loop 82 bleed, 70 px, and the loop 88 bleed, 51 px). NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 93 — with the gap now at 12 px (3.0 px at 270 px), does the tip still read as stopping short at thumbnail, or does 3.0 px now collapse, at 3 m, into the white vertex so that the arrow looks as if it touches the cube — and if it does collapse, is the fix to hold 12 px and sharpen the apex, or to return to 13 px?

- **Loop 93 — SHARPEN THE APEX 46.4° → 36.0°, HOLD THE GAP AT 12 px (study 29 NEW — worker claimed 30, 1 recount — total ~2,562; banned file now 570 unique titles covering loops 61–93):** VERDICT — the weakness is not the 12 px distance itself, it is the blunt 46.4° apex. At full size a 46.4° wedge is 10.3 px wide 12 px back from its tip, so it looks like a broad wedge about to dock with the white vertex; at 270 px its 96 px head is 24 px wide and squeezes the 3.0 px black channel between two large masses, so the black reads as a hairline / anti-aliasing fringe, not a deliberate pause. 12 px is the thumbnail floor for distance, but only if the tip is sharpened. THE CHANGE: HOLD the gap at exactly 12 px and SHARPEN the apex from 46.4° to 36.0°, keeping head length at 112 px. Tip stays frozen at (557.6, 752.0); base centre stays frozen at (460.6, 696.0); head width falls from 96 px to 72.8 px. New base corners: (442.4, 727.5) and (478.8, 664.5). New head area 4,076.8 px² (was 5,376 px²); new length/width 112 / 72.8 = 1.54 (was 1.17); new head/shaft ratio 72.8 / 35.3 = 2.06:1 (was 2.72:1); new shoulder step 18.8 px per side (was 30.4 px). Red width 12 px back from the tip falls from 10.3 px to 7.8 px full size (1.95 px at 270 px, was 2.57 px), so the 12 px gap becomes 16.5% of head width (was 12.5%). Nothing else moves. Top-5: megan collins 'Iron Horse Recycling | Logo' (blunt ~60° arrowheads survive only because they touch and form a continuous triangle — blunt + a hairline separation is not robust); Abbas 'Smart Negative Space Logos VOL 2' (in 'lamp', the tiny white bulb and filament inside the black 'a' collapse at thumbnail to a white blob, while the word 'lamp' still reads — a small negative-space detail that carries the meaning collapses first, and in BLACK BOX the gap carries the meaning); Multiple Owners 'OFF SPACE – Identity & Editorial' (thin separations between large black masses merge at thumbnail; only wide separations stay legible); Tornike Uchava 'S Arrow' (the arrow is integrated into the 'S', with no separate gap — a tip that is integrated needs no gap, while a separated tip must be made legible by its shape); Vadim Sadovski 'NASA Missions — Space Probe Poster Series' (probes are large isolated masses in black space, separated from planets by tens of pixels, so there is no touch ambiguity; their 1–2 px antennae and small type vanish at thumbnail — thin lines and small type are expendable, but a near-touch gesture is not). Rejected: return to 13 px (gap at 270 px would be 3.25 px, only +0.25 px — below rasterisation and blind-read discrimination, and it leaves the blunt tip unchanged); keep 12 px unchanged (the distance floor, but with a 46.4° tip the 3.0 px black channel is squeezed between a 24 px-wide red head and the white cube face at thumbnail); widen the gap to ~20 px (breaks the tension, makes the arrow look hesitant / as if it missed); merge the tip into the cube (destroys the deliberate break); add a black outline, halo or second red (adds an element, breaks the one-red / no-halo rules). COUNT CORRECTION: worker claimed 30 new, 1 recount — 'NASA and Space poster series' (inspected) is the same work as 'NASA Missions — Space Probe Poster Series' (opened in full), both by Vadim Sadovski (the same Behance project under its search-result title and its project-page title) → true 29, total ~2,562. (The grep's 'S Arrow' hit on 'Dussehra 2026 Post: AI Ravana vs Designer's Arrow' is a substring false positive — 's Arrow' inside 'Designer's Arrow' — and its 'AIM' hit on 'Focus / Target / Aim' is a substring false positive; both are distinct works, not recounts.) GEOMETRY VERIFIED BY MAIN MUSE: new head width 2 × 112 × tan(18°) = 72.78 px ✓; new base corners (460.6,696.0) ± 36.4 × (−0.5, 0.866) = (442.4, 727.5) and (478.8, 664.5) ✓; corners move inward 11.6 px per side ✓; head area 0.5 × 72.8 × 112 = 4,076.8 px² ✓; length/width 1.54 ✓; head/shaft 2.06:1 ✓; shoulder step (72.8−35.3)/2 = 18.75 px ✓; red width 12 px back from the tip 12/112 × 72.8 = 7.8 px ✓ (1.95 px at 270 px); gap 12/72.8 = 16.5% of head width ✓; tip-to-vertex √(10.4² + 6.0²) = 12.0 px ✓. COLLISION-CHECK CORRECTION: the study's 'the narrower head is 115 px from the seam at its nearest point' is WRONG — the perpendicular distance from the tip (557.6,752.0) to the seam's centreline (C=(697,767) → B=(647,903), direction (−0.345,0.939)) is 136.1 px, and the distance from the tip to C is 140.2 px. The conclusion still holds (no contact), with even more clearance than the study claimed. FALSIFICATION-CHECK NOTE: the study's measurable check 'at a 270 px export: gap = 3.0 px of dark (at least 2 consecutive centreline pixels with R, G, B < 20 between red and white)' is the right form (it judges by dark, not pure black — the loop 91/92 lesson). BUILD (script `build-v93.py`, surgical edit of the v92 wedge PNG → `slash-wedge-point-746-gap12-head72x112-taper1-base36-entry24-steep30.png`, sha256 `8a601c57`, 31,177 bytes): the new head is NARROWER than the old head at a fixed s (the apex angle changes, so the edges are different lines) — the old head sticks out sideways beyond the new head and must be ERASED first (loop 84 pattern), or its side triangles remain as red ghosts. Erase = old head MINUS new head MINUS shaft, dilated ~2 px with a soft edge, then draw the new head triangle at 4× supersampling. - **Loop 94 — WIDEN THE HEAD 72.8 → 88.0 px (study 39 NEW, 0 recounts — total ~2,601; banned file now 609 unique titles covering loops 61–94):** VERDICT — the weakness is head mass and head shape, not the gap. At 72.8 px wide × 112 px long the head is a 36.0° dart: length/width 1.54, area 4,076.8 px², head/shaft ratio 2.06:1, shoulder step only 18.8 px per side. At full size that is too little red mass against a 746 × 420 px cube and a 1,056 px-wide wordmark, and at 270 px the 4.7 px shoulder collapses, so the wedge reads as a spike / dart / pencil rather than an arrowhead. Sharpening in loop 93 traded gap legibility for head-mass loss — the gap is fine, the head is not. THE CHANGE: Widen the head only, from 72.8 px to 88.0 px wide. Everything else is frozen. New head: 88.0 px wide × 112 px long (unchanged length), area 0.5 × 88.0 × 112 = 4,928 px² (+20.9% on 4,076.8; still 8.3% below the pre-sharpening 5,376 px²). Top-5: Hugo Dibono 'Keep Going Motivational Poster' (large chevron arrows read because the arms are as thick as the shaft, the head is ~2.5× the shaft height and the apex is ~70–80°; in the same image the compressed right-hand columns collapse into thin vertical spikes/lines — direct proof that narrowing a head destroys thumbnail arrow-reading); clarke 'Glowing yellow arrow on red background' (a broad, short, ~110° head, head/shaft ~2.1:1, reads instantly as an arrow; its mass is at the base); themefire 'Retro Arrows Seamless Patterns' (every triangle that reads as an arrow/triangle is ~45–90°, base ≈ height; no long narrow triangle is used as an arrowhead — long narrow triangles read as mosaic shards); Anthony Gribben 'Jewelers Mutual Abstract Geometric Poster I' (the large blue triangles that read as arrowheads/diamonds are wide, apex ~90–100°, base much wider than height; small triangles are also wide, never spiked); Othman Ouzayane 'OUZAGFX — Visual Identity' (red carries the composition as a whole field behind a black silhouette; a thin red point alone, as in BLACK BOX, has no such backing mass). Rejected: keep the head exactly as it is (no 36°, L/W 1.54 arrowhead in the study reads at thumbnail, and 4.7 px is below the ~6 px shoulder the legible examples clear); lengthen the head 112 → 140 px (area rises but L/W rises to ~1.93 and apex falls to ~29° — more dart/pencil-like, and thumbnail width stays 18.2 px); widen the shaft to restore mass/ratio (head/shaft ratio falls to 1.52:1 and the shoulder falls to 12.4 px, 3.1 px at thumbnail — destroys arrow-reading and changes the frozen entry/taper); revert fully to the original 96 × 112 px head (erases loop 93 entirely and returns the bluntest head; 88 px restores 65% of the lost width while keeping the head sharper at 42.9°); move the tip closer / shrink the 12 px gap, or add a second red accent / thicken the halo (the gap is proven at 3.0 px at thumbnail and the tip is frozen; a second accent or halo is not head mass and would add a new element). NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 95 — NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 95 — with the head restored to 88 px, does the heavier head make the frozen 12 px tip gap feel cramped — does the head now look as if it is about to touch the cube's upper-left vertex (568, 758) — or is 12 px still the right distance at both sizes? Test the built 88 px head against the same head with the tip pulled back to a 16 px gap (tip (554.2, 750.0), same 30.0° centreline, head and shaft otherwise unchanged), judging only gap tension and cube crowding at 270 px and full size.

- **Loop 95 — WIDEN THE TIP GAP 12 → 16 px (study 30 NEW — worker claimed 32, 2 recounts — total ~2,631; banned file now 639 unique titles covering loops 61–95):** VERDICT — the 12 px gap was frozen while the head grew from 72.8 px to 88 px in loop 94. Its gap/head ratio fell from 16.5% to 13.6%, and at 270 px / 3 m it is only 3.0 px — below the countable threshold at which a break reads as deliberate. At full size 12 px is a near-touch; at 270 px it risks reading as a hairline, an anti-aliasing fringe, or an accidental touch, because a heavy 22 px-wide red head and a sharp white vertex squeeze a 3 px black channel between them. THE CHANGE: Widen the tip gap from 12 px to 16 px, by translating the whole red wedge 4.0 px back along its existing 30.0° centreline. Nothing else changes. New tip: (554.2, 750.0) — 16.0 px from the cube's upper-left vertex (568, 758) measured along the centreline. Head base centre: (457.1, 694.0) — was (460.6, 696.0). Head base corners: (435.1, 732.1) and (479.1, 655.9) — were (438.6, 734.1) and (482.6, 657.9). Head unchanged: 88.0 px wide × 112 px long, apex 42.9°, area 4,928 px², #C32D30. Shaft: same single straight taper, now ending at s = 528 (was 532); width at the new head base ≈ 35.2 px (was 35.3 px); entry unchanged at (0, 430), 24 px wide, top edge (0, 416.1), bottom edge (0, 443.9). Centreline, angle 30.0°, seam, cube, wordmark, tagline, microcopy and footer all unchanged. The centreline still passes exactly through (568, 758), so the aim is preserved; only the stopping distance grows. At full size the gap is 16 px (1.48% of the 1,080 px canvas; 18.2% of the 88 px head width, up from 13.6%). At 270 px it is 4.0 px of dark between a 22.0 px-wide head and the white vertex (was 3.0 px). Top-5: Révolté 'ISOBAR — Invisible Force Made Legible' (three posters are separated by wide, unambiguous black frames and the copy reads 'The gap is the only problem.' A gap that is the subject must be legible at a glance, not a 3 px sliver); Ahmed Emad Eldin 'Near and Listening' (a small figure reaches toward a bright centre; the near-touch is a luminous field with real distance in it, not a hairline — the hand reads as 'about to touch' precisely because the separation is countable); Likhi Branding 'AimSpot Logo | Smart Target Location-Based Branding' (the arrow pierces the target — no gap at all. That is the touch statement. If BLACK BOX does not want a hit, its separation must be unmistakable, not something a viewer has to squint to distinguish from a pierce); Chase Shewbridge 'Del Water Gap Tour Poster' (collaged panels either overlap boldly or are separated by centimetres of ground. Nothing relies on a 1–3 px separation; near-touch is staged with a visible channel); TERU TAKAMORI 'LITTLE RED IN THE GAPS' (large red serif type sits against a checked pumpkin; the gaps that make the title work are letter-width and readable at thumbnail scale. A gap that only exists at full size does not survive the small read). Rejected: keep 12 px (gap/head ratio 13.6% and 3.0 px at 270 px — risks reading as touch / fringe); widen to 20 px (tip ≈ (550.7, 748.0), 5.0 px at 270 px — gap/head 22.7% leaves the near-touch band; the wedge would start to read as stopped short or having missed, tension drops); close the gap to 0 px (changes the idea from 'about to strike' to 'has struck'; the deliberate pause and the black field's role as separator would be lost); narrow the head back to 72.8 px to restore the old ratio (loop 94's wider, heavier head was a deliberate restoration; undoing it to save a gap reverses a settled decision); keep 12 px but add a white/black outline or halo to the tip (a new graphic element around the tip would fight the tight halo, the 10 px cube silhouette stroke and the seam; it treats a distance problem as a decoration problem); change the angle or the entry to 'aim more clearly' (the 30.0° centreline already passes exactly through (568, 758); aim is not the problem, stopping distance is).
