- **Loop 96 — KEEP THE GAP AT 16 px: FREEZE, NO GEOMETRY MOVES (study 35 NEW — worker claimed 36, 1 recount — total ~2,666; banned file now 674 unique titles covering loops 61–96):** VERDICT — the 16 px gap is kept exactly as built in v95. The single biggest remaining weakness is not the gap and not the shoulder: at 270 px the whole 'about to strike' reading hangs on a single 4.0 px band of black between the red tip and the white cube face. That band is preserved and exactly large enough — but it is fragile to any further widening, so loop 96 freezes it rather than adjusting it. THE CHANGE: keep the wedge exactly as built in v95. Lock the tip at (554.2, 750.0), the centreline (0, 430) → (554.2, 750.0) at 30.0°, and the gap at 16.0 px measured along that centreline to the cube's upper-left vertex (568, 758). Head unchanged: 88.0 × 112 px, apex 42.9°, base centre (457.1, 694.0), base corners (435.1, 732.1) and (479.1, 655.9). Shaft unchanged: one straight taper, 24 px at the left edge → 35.2 px at the head base (s = 528), 0.61° per side, entry vertical intercept 27.7 px, top edge (0, 416.1), bottom edge (0, 443.9). No element moves. This is the one change — a freeze, specified buildably, not a move. At full size the tip is 16 px from the vertex and the wedge still points at the cube with clear intent. At 270 px the gap is 4.0 px of dark and the head is 22.0 px wide, and the aim still reads, because the wedge's 30° line still points exactly at the vertex. The extra 4 px over loop 95's 12 px is 1 px at 270 px (3 px → 4 px) — below the threshold at which a viewer can re-read 'about to strike' as 'has stopped'. The shoulder does NOT create a second near-touch. The head's upper shoulder corner (479.1, 655.9) is 135.4 px from the vertex, and its projection onto the UL–T edge (568, 758) → (672, 670) falls 1.9 px before the vertex, outside the segment, so the closest point on that edge is the vertex itself. Shoulder-to-edge is therefore also 135.4 px full size, 33.8 px at 270 px — 8.5× the tip gap, and wider than the whole head (22 px at 270 px). There is one unambiguous near-touch, the tip, not two. WHY, grounded in the designs studied: a miss is off-line, not short — in the designs studied, an arrow that has missed is shown landed somewhere else, off the aiming line, or pointing in a different direction, never by a small on-line gap, so a gap on the exact centreline cannot read as a miss at either size; contact means 'has struck' — across the bullseye/target works studied, the arrow tip touches, overlaps or pierces the centre with zero gap, so closing the BLACK BOX gap to 0 would flip the meaning from 'about to strike' to 'has struck', and keeping a gap is what preserves the tension; aim is read from shaft alignment, not from contact — posters with nocked arrows, drawn bows and arrows with no target at all still read unambiguously as aiming, because the shaft line is straight and its extension hits the target point; the ratio is scale-invariant and inside the 'imminent' range — gap/head width = 16/88 = 0.18 at full size and 4.0/22.0 = 0.18 at 270 px, and the loop-95 change (12 → 16 px) is 1 px at 270 px, within anti-aliasing/print tolerance, while buying a cleaner black separator (4 px of black is a definite, countable band between saturated red #C32D30 and the white top face #F5F5F5, whereas 3 px can anti-alias down toward 2 px); the shoulder cannot compete because it is not near anything — at 135 px it is further from the cube than the head is long (112 px), and at 270 px its 33.8 px separation is larger than the head itself. Top-5: Sebastian Vollmar | Vollmar & Vision 'Vivid Style – Arrow' (Dribbble — the caption states the figure 'is immersed in a playful action to shoot an arrow, but does not hit' (a 404 page); the miss is shown by other arrows landed off-line around the target — one stuck in the ground below it, one in its top edge — while the arrow still on the bow, on its aiming line and short of the target, reads as about to shoot; this proves a short on-line gap is not a miss — a miss is displacement off the line); Abhijeet Kumar 'Archery Meeting poster' (Behance — black field, white bow, three horizontal nocked arrows whose tips sit in empty black void with no target at all, yet direction and readiness are unmistakable; aim is carried by shaft alignment alone — exactly the mechanism the 16 px gap relies on); Kakon Ghosh 'AimOcean Brand Identity Design' (Dribbble — the arrow mark is fused into its target ring with zero gap: contact reads as 'hit'; closing the BLACK BOX gap would therefore change the meaning, not sharpen it); Cameron Thornton 'LUAC Archery Posters 2016' (Behance — red field, drawn bow, and a small target swirl whose arrow pierces the centre: contact again means hit, while the tension before release lives in the drawn bow, not in touching the target; the BLACK BOX wedge is the drawn-bow moment, not the pierced-target moment); Ainslee McAndrew 'The Origin of the Universe and the Arrow of Time' (Behance — a huge black diagonal arrow/paper-plane form dominates a white field; direction is read from its long converging edges alone, with no target and no contact; it proves a single clean diagonal, on a clear line, carries aim at poster scale without needing to touch anything). Supporting pattern (not in the top 5): the bullseye group — 'Target And Arrow Bullseye Vector Cartoon Illustration' (Kruweks Studio), 'Shot in target with arrow' (Studio Humaira), 'Red white target with arrows in the bullseye' (Konstantin Mironov), 'Precise' (Robby), 'Bullseye' (Stephen W. Piercey), 'Target With Arrows Animation' (Serkan Aydınlı) — all show the tip in, touching or piercing the centre; none uses a small gap to mean 'about to hit'. Rejected: narrow the gap back to 12 px (tip 557.7, 752.0; base centre 460.6, 696.0; corners 438.6, 734.1 and 482.6, 657.9; whole wedge shifted +4 px along the centreline — it gains exactly 1 px at 270 px, no tested reader can distinguish 3 px from 4 px of black as 'more about to strike', and it thins the black separator between red and white, risking optical red–white contact at small size/print); widen further, e.g. to 24 px (tip 547.3, 746.0; base centre 450.2, 690.0; corners 428.2, 728.1 and 472.2, 651.9 — gap/head width becomes 0.27, gap 6 px at 270 px against a 22 px head, crossing from 'imminent' into 'parked / has stopped', the very reading loop 96 was asked to avoid); close the gap to 0 / touch the vertex (tip 568, 758; base centre 471.0, 702.0; corners 449.0, 740.1 and 493.0, 663.9 — it flips the poster from 'about to strike' to 'has struck', as every contact bullseye design shows, and red would touch the white top face); move the head's shoulder / narrow the head (the shoulder is 135.4 px from the cube, so there is nothing to fix; narrowing the head would change the frozen 88 × 112 px head and 42.9° apex and break the 2.50:1 head/shaft ratio for no benefit); add a second cue (motion lines, a target ring, a glow at the tip — it adds an element, violates the frozen minimal system, and is not needed, since the centreline already points exactly at the vertex). - **Loop 97 — WIDEN THE SEAM 20 → 24 px, ALL SOLID, 0 FEATHER (study 27 NEW — worker claimed 28, 1 recount — total ~2,693; banned file now 701 unique titles covering loops 61–97):** VERDICT — the blue seam is asymmetric in visibility. Against the pale front-left face it is ~3.98:1, but against the dark right face (46,90,120) #1D4ED8 is only ~1.10:1 in relative luminance, and its 2 px feather on that side is swallowed by the dark face. At full size it therefore reads as a line hugging the pale face, apparently narrower than its 20 px total; at 270 px its 4 px of saturated blue is effectively ~2–2.5 px of clearly blue, at the threshold of dissolving, and it is exactly the same 4.0 px width as the frozen tip gap. THE CHANGE: replace the seam stroke specification only, nothing else — seam becomes 24 px total, all solid #1D4ED8, 0 px feather (was 20 px total: 16 px solid #1D4ED8 + 2 px linear feather each side). Colour #1D4ED8 unchanged. Centreline unchanged: (697,767)–(647,903), length ~145 px, direction ~110° from +x. C end (s=30) remains a flat cap; B end still tapers to the bottom vertex (647,903) by the hexagon clip. At 270 px this is 6.0 px of solid saturated blue (24 × 270/1080). No other element moves. WHY: the failure is not hue or angle, it is width plus a feather on the low-contrast side. A 2 px feather is 0.5 px at 270 px — sub-pixel blur, not a visible transition — so the dark-side half of the seam has no hard edge to anchor it. Everywhere in the study, lines that survive at thumbnail are hard-edged and either much lighter than both sides or thick enough to be a band; soft, feathered, low-luminance-contrast lines dissolve. Widening the solid core to 24 px and deleting the feather gives a 12 px hard blue field on each side of the centreline, so the chromatic step (blue 216 vs teal-blue 120, saturation) is abrupt and 12 px wide on the dark side instead of a 2 px gradient. It does not change the hue, so the seam stays the only chromatic line in the cube and stays subordinate to the red wedge (wedge head 22.0 px wide at 270 px vs seam 6.0 px). It also breaks the accidental 4 px = 4 px identity with the tip gap. Top-5: hooray 'Sci-Fi Blueprint Poster Set' (Dribbble — its blue survives at thumbnail not as a hairline but as thick, hard-edged solid blue bars on cream/black; proves a single blue accent needs band-like solid width and a hard edge to read small — the model for 24 px solid); Vlad Grama 'BLUEPRINT - Minimalist poster design' (Dribbble — thin white hard lines on saturated blue (#051472/#123193) hold because they are far lighter than both sides and have no feather; proves the inverse of the BLACK BOX case — a line whose luminance contrast is ~1.1:1 on one side cannot rely on thinness, it needs width and a hard edge); Andrew Pabon 'Glass Refraction' (Dribbble — a broad soft refraction gradient dissolves at distance; only the single hard, bright specular point/edge at the lower right still reads; proves soft/feathered refraction is the part that vanishes, hard edges are the part that survives); Galih Studio 'Poster Wave Line Ocean Blue' (Dribbble — many thin white lines on #171B63 read only as a field, because each line is white against dark blue; a single such line would not; proves a solitary line cannot borrow the redundancy of a field — it must be individually wide enough); Louis Coyle 'Water sphere - Glass refraction' (Dribbble — refraction reads because the blue/orange patches are large, saturated areas with hard tonal boundaries, not a 4 px line; proves that at small size, area and hard boundaries, not feathered subtlety, carry refraction). - **Loop 99 — RESHAPE THE SEAM INTO A TRUE POINTED LENS (VESICA PISCIS) OVER ITS FULL LENGTH (study 32 NEW — worker claimed 31, then corrected itself to 30, but its own lists add to 33 with 1 recount — total ~2,752; banned file now 759 unique titles covering loops 61–99):** VERDICT — the middle still reads as a third face. The pointed ends of loop 98 occupy only 36 px at C and a short clip at B, while ~109 px (75% of the ~145 px seam) is a 24 px-wide solid band with two straight, parallel, hard edges — the textbook geometry of a face. THE CHANGE: remove the plateau and reshape the seam into a true pointed lens (vesica piscis) over its full length, keeping the colour #1D4ED8 solid, 0 px feather, the centreline (697,767)–(647,903), and both endpoints exactly (C and B, both still sharp points). The vesica is two circular arcs of radius 224.7 px (poster), meeting in cusps at C and B; the arcs' centres lie 212.7 px from the midpoint on opposite sides of the centreline. Maximum width is still 24.0 px, but at the midpoint (672,835) ONLY — side points (683.3,839.1) and (660.7,830.9), 12.0 px perpendicular from M on each side. Width profile (s = distance from C): s=0: 0; s=18: 10.6; s=36: 18.0; s=54: 22.4; s=72.5 (midpoint): 24.0; s=91: 22.4; s=109: 18.0; s=127: 10.6; s=144.9 (B): 0. No straight segment and no run of parallel edges anywhere; the width changes at every point. At 270 px the lens is 36.2 px long, 6.0 px at its widest point only. Total blue area falls from ~3,050 px² to ~2,300 px²; the maximum width and the colour do not change, so the seam does not disappear. WHY: in every real caustic and refraction image studied, light is recognised by width that varies continuously along its length, curved rather than straight edges, and no constant-width plateau; a face is recognised by the opposite (a flat fill bounded by straight, parallel, hard edges). Real caustics never hold a constant width for 75% of their length — they taper continuously, vary in width from point to point, and fade at their ends. A single thin, tapering line reads as light because it has no second parallel edge to bound a plane. Pointed ends alone do not change the middle — the eye groups the 109 px plateau as the object. Top-5: Maurizio Griffa 'Shadow Caustic' (Behance — caustics are focused, varying, soft marks, never a solid constant-width strip exactly on a fold; the face/caustic distinction is made by the light's shape, not its colour); Amit Chippa 'Caustics much? : Chapter 1' (Behance — white caustic streaks taper to points at both ends, change width continuously, and have soft fall-off; no streak holds a constant width for most of its length); DSORDER . 'Lights & Crystals' (Behance — caustics are pointed, curved, irregular light marks on black, with no hard parallel edges); Multiple Owners 'MICROSOFT • Fluent UI' (Behance, enlarged crop — glass cubes are flat, uniformly coloured polygons with straight hard edges; the light on them is curved, graded and varying in width; the current seam's straight parallel edges are the face cue, and curving the edges is the cue that removes it); Multiple Owners 'MICROSOFT • Surface Copilot+PC' (Behance — a blue glass key's light is a soft, graded glow with a bright core that fades, not a solid band with two hard edges; even saturated blue light is read as light when it has no second hard boundary). Rejected: keep as-is; narrow the middle to 18 px (an 18 px band for ~109 px is still a band — two straight parallel hard edges, only a thinner face; at 270 px ~4.5 px of solid blue, still a constant-width band); add a 2–3 px gradient fall-off on the dark-face side only (leaves a ~21 px solid plateau with one hard edge, so the face reading persists; at 270 px a 2–3 px fall-off is 0.5–0.75 px, invisible — and if the shape is still a plateau, softening one edge makes it a softer face, not a lens); add fall-off on both sides, or a pale core (both already removed in earlier loops for good reason; a two-sided fall-off would blur the fold itself); make the seam a straight diamond (straight edges would still read as a rhombus — a flat polygonal face — whereas the vesica's curved arcs cannot be mistaken for a cube face, whose edges are straight); move, shorten or recolour the seam, or change the faces/wedge (none needed — the new lens fits strictly inside the old footprint). - **Loop 101 — FREEZE THE HARD EDGE: BUILD NOTHING ON THE SEAM (study 16 NEW — worker claimed 18, 2 recounts — total ~2,795; banned file now 802 unique titles covering loops 61–101):** VERDICT — the single biggest remaining weakness is the full-size pale-face edge of the seam: a hard 1 px step from solid #1D4ED8 to the pale face ~(199,201,189) on a flat face, which is the one place a first-time viewer could momentarily parse the blue as a painted sticker. The evidence says that risk does not materialise, and the posed fix would make it worse. THE ONE CHANGE: build nothing on the seam. Freeze v100 exactly — seam centreline (697,767)–(647,903), length ~144.9 px, solid #1D4ED8, 0 px feather, no pale core, maximum width 20.0 px at the midpoint (672,835) only, width profile s=0: 0; s=18: 8.8; s=36: 15.0; s=54: 18.7; s=72.5: 20.0; s=91: 18.7; s=109: 15.0; s=127: 8.8; s=144.9 (B): 0, both long edges hard 1 px transitions, true points at C (697,767) and B (647,903), dark edge hard. WHY: painted/sticker readings occur where flat colour sits on a uniform substrate with no geometry underneath it (a flat blue bar on black reads as paper/paint precisely because nothing changes beneath it; hard edges read as torn paper, a material, not light). Our seam fails that test for a sticker: its centreline coincides with the C–B fold, both its points sit on the fold, and it is symmetric about the fold, so it straddles two faces whose tones already step ΔL* ~44 (pale L* ~76–80 against dark L* ~36.5). A sticker sits on one face; this seam is visibly one object crossing a fold, and its two edges accordingly have different contrasts — a high lightness step on the pale side, a hue-only step on the dark side — which is the signature of light on a fold, not of a flat graphic. Second, the vesica shape itself is anti-sticker: its edges narrow continuously to points and are never parallel, so there is no run of "sticker edge" anywhere. Third, in every light example studied, hard edges are the norm and softness lives inside the light form or in the surrounding field, not in a 2 px edge ramp. A 2 px ramp is below the threshold of an optical effect and at the threshold of a print artefact: at 270 px it is 0.5 px and invisible, so it cannot change the distance reading, and at full size it would read as a pale-blue fringe — a printed gradient edge — rather than as light. Top-5: Robert Sekulić for PROTOTYP 'Division' (Dribbble — fluted/fractal glass, "Tension between hard edges and soft forms. A bit of a contrast light." — hard edge plus soft form is the working language of light in glass, not a defect to feather away); GUDRIX 'Brand Identity & Guidelines, Siji Labs' (Dribbble — Electric Blue #0613E8 ribbons on near-black #010106 with hard, crisp ribbon edges and internal gradient; saturated blue on black with hard edges reads unambiguously as light/energy, not paint — the closest analogue to our #1D4ED8 on #000000); Javier Pardina 'PRISMATIC BLOOM' (Behance — overlapping glass spheres whose rims are hard 1–2 px bright lines over smooth-gradient interiors; the hard rim is what makes glass/light legible; feathering it would dissolve the form); Mocku Official 'Newsprint Folded Poster Mockup Set' (Behance — a paper fold reads as a fold precisely because its tonal step is hard; feathering a fold turns it into a stain); Reza Gholipour 'Sigma BF Modified Visual Language' (Behance — hard aluminium facet boundaries with large tonal steps read as light on solid form) and Cuong Nguyen "OCT_2X26, 'CODE LYOKO: RECODE'" (Behance — hard blue circuit lines on black, with glow in the surrounding field, not in the line's own edge). Rejected: 2 px linear fall-off, pale side only, 20 px core kept (as posed — invisible at 270 px, too small to read as optics at full size, asymmetric (12 px vs 10 px from centreline), +45% widening at s=18, blunts the points at C and B, and adds a pale-blue fringe that reads as a printed gradient/sticker shadow); 2 px Gaussian fall-off, pale side only (same asymmetry, wider effective tint from the Gaussian tails, worse point-blunting, harder to verify); symmetric 2 px fall-off both sides (preserves symmetry but still blunts the points, widens the maximum to 24 px, and contradicts the loop 80/99–100 hard-edge decisions for no gain at either distance); larger fall-off (4–6 px, pale side — comparable to the vesica's own width at its thin ends (8.8 px at s=18), so it would swallow the lens into a soft smear and destroy the points; the card already carries its bloom in the Transparency-20 halos elsewhere); narrowing the solid core (e.g. 16 px solid plus ramps — breaks the verified 20 px, and thins the seam to ~4.0 px at 270 px, risking losing it at distance); re-adding a pale core inside the blue (reversing loop 80 — would make the seam read as a tube/rod rather than a caustic line); moving the seam off the fold (would destroy the fold anchoring that is the reason the hard edge reads as light). COUNT CORRECTION: worker claimed 18, but 2 of the 18 are recounts — "Spectral Gradient" (Damon xart) and "3D LIGHTING" (Mostafa Hassan) are both already in the banned file (loop 98) → TRUE 16 NEW, total ~2,795. The other four top-5 items (Division, Siji Labs, PRISMATIC BLOOM, Newsprint Folded Poster Mockup Set) are new, and the freeze verdict does not depend on the reused item (the §2 reasoning also cites the new Siji Labs, PRISMATIC BLOOM, Sigma BF and CODE LYOKO designs). NEXT-BEST QUESTION (named by the study): Loop 102 — with the seam's edge frozen, is the seam's flat solid #1D4ED8 interior — the one remaining property it shares with a painted mark — doing any work against the light reading at full size, or does the vesica's pointed geometry plus the fold anchoring already settle it? Test: at full size only, compare the frozen flat seam against a variant with a single internal luminance step (a 4 px brighter core, #3B82F6, centred on the centreline, hard edges kept), judged by the same blind-read protocol; do not touch the edge, the width, the points or the fold.

- **Loop 102 — FINAL LOOP: FREEZE THE FLAT INTERIOR, KEEP THE SEAM AS-IS (study 23 NEW — worker claimed 24, 1 recount — total ~2,818; banned file now 825 unique titles covering loops 61–102):** VERDICT — the single biggest remaining weakness is the seam's flat solid #1D4ED8 interior — the one remaining property it shares with a painted mark. The evidence says the flatness is not what makes a mark read as paint; the geometry and the anchoring do the work. THE ONE CHANGE: keep the seam as-is and freeze its flat interior — one single flat fill, solid #1D4ED8, 0 px feather, no internal edge, no gradient, no core and no second blue anywhere in the seam; vesica geometry unchanged (arcs of radius 267.45 px meeting in sharp points at C (697,767) and B (647,903), centreline (697,767)–(647,903), length ~144.9 px, maximum width 20.0 px at the midpoint (672,835) only, width profile s=0: 0; s=18: 8.8; s=36: 15.0; s=54: 18.7; s=72.5: 20.0; s=91: 18.7; s=109: 15.0; s=127: 8.8; s=144.9: 0, both long edges hard 1 px transitions). The proposed 4 px brighter #3B82F6 core is REJECTED and must not be built. WHY: in 'Flat vs Gradient Colors' (Aqeela Musharraff) the same crystal is drawn twice side by side: on the left every facet is a single flat colour with hard points and no internal gradient, and it still reads unmistakably as a crystal — a light-refracting solid — because the facets meet at points and differ in tone; on the right the gradient version, with internal diagonal streaks and bright spots, reads as a glossy render, a different register altogether. Adding a #3B82F6 core would move BLACK BOX toward that right-hand register. In 'THE END' (IO Rosemary) a flat, near-white, hard-edged vertical sword-blade streak on black reads unambiguously as light/steel with no internal gradient at all, because it is anchored — hilt above, body below, black field around it. The BLACK BOX seam is anchored far more tightly: it sits exactly on the C–B fold, its two points are the fold's own vertices, and the faces either side step by ΔL* ~44. A painted mark would be a uniform-width stripe, or a shape crossing the fold indifferently; no painted mark tapers continuously from 20.0 px to true points at exactly the two fold vertices. That width profile is itself the light evidence. Conversely, 'Light Portal Core' (Lucid mk2) shows what a genuine bright core actually looks like: a soft, diffuse, symmetrical glow, brightest at the centre and fading outward with mottled texture — not a hard 4 px step. A hard step would not replicate that; it would be a third device, a pinstripe. And a 4 px core cannot even be built cleanly inside this vesica: scaled to the vesica it would be 4.0 px at the midpoint, 3.7 px at s=54/91, 3.0 px at s=36/109 and only 1.8 px at s=18/127, vanishing and aliasing near the points; kept at a constant 4 px it would end in flat 4 px stubs at C and B and destroy the true points. Either way it damages the one thing that settles the reading. It would also break the card's one-blue discipline and re-open loop 80, which deleted the pale core for the same reason. At 270 px the core would be 1.0 px and invisible, so it buys nothing at distance and risks a stripe at full size. Top-5: Aqeela Musharraff 'Flat vs Gradient Colors' (flatness does not equal paint, and an internal step changes register, not just brightness); IO Rosemary 'THE END' (a flat solid streak plus anchoring settles a light reading); Lucid mk2 'Light Portal Core' (a real bright core is a soft, diffuse, symmetrical fall-off — the proposed hard 4 px step is not how a core reads as light, and would be read as a stripe instead); Multiple Owners 'Sumerge — Brand Identity' (flat saturated-blue diagonal streaks radiating on black, with white wordmark, read as light/energy with no internal core or gradient); Mihir Bhavsar 'Mentora Men's Care Brand Identity' (a flat saturated-blue asterisk/star on dark navy, no internal step, reads as a crisp mark/light device). Rejected: the proposed 4 px brighter #3B82F6 core, full length, scaled to the vesica (sub-2 px near the points, aliases and vanishes there; reads as a pinstripe splitting the lens into 8 px blue / 4 px light / 8 px blue; adds a second blue; invisible at 270 px); a constant-width 4 px core, not scaled (would end in flat 4 px stubs at C and B, destroying the true points); a soft/feathered gradient core or glow (reintroduces the fringe deleted in loops 77, 78 and 80, breaks the hard-edge freeze of loop 101, and at 270 px would blur the 5.0 px-wide lens); a wider/brighter core (8–10 px, white-blue — would dominate the 20 px seam, split it into two blue rails and read as two lines, and would change the seam's tonal balance against the ΔL* ~44 faces); lightening the whole seam or widening it (out of scope, frozen in loop 101, and would weaken the contrast that makes the seam the only chromatic line); removing the seam (would remove the card's single chromatic event and the fold's only marker). COUNT CORRECTION: worker claimed 24, but 1 of the 24 is a recount — "Bicoca Lamp" (Maria Leonova) is already in the banned file (loop 100) → TRUE 23 NEW, total ~2,818. STUDY NOTE: only 7 of the 24 designs were opened in full on Behance; the other 17 were inspected in Behance search results with enlarged crops of the result grids (the brief allowed inspecting in search results, and the worker reported this honestly; Dribbble search loaded blank and Awwwards search matched the word 'search' rather than refraction, so no designs were counted from either). NO BUILD, NO REBUILD, NO NEW EXPORT — the design on canvas stays v100 (cube `glass-cube-black-v100.png`, sha256 `3d53e8beda4779d8a8b034088765c89ed5e36b45bd2ca98b5bb41345c29f31f0`; export sha256 `ee658f39`, 248,992 bytes, verified in loop 100). The loop 102 measurable file checks (eyedropper at the midpoint (672,835) = #1D4ED8; at ±6 px perpendicular to the centreline from the midpoint also #1D4ED8, no #3B82F6 anywhere in the seam; midpoint cross-section 20–21 px saturated blue, 18.5 px exact #1D4ED8 as export-verified; no blue beyond C or B; faces byte-identical outside the seam band; wedge tip gap still 16 px from (568,758); no second blue hex present in the cube) were all already established by the loop 100 export verification. FINAL DELIVERY NOTE: the card is now a 1080×1080 black poster in which a white kerned Montserrat ExtraBold wordmark sits in a 118 px black void above a left-aligned two-line tagline, a pale-and-dark glass cube carries a single flat solid #1D4ED8 pointed lens on its C–B fold, and a single flat #C32D30 wedge stops 16 px short of the cube's upper-left vertex, with only 'N° 01' / 'EST. 2026' above and '@BLACKBOXSYSTEMSLAB' / 'VOL. 01' below — every element frozen, the seam's flat interior frozen with it, and the light reading settled by the vesica's pointed geometry and its exact anchoring on the fold rather than by any internal luminance step. Nothing is still open: the final question has been answered, the one change is a freeze, and no further loop is required. ALL 102 LOOPS COMPLETE.
